The authority for platform rules stays with `docs/PRODUCT_CONTINUITY.md` (including the permanent dashboard decision), `docs/MASTER_BUILD_REGISTER.md` and `docs/FOUNDER_PHONE_TEST_RUNBOOK.md`. ## 0. THE AUTHORITATIVE MILESTONE ROADMAP AND DEFINITION OF DONE (established 2026-11-02; this section wins over any other milestone, order or reporting rule in these documents)

**How this section was made.** The founder instructed on 2026-11-02 that ONE milestone roadmap and ONE Definition of Done exist, reconciled with the existing records and not duplicated. The decisions below that the founder has already made are marked as such; the **milestone breakdown (CL-0 to CL-6) and the names of the five report parts were drafted by the assistant from the existing records and stand until the founder edits them in writing.** Other documents point here (`docs/ROADMAP_BACKLOG.md`, `docs/PRODUCT_CONTINUITY.md`, the handoff); they do not repeat it.

### 0.1 What the product is, and what is inactive
- **Vision (finalized 2026-11-02 at the founder's instruction; the founder edits it in writing).** **Drew is an AI-native operations platform for residential cleaning companies, not merely a receptionist.** It is **one AI agent** that does a cleaning company's front-office work: it takes the request, quotes within limits the owner sets, books in the company's own scheduling software, checks that every action really happened, follows up on lost revenue, and reports what happened, all under controls the owner sets and with every claim backed by evidence. **Answering the call is the first capability being tested; it is not the limit of the product.** What exists today and what is only a goal are kept apart in 0.1a, and nothing in this vision authorizes or schedules any work.
- **One residential-cleaning AI agent** (founder decision, 2026-11-02): `poc/intake-runner/src/fast-call-handler.ts` and its dependencies. The four-agent direction (Intake, Processing, QC, Reporting) is **retired**; its code and records are **historical** unless the single agent actively uses them (record 5a-52).
- **Preserved, unchanged:** the owner dashboard (section 1a, `docs/PRODUCT_CONTINUITY.md`), the simulated-booking checkpoint `2f23a51`, and the commit checkpoints `866bea3`, `140f6b0`, `8c69764`, `c3c7a7b`. All five commits are in `main`; no commit of the 2026-11-02 series (C1 `6b89658`, C2 `2e298a3`, C3 `a85cfe4`) touches `prototype/`, `dashboard-server.ts`, `owner-view.ts` or `simulated-booking.ts` (checked 2026-11-02 against `1362bd4`). **CL-1's paused test is preserved with every safeguard it has:** texts OFF unless `FAST_TEXTS` is exactly `on`; Jobber writes OFF unless `JOBBER_ALLOW_WRITES=test-account` and only for the pinned test account; the approved-caller restriction; the consent gates; duplicate prevention and the independent read-back; and the live-call evidence in the log and in roadmap sections 5a-14 to 5a-52. None of this changes with the scope decision below.
- **Product scope (founder decision, 2026-11-02). Residential cleaning is the SOLE ACTIVE product and test.** **Carpet cleaning, window cleaning and pressure washing are RESEARCH CANDIDATES only: they are not build commitments, are not scheduled, and no design, code, configuration or build task exists for them.** A candidate can become a task only by the founder's written approval naming it, and only after the Cleaning gate (0.3); research alone is never approval to build (section 1b rule 8). **The earlier industry order (Cleaning -> Towing -> Plumbing -> HVAC) and the four-agent expansion are RETIRED and HISTORICAL:** towing, plumbing and HVAC are not planned and are kept in section 2 only as the record of how the earlier plan was reasoned. Other industries are not planned.
- **GPT-Live stays paused.** `DRF-03` (recovery workflows) and `DRF-07` to `DRF-09` stay inactive; `DRF-10` (Jack's voice) is in use.

Nothing here expands scope, adds a requirement, or changes the paused test, its safeguards, the dashboard, or any recorded decision.


### 0.2 Status words, and the Definition of Done
| Word | Means | Evidence required |
|---|---|---|
| **PLANNED** | Written down only | The record |
| **BUILT** | The code exists | The file path and the commit |
| **TESTED OFFLINE** | Automated tests pass against stand-ins | The suite result (count, pass, fail), the commit, and the CI run; safety rules also need a mutation check that fails when the rule is broken |
| **VERIFIED LIVE** | Seen on the real service | A named, privacy-safe source: log codes, a saved record, Jobber's own record, a delivery status; and the date |
| **UNVERIFIED** | A claim with no evidence yet | Say so, in the same sentence |
| **PARTIAL / BLOCKED / PAUSED** | Some parts met / cannot proceed (name the blocker) / the founder has stopped it (name the condition) | The record |
| **DONE** | Accepted by the founder against this definition | See below |

**A milestone is DONE only when every one of these holds, with the evidence cited by file, commit, CI run or log, not asserted:**
1. **Each acceptance item has evidence at the level the milestone names.** Tests passing is never written as working live.
2. **Every defect found while doing it has a regression test,** and no protected test was changed to make a repair pass without being reported first.
3. **CI is green on the pushed commit** that contains the work.
4. **The records are updated in the same change:** the milestone table (0.3) and the roadmap record; the status words match the evidence.
5. **Protected work is unchanged** (0.1), and a secret and private-data scan of what was committed is clean.
6. **Irreplaceable private records created by the milestone have a verified backup** (`docs/PRIVATE_BACKUP_AND_RESTORE.md`: a restore must have passed); keys that can be re-issued are not backed up.
7. **The founder accepts it in writing,** after being shown the evidence. Nobody else, and no test result, can set DONE.

**Evidence before DONE; no self-declared DONE.** A report may say a milestone is "evidence complete", never DONE, until item 7.

### 0.4 Founder approvals: what exists, and what is still needed
- **Approved and recorded:** one residential-cleaning AI agent; the four-agent direction retired; the live Drew test **paused** pending Twilio SMS approval, **SMS OFF**; the Jack greeting and the AI-honesty behavior; $199 for two bedrooms and two bathrooms; the Jobber Developer Test Account and the standing test approval in the configuration; the texts and consent wording submitted to Twilio; the owner's consent step (reported recorded); the public privacy and SMS pages; commits C1, C2 and C3 and their push; the backup direction (encrypted cloud copy and a USB copy; OneDrive Personal only **proposed**).
- **Still needed, each as its own explicit approval:** lifting the pause and each live call; switching Jobber writes on; any restart or replacement of the old server; applying or changing any private configuration; any Twilio change or text; the backup decisions (`PRIVATE_BACKUP_AND_RESTORE.md` section 8); the disposition of `public-site/` and the Pages repository's unpushed edits; each milestone task (an approved milestone does not approve its tasks); every commit and every push.
- **One-time approval rule:** an approval names one action and ends when it is done. It never carries over to the next action, even the one that obviously follows.

### 0.5 The working rules (these replace the older standing rules where they differ)
1. **Approval before each task** (section 1b stands). **Explicit approval before any commit, any push and any live action** (a call, a restart, applying configuration, a Twilio or Jobber change or write, a text, a deployment, publishing, running a backup or opening private data, any spend or install). **This supersedes the older standing rule that completed, tested work is committed and pushed automatically** (`PRODUCT_CONTINUITY.md` rule 8).
2. **Plain-English five-part report for every task** (replaces the six-area report of `PRODUCT_CONTINUITY.md` rule 10 and the paragraph in section 1b):
   1. **What was done**, in plain words, in a few sentences.
   2. **The evidence,** each claim labelled BUILT / TESTED OFFLINE / VERIFIED LIVE / UNVERIFIED and naming its source (file, commit, CI run or log).
   3. **What is still not proven or is blocking.**
   4. **What is needed from the founder:** each decision or approval as one explicit question.
   5. **The next single step, and what will not happen without approval.**
   Facts and recommendations are kept apart. No secret, number, host or private value appears in a report.
3. **No repeat audits.** An audit is recorded once (date, scope, the commit it covered). It is repeated only when the founder asks, or when the audited code or risk has materially changed since; otherwise the record is cited and updated. The 2026-11-02 takeover audit, the commit-boundary audit, the docs review and the backup review are recorded (5a-52 and the backup document) and are not repeated.
4. **Reconcile, don't duplicate.** One place for each thing: this section for milestones, done, approvals and reporting; the roadmap's 5a records for evidence; `ROADMAP_BACKLOG.md` for the DRF requirements; `PRODUCT_CONTINUITY.md` for the dashboard rule; `PRIVATE_BACKUP_AND_RESTORE.md` for backups. A change that would restate another document's content points to it instead.
5. **The existing code and the dashboard are preserved.** Nothing is deleted, replaced or rebuilt without the founder's written instruction naming it.
6. **Not complete until enforced, tested and caveated.** For any behavior a task adds or changes that protects a caller, a booking, an email, a log or a spend:
   1. **Enforced in code.** The protection is enforced by code that fails closed, not by wording, a prompt or the model. A spoken promise (for example "a person will follow up") is behavior: something in code carries it out.
   2. **Failure and recovery are defined.** For each way it can fail, the code and the report say what the caller hears, what is recorded (fixed codes only), who is told, and how it recovers or stops safely.
   3. **Material paths and regression are tested.** A test pins the normal path and every failure path above, a hand-broken copy of the rule fails that test (a mutation check), and a defect found on the way gets its own regression test. The new tests run in the suite that `npm test` runs; if they do not, the report says so.
   4. **Evidence and gaps are explicit.** Every claim carries its status word and source (0.2). Every path that is not enforced, not tested or not seen live is named, in the same sentence, as UNVERIFIED, PARTIAL or MISSING. A task that lacks any of 1 to 3 is reported PARTIAL, never complete, and only the founder can set DONE (0.2 item 7).


## 1. The product in one paragraph

**HISTORICAL as of 2026-11-02.** The title of this file and the paragraph below describe the retired four-agent platform. The current product and vision are in section 0.1 (one AI-native residential cleaning operations platform, one agent); the paragraph is kept as the record of the earlier direction.

A B2B platform for service businesses, built from four cooperating agents, each judged by evidence the previous one did not write: **1 Intake** (take the customer's request, today by phone; mask personal data; classify and extract; confirm by read-back; produce a validated work item), **2 Processing** (run a fixed plan of allow-listed tools; human-approved tools pause for a named approver), **3 Quality Control** (judge the result from evidence Processing did not write: pass, rework or escalate), **4 Reporting** (verify the chain, keep an append-only history, produce per-tenant reports). The owner's window onto all four is the dashboard. Standing rules for every step below: synthetic or invented data in tests, no live or paid calls and no public endpoints without the founder's say-so, no commits or pushes except under the founder's standing rule in section 7, secrets never displayed, safety limits and gates preserved.

## 1a. Founder-locked constraint: the owner dashboard is preserved and evolved

**Status: LOCKED by the founder. It outranks every step, track and estimate in this file.** It restates the permanent decision in `docs/PRODUCT_CONTINUITY.md` section 1; if the wording differs, that file governs.

- **Preserve** the existing prototype owner dashboard: its layout, its components, its records and everything it does today (the files under `prototype/intake-work-items` and the strict owner view that feeds it).
- **Enhance incrementally.** Add to it as customer intake, pricing, booking, QC and reporting become functional, one small, reviewable addition at a time, each keeping what was there working.
- **Never** replace it, rebuild it, redesign it from scratch, or delete existing dashboard functionality. This includes "temporary" replacements, a parallel new dashboard that is meant to take over, and removing a screen or record type because a newer one exists.
- **Only the founder can lift it, in writing, explicitly.** An instruction to "improve", "clean up", "modernise" or "build the dashboard for step N" is not that authorisation. If a step seems to need a replacement, stop and ask; do not decide.

What this means in practice for the order in section 3:

- Each of steps 2-7 ends with **a small addition to the existing dashboard** (a new detail on a call, a new status, a new section), not a new application. Step 8 is the sum of those additions plus whatever the founder separately approves; it is not a rewrite step.
- Every addition keeps the existing allow-list discipline: the dashboard shows only what the strict owner view lets through, has no login yet, and must never show personal contact data (the vault is reached only through the restricted lookup unless the founder decides otherwise).
- Existing records stay readable and shown, including the earlier plumbing-era test records; a new field must not make an old record fail to display.
- A pull request or session that touches the dashboard should say in its first line which existing behaviour it leaves unchanged.

## 1b. Working rule: one task at a time, each approved by the founder before it begins

**Reporting (founder, 2026-10-29).** **Standing communication rule (founder, 2026-10-29): every task report states our position across six areas: Intake, backend / current-call state, dashboard, Processing / booking, QC and Reporting.** For each area it says, separately and without merging them, what is **built** (code exists), what is **tested offline** (automated tests pass against stand-ins), what is **verified live** (seen on a real call or service, with the evidence), and what is **still missing**. It also names the **active task** and the **next authorized step** (or says that none is authorized). Section 5c-1 below holds the dated baseline that reports update; a report that has nothing new for an area says "unchanged". The rule changes how work is reported, not what is authorized: it starts no task, and the rules against using the API key, restarting, changing configuration or Twilio, calling and any live or paid action stand.

**[SUPERSEDED 2026-11-02 by section 0.5: the six-area report is replaced by the plain-English five-part report. Section 1b rules 1 to 8 (approval first, one task at a time, no commit, push or live action without its own request) stand.]**

**Status: required.** It applies to every step in section 3, every track in section 4, every mode switch in section 3a, and every small piece of work inside them. It adds to, and does not weaken, section 1a (the dashboard) and the standing rules in section 1.

1. **Approval first.** No development task begins until the founder has explicitly approved *that task*. The approval names the task (what it will change and what it will not). An earlier approval, a roadmap entry, a dependency order, or "the next step" is not approval.
2. **The cycle for each task, in this order, never skipped or reordered:**
   1. **Implementation**: the smallest change that does the approved task, in the files the task names.
   2. **Automated tests**: focused tests for the new behaviour, then the full suite. Failures are reported, not hidden; the task does not advance on a red or unrun suite.
   3. **Live validation, when applicable**: only for a task that has a live aspect (a real call, a real service, a real calendar), only with the founder's separate go-ahead for that live step, and only within the standing limits (no live or paid calls, public endpoints or restarts unless asked). Tasks with no live aspect state "not applicable" rather than skipping silently.
   4. **Founder review**: the founder is given what changed, the test results, the live-validation result if any, and the known limits, and decides whether the task is accepted.
3. **Completing one task never authorises the next.** After founder review the work stops. The next task, including the one that "obviously" follows, starts only after its own explicit approval under rule 1. A finished step in section 3 does not unlock the following row; it only makes that row eligible to be proposed.
4. **Rejection or rework** returns the task to implementation. It does not become a new task and does not start another.
5. **Commits, pushes, restarts, deployments, configuration or digest changes, and anything that touches live services** are each their own explicit request, never a side effect of finishing a task.
6. **Scope stays small.** If a task turns out to need more than was approved (another file area, another agent, a dashboard change), stop and ask; do not widen it.
7. **The dashboard constraint is part of every review.** A task that touches the dashboard states what existing behaviour it leaves unchanged (section 1a); the founder's review confirms it.
8. **Researching a tool is not approval to implement it (founder rule, recorded 2026-10-28).** Reading a tool's documentation or source, comparing it with the current design, or proposing a design for it does not change the active direction and is not approval to build it. Before the active direction changes (for example from the current voice connection to GPT-Live), the effects are explained to the founder (what changes, what is kept, what is at risk, what it costs, what stops working) and the founder's explicit approval is obtained. Work begun ahead of that approval is paused and preserved separately, not finished. Example: the GPT-Live consent edits were paused on 2026-10-28 and saved outside the repository (unfinished, untested); the committed Intake memory checkpoint remains the active build.

## 2. Delivery strategy

- **Cleaning first.** The first business type taken end to end is a cleaning service. It was chosen because a request is simple (rooms, date, time window), the harm of a mistake is low, and it exercises every stage (price, approval, availability, booking) without an emergency dimension.
- **[RETIRED 2026-11-02; HISTORICAL. Replaced by section 0.1: residential cleaning is the sole active product; carpet, window and pressure washing are research candidates only; towing, plumbing and HVAC are not planned. The bullets below are kept only as the record of the earlier reasoning.]**
- **Industry order is fixed by the founder: Cleaning → Towing → Plumbing → HVAC, one at a time.** Each later business is a new skill configuration plus whatever its own policy needs. None is started while the one before it is unfinished, and none is built in parallel with cleaning: the shared stages (contact, pricing, approval, availability, booking, QC) must be proven on one business type first. Starting each one is its own founder-approved task (section 1b); finishing one does not authorise the next.
  - **Towing** (second) is the hardest of the three: the caller is often roadside and stressed, location is the key field (and the most sensitive), time to arrival matters more than a date, and a wrong promise has an immediate cost. Its location and time-to-arrival handling must be designed and approved before its task begins; it stays in SIMULATION until then.
  - **Plumbing** (third) has an existing sample skill and the earlier live test used it. It adds emergency triage (a burst pipe is not a quote request) and a safety line the assistant must not cross.
  - **HVAC** (fourth) adds seasonal peaks, equipment and model details, and diagnosis the assistant must not give.
- A business type is "done" only when the same eight stages in section 3 are working for it, not when its intake works.
- **Cleaning is complete when, and only when, this whole chain works end to end in the order shown, each link verified before the next is relied on:**
  **verified contact → service → simulated quote → approval → availability → booking → QC → the existing dashboard.**
  - *Verified contact*: name, confirmed callback number and service address captured and held as in step 1.
  - *Service*: the caller's request is resolved to one of the business's configured services (step 2), or clarified, never guessed.
  - *Simulated quote*: a price or range from the mock approved-rate fixtures, using the quote-versus-estimate policy (section 3a). "Simulated" is deliberate: cleaning is not complete in LIVE terms until the business approves its real rules.
  - *Approval*: the customer's explicit yes, and the named human approver where the business requires one (step 4).
  - *Availability*: a slot read from the mock calendar (step 5).
  - *Booking*: written once and verified by reading it back (step 6).
  - *QC*: judged from evidence the booking agent did not write (step 7).
  - *The existing dashboard*: the result is visible to the owner as additions to the preserved prototype dashboard (section 1a), not a new one.
  Each link is its own approved task with its own implementation, automated tests, live validation where applicable, and founder review (section 1b). Cleaning moving to LIVE is a separate founder decision (section 3a).

## 2a. Founder requirement: how every call opens (implemented in code and tests; NOT live)

**Founder requirement (stated 2026-10-26; implemented in code and tests the same day; NOT live).** Every call opens by (1) introducing the **configured business** and the **agent** by name, (2) giving the **disclosure** (that the caller is speaking with an AI assistant, and the recording notice the business needs), and (3) mentioning a **special or promotion only if that business has approved one**, as exact approved text. The business name, agent name, disclosure wording and any special are per-business configuration, never invented or reworded by the model, and a special never carries a price or a commitment. In code all three are now configurable through an optional `opening` block (`src/opening.ts`); the live config has no such block, so live calls still use the original notice (`docs/PRODUCT_CONTINUITY.md` section 4, stage 2).

**What exists (uncommitted, tested locally, not live):** the optional `opening` block (business name, agent name, optional disclosure wording, optional special) is validated by the config gate and is part of the approval digest; the opening is fixed text built by code and spoken from the Twilio TwiML before the consent question, never generated by the model; a special is spoken only if the config carries one, exactly as written, and the code never adds one; the existing consent step is unchanged. **Still open (not approved):** applying it to the live config needs a new digest, new TwiML in the Twilio bin and a restart (each its own request, section 1b); the disclosure wording needs the business's legal check; no live call has heard it.

This is recorded as a requirement, not a built feature. It applies to every business in the order of section 2, cleaning first, and it does not change the preserved dashboard (section 1a).

**[2026-11-02: it applies to the residential cleaning agent; "the order of section 2" is historical.]**

## 3. Dependency order (cleaning-first critical path)

**HISTORICAL (four-agent plan).** Superseded for milestones and order by section 0. Kept as the record of how the earlier plan was reasoned.

Each step needs the one before it. Do not start a step on the assumption that an earlier one will "probably" work. Being next in this table does not authorise a step: each task still needs the founder's explicit approval first (section 1b).

| # | Step | Depends on | What "done" means |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Secure contact** | Intake, masker | A confirmed caller's name, callback number and service address are held encrypted, by tenant and call, for at most 30 days; never in a prompt, log or unauthenticated view; the owner can retrieve one contact through a restricted path. Contact is required before a request is actionable. |
| 2 | **Cleaning categories** | 1 | The cleaning skill is live with the categories the business actually sells (for example standard, deep, move-out), each with its own required fields. Unknown requests are clarified, not guessed. Needs the founder's category list; the sample has one category. |
| 3 | **Pricing** | 2 | A deterministic price or range from the category and the rooms/size, from a rule table the owner approves. The assistant never invents or quotes a price the table does not give. Built first in SIMULATION against mock approved-rate fixtures (section 3a); LIVE needs the business's approved rules (none exist). |
| 4 | **Approval** | 3 | Anything with money or a commitment pauses for a named human approver (the Processing agent already has the pause mechanism, with mock tools). |
| 5 | **Availability** | 4 | A calendar the owner controls (slots, crews, travel). Offering a slot reads from it. Built first against a mock calendar (section 3a); LIVE needs real availability. No calendar tool exists yet. |
| 6 | **Booking** | 5 | A confirmed slot is written once, with a confirmation the customer receives. Double booking is impossible by construction, and the booking is verified by reading it back (section 3a). No booking tool exists yet. |
| 7 | **QC** | 6 | The QC agent judges the booking from evidence Processing did not write (the calendar entry, the quote, the contact on file). Today it runs against a mock evidence reader only. |
| 8 | **Owner dashboard** | 1-7 | The owner sees, per call, what was asked, what was decided, what needs a human, and what was booked (the founder's two-minute requirement is in section 3b, proposed and not yet implemented). **Founder-locked (section 1a):** the existing dashboard is preserved and enhanced incrementally by the additions made in steps 2-7; it is never replaced, rebuilt, redesigned from scratch or reduced. |

The bridge between Intake and Processing is a prerequisite of steps 3-7: the phone path now keeps the sealed hand-off in memory and offers it to an optional seam that the server does not pass, so nothing passes it onward and nothing consumes it. `eligibleForProcessing` (confirmed, ready, handed off, no unverified address) is the single test the bridge must use.

## 3a. Simulation and live business modes (pricing and booking)

Pricing (step 3) and booking (steps 5-6) have two explicit modes. A business is in exactly one at a time, the mode is a named setting in that business's configuration (never inferred), and the mode is visible to the owner on the dashboard as an addition to the existing one (section 1a). Nothing is promoted from one to the other by default.

| | **SIMULATION** | **LIVE** |
|---|---|---|
| Purpose | Build and test the whole chain safely. This is the only mode used for cleaning development. | Serve real customers of a real business. |
| Rates | **Mock approved-rate fixtures**: invented rate tables, clearly labelled as fixtures, checked in as test data. | **Business-approved rules only**: a rate table the business owner has approved, recorded with who approved it and when. |
| Calendar | **Mock calendar**: an in-memory or fixture calendar with invented slots and crews. | **Real availability** read from the business's actual calendar; no slot is offered that the calendar does not show as free. |
| Customer-facing words | Every price, slot and confirmation is marked as a simulation in the owner's view, and the assistant says it plainly if a simulated call is ever spoken to a real person. No real customer is committed to anything. | Prices, slots and confirmations are real commitments, so every one passes the approval and verification rules below. |
| Data | Synthetic or invented personas only (standing rule). | Real customer data under the contact rules in step 1. |
| Switching | Cannot be turned into LIVE by editing the fixtures. | LIVE requires the founder's explicit written approval **and** the business's approved rules **and** real availability connected, checked before the first call. If any is missing the business stays in SIMULATION. |

Rules that apply to both modes (they are tested in simulation so they are known to work before live):

- **Per-business configurable services.** Each business defines its own services, the fields each needs, and its own rates. Cleaning's categories are one configuration, not code. No service, price or policy is hard-wired for one business.
- **Quote versus estimate policy, per business.** The business chooses, per service, whether the assistant may give a **quote** (a firm price the business will honour) or only an **estimate** (a range that a person confirms), or neither (a person always prices it). The assistant uses the exact word the policy allows and never upgrades an estimate into a quote. If no policy is set, the answer is "a person will price this", with no figure.
- **Customer approval.** A price or a booking is not final until the customer has heard it in fixed words and said an explicit yes. The business's own approval step (step 4) is separate and comes first for anything the business wants a human to see.
- **Verified booking.** A booking is complete only when the slot was read from the calendar, written once, and then read back from the calendar by something other than the writer (the QC agent's evidence rule) and found to match. A booking that cannot be verified is not reported as booked.
- **Safe human fallback.** At any point (no rule matches, availability unreadable, a tool fails, the customer is unsure or upset, a price is outside the approved table, the call hits a limit), the assistant stops quoting and booking, says nothing it cannot back, and hands the request to a person as an unconfirmed item with the reason recorded. The customer is never told a person will call back unless the business has a callback process and a stored callback number (this is why contact capture comes first).

Cleaning development in SIMULATION proves these rules; towing, plumbing and HVAC (in that order, section 2) each repeat the same switch to LIVE only after their own rates and availability exist. Towing in particular should not leave SIMULATION until its location and time-to-arrival handling are designed.

**[HISTORICAL 2026-11-02: towing, plumbing and HVAC are retired. The simulation-then-live rule applies to the residential cleaning agent: nothing goes live without the founder's written decision.]**

## 3b. Founder requirement: the two-minute owner dashboard (PROPOSED; not yet implemented or validated)

**Status: a requirement recorded from the founder, not a design, not built, and not tested with an owner.** It is delivered only as incremental additions to the existing dashboard under section 1a (preserve it, evolve it, never replace or redesign it from scratch); it restates no part of that decision and weakens none of it. It is built only after the dependencies below, one separately approved task at a time (section 1b), and it follows `docs/PRODUCT_CONTINUITY.md` (which governs the dashboard) on the owner view's strict allow-list.

**The requirement.** A business owner, with no training, can open the existing dashboard and within **two minutes**, for any call, answer these five questions:

1. **Who called, and how do I securely get their contact details?** The open page identifies a call in a privacy-safe way (time, kind, and whether a contact is on file). Name, callback number and service address are **never** on the open page. They are retrieved through a separate, authenticated and audited reveal action. Until real owner authentication exists, retrieval stays the restricted command-line lookup.
2. **What service did they ask for?** The service type and its key details (for cleaning: standard, deep, or move-in/move-out, and the answers collected, such as rooms, date and time window).
3. **What was quoted, and did the customer approve it?** The quote or estimate (or "a person will price this"), and whether the customer gave an explicit yes.
4. **Is there a verified appointment?** Booked and verified, pending, failed, or not booked. "Verified" means the booking was read back from the calendar by something other than the writer (section 3a).
5. **What should I do next?** One clear next action for the owner, extending the existing next-steps list.

**Simulated outcomes are clearly labelled.** Any quote, slot, approval or booking produced in SIMULATION (section 3a) is marked as a simulation wherever it appears, is never styled or counted like a real outcome, and never implies a real customer was committed to anything.

**Acceptance (proposed, not yet run):** the founder, looking at the existing dashboard in a real browser (never yet done), can answer the five questions for a sample call in two minutes, in light and dark themes and at phone width.

**Placement: each increment comes after the step it depends on, and nothing is shown before the thing it shows exists.**

| Dashboard increment (an addition to the existing dashboard) | Needs first |
|---|---|
| "Contact on file" marker and the service requested with its answers | Steps 1 and 2 (secure contact, cleaning categories) |
| Authenticated contact reveal (replacing the command-line lookup) | Step 1, plus real owner authentication (FND-02 / R01, continuity §4 stage 5) and the founder's decision on audit and retention |
| Simulated quote label and the customer's approval status | Steps 3 and 4 (pricing, approval) |
| Appointment status, verified or not, labelled simulated or real | Steps 5 and 6 (availability, booking) and the section 3a verified-booking rule |
| QC result in plain words | Step 7 (QC) |
| Owner summary and history from Reporting | The Reporting entry shown on the dashboard (continuity §3: not yet) |
| The single next action covering all of the above | All of the above |

**Constraints that stay in force.** Every addition keeps the strict owner-view allow-list, its tests, the routes `/`, `/data.js` and `/api/owner`, and old records displaying. Adding a field to the allow-list is a contract change that needs its tests and the founder's say-so. The current rule that nothing on the dashboard says or implies a booking stays until a verified booking exists; relaxing it is its own approved contract change, and simulated bookings stay labelled. The dashboard has no login today, so nothing personal may be added before authentication exists.

## 4. Tracks that run beside the critical path

**HISTORICAL (four-agent plan).** Superseded by section 0; read only for the reasoning.

These are real work, but they do not block (and must not be allowed to quietly change) the order above. Each needs its own decision before it starts.

- **Speech-to-speech.** Replacing the current speech-to-text, text model, text-to-speech path with a voice-native model. Potential gains: latency and naturalness. Costs and risks: the masker works on text, so personal data must be handled before it can reach a voice model, which is a different design problem; the read-back is built from text; cost and logging differ. Evaluate on synthetic calls only; do not adopt until masking can be proven equivalent.
- **Memory.** Remembering a returning customer across calls. This is personal data kept beyond one call and beyond the 30-day pilot window, so it needs the founder's retention, consent and deletion policy first. Nothing here should be built on the pilot vault.
- **UI work.** Dashboard evolution, owner screens, contact lookup beyond the command line, report pages. Governed by the founder-locked constraint in section 1a: preserve the existing dashboard, enhance it incrementally, never replace, rebuild, redesign from scratch or delete functionality without the founder's explicit written authorization. The read-back, hang-up and owner screens have been tested locally only.

## 5. Verified status (2026-10-26; a historical snapshot: the current status board is section 5c)

Verified by running, not assumed:

- **1261 tests pass, 0 fail, 2 todo** (full suite, `poc/intake-runner`). Snapshot 2026-10-27 (a dated automated-test figure, not maintained): the last full run of `npm test` in `poc/intake-runner` was **1261 passing, 0 failing, 2 todo**, taken after the silence fix (reply timing limits, refusal handling). Mock and stand-in tests only: it says nothing about live behaviour. Re-run the suite for the current figure.
- **Git state, 2026-10-27.** Everything through commit `43ad77e` (Option A and the contact vault; address confirmation, `caller_confirmed` and the pricing fixture; the dashboard wording fix; the silence recovery `4b8932b`; the transport seam `15c0862`; the OpenAI text adapter and masking fixes `43ad77e`) is on `main` and on GitHub. The OpenAI text adapter, the two masking fixes and the record updates are in `43ad77e`, also on GitHub. **Local and uncommitted:** the Realtime adapter and simulator, the new `reply_failed` event, the unsent read-back fix and this roadmap update. Read `git status --short` and `git log origin/main..HEAD --oneline` for the current state.
- **Option A is implemented**: one fresh validated Intake run per caller turn, the next field chosen by code (`src/next-field.ts`), the dialogue model given one `NEXT` line of names only, an 8000 ms preview budget, a late or failed result chooses nothing, and an incomplete request is kept unconfirmed with no follow-up promise. The confirmed closing line is "Thank you. Your request is recorded. Goodbye."
- **Server state (2026-10-27):** the Drew Cleaning test configuration has been applied and the server was started with it (contact capture on, the code of commit `46fbdde`). The approved Drew greeting was used on one live call on 2026-10-27. **The silence fix below is committed (`4b8932b`) and pushed, but is NOT loaded by that server:** it needs a founder-approved restart before any call can use it. Backups of the earlier config and database are outside the repository.
- **Drew Cleaning test greeting.** **Approved Drew test greeting (applied to the test configuration and the TwiML Bin; used on one live call on 2026-10-27):** "$25 off a qualifying first standard cleaning", spoken after the business introduction and before the unchanged disclosure and the yes-to-continue consent. **Existing SIMULATION fixture terms it must stay aligned with:** one rule, Standard cleaning for a house or apartment with up to 2 bedrooms and 2 bathrooms, a $199.99 base less a $25.00 first-clean discount, priced only when the caller confirmed the details and that this is their first cleaning with the business; Deep, move-in/move-out and commercial requests go to a person for pricing. The greeting does not state those terms (property size, first-clean confirmation, expiry, stacking) and they are not otherwise configured. **The fixture is not connected to the phone and is not approved for quotes to real customers;** the offer is not tied to any live price rule, and the disclosure wording still needs the business's legal check.
- **First live Drew call (2026-10-27), read only from fixed-code diagnostics and the saved owner view (no speech or contact data is recorded here):** consent was granted and two questions were generated and sent with no refused write; after the caller's answer about the property the model returned no text on two consecutive turns (`REPLY_EMPTY`: zero characters and no exception, about 1.2-1.5 s each), so nothing was spoken for about a minute; the connection was then closed normally by the peer (code 1000) about 47 s after the last prompt, not mid-reply. The request was saved unconfirmed (property type and bedrooms captured, no address). **Not established:** why the model returned no text (its stop reason, token counts and non-text blocks were not logged then), which field was being asked, who hung up, what a second very short peer connection (about 131 ms, no call start) was, and whether this explains the two earlier silent calls.
- **Silence fix (2026-10-27; committed in `4b8932b` and pushed, not yet run on a live call).** One caller turn still gets one awaited reply, now with bounded time and a fixed-wording safety net. (1) **Empty reply:** the model streams nothing and does not fail -> one retry, then the server asks the same thing itself in fixed words built only from the configured field, choices or category names (`fallbackText`; no caller words, no brackets, no prices or promises), recorded in the dialogue history like any spoken line. (2) **Stalled start:** no first word within `replyFirstWordMs` (default 5000 ms) is treated the same way, the retry waiting at most `replyRetryMs` (4000 ms): worst case about 9 s before the fixed wording. (3) **Stall after it starts:** no next piece within `replyGapMs` (4000 ms), or a reply longer than `replyTotalMs` (15000 ms), is closed with an end marker where it stands; its words are never repeated, never kept in the history and never count as a delivered question, so a plain yes after a half-sent first-cleaning question is not taken as its answer; the fixed question follows once, and only that complete question can be answered. (4) **Model refusal** (the model's own `REFUSAL` stop reason, never inferred from text): no retry and no further intake question; any words produced are closed, one fixed goodbye with the emergency line is said, the call ends and the request so far is saved unconfirmed (needs a person, not handed off; nothing is saved if no caller line exists yet). (5) **Fallback cannot be sent:** the request is saved unconfirmed and the call ends. (6) A caller talking over any of this still stops it and nothing more is said; a model that fails because it was stopped no longer ends the call, while a real model failure still ends it with the fixed farewell and is not retried. (7) An empty try sends no end marker, so a retry or the fixed wording never follows a stray one. **New log codes** (fixed words and numbers only): `REPLY_RETRY`, `REPLY_FALLBACK_SENT`, `REPLY_FALLBACK_FAILED`, `REPLY_STALLED`, `REPLY_REFUSED`; an empty or refused try also logs the attempt number, whether it timed out, the model's stop reason as a fixed word, its output-token count and its count of non-text blocks.
- **Remaining live-test limits for the silence fix.** None of it has run on a live call. The cause of the empty model output is still unknown; the new stop-reason, token and block numbers should show it on the next call, and the fix hides the silence, it does not explain it. No real model refusal has ever been observed, so that path rests on mocked stop reasons; any refusal ends the call (there is no second chance). Whether the phone provider plays a fixed question that follows a cut-off sentence naturally, and whether the interruption and end-marker behaviour matches the mocks, is unverified. The mid-stream stall bound is the handler's own timer; the SDK's 60 s timeout is not relied on. The fixed wording is generic (for example a lower-cased field name) and may sound stilted. The cause of the peer's close, the short second connection and the earlier silent calls remain unexplained. The Processing handoff is still unwired, the name is not read back, and the main read-back yes is not delivery-checked. One founder-placed call with invented details on a restarted build is needed before any of this counts as validated.
- **OpenAI voice plan (2026-10-27; planning and offline steps only, nothing live).** **Founder decisions:** (1) the phone voice is **OpenAI speech-to-speech**, with our server staying in charge of consent, masking, the next-question rules, exact read-backs, records and handoff ("Option 2"; a cascade of OpenAI speech-to-text, our code and OpenAI text-to-speech is the fallback if the model cannot read fixed text exactly); (2) the voice is a **Drew Cleaning receptionist persona**; (3) founder tests use **invented details only** (an invented caller, address and number), and raw caller audio reaching OpenAI is acceptable for those tests only (real customer calls need a separate data-terms and disclosure decision first); (4) **OpenAI replaces the Claude API inside the phone agent entirely** (the spoken replies and the Intake extraction), so rollback is a git revert plus restoring the Twilio TwiML Bin (commit `4b8932b` is the rollback point), not a setting; the Claude adapters stay in the code until they are retired in a later, separately approved step. **Claude Code remains the founder's builder and tester** for the whole project (it writes, tests and reviews the code and the records; it is not part of the product and is not replaced). **The persona is a voice and a manner, not new authority:** it must still say it is an automated AI assistant, keep the existing disclosure and consent, never claim to be a person, and give no prices, availability, promises or follow-up calls beyond what the cleaning skill and the simulation fixtures already allow. The persona is written offline (step 4 below) but is not wired in, not approved by the business and has not been heard on a call. **Ordered plan:** 0 decisions (done); 1 transport seam (done, `15c0862`); 2 OpenAI text adapter (done offline, `43ad77e`); 3 offline Realtime adapter and simulator (done offline, `57d0f1f`); **4 persona (done offline, committed locally, NOT wired and NOT live):** `src/persona.ts` makes a business-configurable manner from a closed list of traits (warm, friendly, clear, patient, professional, concise, calm), one or two sentences per reply and a calm or normal pace, with no free-text field, so it cannot carry an offer, price or promise; the example `examples/persona-drew-cleaning.json` is Drew Cleaning (warm, clear, patient, professional, concise; two sentences; calm). It adds one line to the text model's instruction after every existing rule and before the NEXT line (which stays last), and one sentence to the voice adapter's speak-only instruction; only an object made by `createPersona` is accepted. The AI introduction, disclosure and consent still come only from `opening.ts`, the cleaning questions from the next-question rules, and the safety rules are unchanged (13 persona tests, including a sweep of 508 combinations for added numbers, prices, offers or promises). **Not wired:** the live entry point, the config and the approval digest do not use it (adding a config key changes the digest and needs the founder's approval), the wording is a proposal for the business to approve, and no model has followed it or been heard on a call. The unrelated `persona_names` setting lists caller names to mask; **5 voice connection (the offline part is done, local; the real connection is NOT built):** `src/realtime-bridge.ts` joins the phone's audio stream (Twilio Media Streams style, mu-law 8 kHz) to a voice socket and is the link the Realtime adapter uses; `startRealtimeCall` hands the adapter and the Drew persona to the unchanged call logic. It sets both audio directions to the phone's format and refuses any other; starts the call only when the stream has started and the socket is open; forwards caller audio only after the consent notice has been played to the end (earlier audio is dropped, not stored); reports a reply finished only when the phone confirms it played (an unconfirmed or unsendable reply counts as failed, so the question is asked again); works out how much of an interrupted reply was played from the phone's markers and clears the queue, never clearing the notice or a read-back; ends the call by closing the stream (the TwiML must continue with a hang-up verb, so no phone credential is needed); drops and counts bad, oversized or non-base64 frames; ends a call whose socket keeps refusing audio; and enforces its own 10-minute ceiling. Tested with wire-level stand-ins for the phone and the voice service (`tests/helpers/realtime-wire-sim.ts`, `tests/realtime-bridge.test.ts`, 28 tests, four of them with the real call logic on top). **Aligned with the documented Realtime schema (read 2026-10-27, offline, local):** every settings update names `session.type: "realtime"`; a cancel names the reply it cancels (a reply requested out of band is not in the default conversation, so a bare cancel would not reach it), and a caller who talks before the reply is reported as created has it cancelled the moment it is; a second talk-over sends no second cancel, a finished reply is never cancelled, and a service that ignores the cancel cannot stall the call (the reply is given up after the wait and its late audio is dropped by the bridge); cancel and delete requests carry their own ids, so the error a service gives for a cancel that came too late or an item already gone is not counted as a fault of the call (any other error still is); and every speech request repeats the speak-only rule and the Drew manner, because the instructions of a request may replace those of the session. The two simulators now refuse what the documented schema refuses, and 12 new tests (11 in `tests/realtime-schema.test.ts`, 1 through the bridge) cover it. **Stream TwiML (built offline, local, NOT applied to any Twilio number):** `src/stream-twiml.ts` writes the TwiML Bin for the OpenAI route: `<Connect><Stream url="wss://.../relay/SECRET"><Parameter name="to" .../></Stream></Connect>` then `<Hangup />`, with no `<Say>` (the voice service speaks the consent notice; a Say after the stream would play after the goodbye). A Media Streams `start` message carries no dialed number, so the number is a stream parameter named `to`, which the bridge reads and the call logic checks against its own table; it is written either as a literal E.164 number (one Bin per number, the safe form) or as the `{{To}}` variable (UNVERIFIED: Twilio's TwiML Bin page documents variables only in element text, not `{{To}}` or attribute values). Twilio runs the verbs after the stream only once the server closes the socket, so the `<Hangup />` ends the call when the bridge closes the stream after the goodbye; if the stream never connects the same hang-up runs at once and the caller hears silence, then the line drops. The address must be `wss`, with no query string, on the existing long secret path. The live ConversationRelay TwiML (`opening.ts`) and the phone number's setup are unchanged and nothing in the live path uses the new module. **How the stream is authenticated (offline check):** Twilio's pages say to validate `X-Twilio-Signature` on a stream (lowercase header on WebSockets) but do not say which URL is signed or whether parameters are included; the existing WebSocket server (secret path plus a signature of the address tried in four URL forms, connection cap, lockout) is reused unchanged and was tested over loopback with a Media Streams style conversation (8 tests in `tests/stream-twiml.test.ts`): a signed handshake on the secret path is accepted and runs the bridge, closing the stream sends close code 1000, and a missing, wrong or other-address signature, a wrong token or a wrong path never reaches the bridge. The TwiML parameters arrive after the handshake and are NOT signed, so anyone past the handshake can name any dialed number; the call logic's number table is what refuses one that is not ours. **Still unverified:** which URL form Twilio signs for a stream, whether `{{To}}` works in a Parameter, and whether the port is dropped when signing. **OpenAI voice socket (built offline, local, never connected):** `src/openai-voice-socket.ts` is the `VoiceSocket` the bridge is given: it connects to `wss://api.openai.com/v1/realtime?model=<model>` with an `Authorization: Bearer` header (and an optional `OpenAI-Safety-Identifier`). Node 24's built-in WebSocket sends custom request headers (checked on loopback with an invented token), so NO package is needed. The key comes from a `getKey()` function, is read once per call, must be header-safe, goes only into the handshake headers and is never stored, logged, put in an address, an error or a diagnostic; a missing or unusable key means no connection is attempted and the bridge ends the call. The address is fixed in code, so configuration cannot send the key elsewhere; the model is validated and has no default. A refused handshake (an error then close 1006) or a connection that does not open in time (5 s) is reported once as closed, and there is never a retry (a retry could repeat a paid call). It sends text only, only while open, and refuses past a backlog limit; drops binary and oversize frames; and closes idempotently with a legal close code, detaching every handler so a late event does nothing. Diagnostics are fixed codes only. 19 tests with a mocked connection (`tests/openai-voice-socket.test.ts`), including the socket under the real bridge. **Not built / live-test blockers:** (1) the key is entered only by the founder, in their own terminal, as `OPENAI_API_KEY` at serve time; (2) the composition is built (next paragraph) but the founder must still choose the models and the voice, and no dollar ceiling is computed because OpenAI prices for these models are not on file; (3) the public endpoint, the stream TwiML in a Bin and the Twilio number change each need the founder's approval; (4) the unverified live facts listed under "Kept open" (the URL form Twilio signs for a stream, `{{To}}` in a Parameter, G.711 over the Realtime WebSocket, whether a request's instructions replace the session's, the error shape for a refused cancel or delete, the model and transcription model names, zero data retention, latency, cost per minute); (5) the open gaps (consent-before-audio, the unknown-number notice, the interrupted read-back, the `$25` exact-match risk, the masker gaps); and (6) the later live steps (the paid Intake check, the spike call, acceptance and a rollback rehearsal) each need separate approval. **Mocked OpenAI phone connection (built offline, local, never connected):** a new composition root, `src/founder-voice-entry.ts`, with `founder-voice-service.ts`, `founder-voice-gate.ts`, `openai-http-client.ts` and `openai-dialogue-model.ts`, joins the pieces above into one service: the existing WebSocket server takes a Twilio Media Streams connection (secret path and signature), the bridge hands it to the OpenAI voice socket, the Drew persona is on every speech request, and the EXISTING call logic (consent notice, masker, next-question rules, exact read-backs, limits, record store, contact vault) runs unchanged on top, with the Intake extraction and the spoken wording both answered by OpenAI's text model (the Responses API over `fetch`, no package; the wording is not streamed because the voice speaks a reply only after its full text exists). The live Claude and ConversationRelay path (`founder-call-entry.ts`, the call config and its digest) is untouched, as are the dashboard and the cleaning workflow. A separate private `founder-voice.config.json` holds the exact text, Realtime and transcription models (no defaults), the voice, a bound on OpenAI text calls and the Drew persona; the example carries `REPLACE-WITH-` placeholders that fail validation on purpose. The voice gate keeps every gate of the existing call (digest, `INTAKE_ALLOW_LIVE_CALLS`, Twilio token, 30-day expiry, caps) and adds `FOUNDER_VOICE_APPROVED_DIGEST` (a digest of the voice config bound to the call config's digest, so editing either file withdraws the approval) and the presence of `OPENAI_API_KEY`. The dry run (default) prints both digests, the bounds, the gate and the stream TwiML for each number and opens nothing; `--serve` is refused until the whole gate passes. **Security review of the execution-context change:** `founder-voice-entry.ts` is a second composition root that mints an execution context, so the three tests that pin the minters (`founder-call-gate`, `import-pinning`, `intake-entry`) now list it, still as exact lists of four files. Its mint is identical in power to the existing entry's: agent `intake`, the single grant `intake:run`, a system identity, a correlation id from the call reference, and the tenant fixed by the config and re-checked on every call (a different tenant throws); the caller, the stream and the voice service cannot choose any of it. The mint sits behind the serve gate, and `founder-voice-service.ts` receives the minting function and never mints. The change widens the set of files that may mint by one, and nothing else. **Tests (mocked services only):** 1376 tests, 1374 passing, 0 failing, 2 todo at that checkpoint (1389 and 1387 after the settings below); the new tests cover the HTTP client and dialogue model (12), the gate and the command line (10, run as a child process in dry-run and refused-serve mode), and the whole chain over a real loopback WebSocket server (11): a full consented call to a saved confirmed record, privacy (raw caller words never reach the text model, the key only in the handshake, no speech in logs), a declined consent, an unknown or missing dialed number, a bad signature or path, a missing key, an empty or failing text model, a caller hang-up and a dropped voice connection. **Reasoning and transcription settings (built offline, local):** `src/realtime-settings.ts` and the voice config now carry the settings the OpenAI documentation points to (client-events and Responses references, read 2026-10-27, summarised by a fetch tool). `realtime_reasoning` (minimal, low, medium, high, xhigh; default **minimal**, because the voice only speaks words it is handed) is sent on the session and on every speech request; `text_reasoning` (none, minimal, low, medium, high, xhigh, max; default **none**, which gpt-6-luna documents and which keeps every spoken line and every extraction fast) is sent on every text request; `transcription` hints (`languages`, two-letter codes, default English; `keywords`, single-line hints such as the business name) are sent only for `gpt-transcribe` and `gpt-live-transcribe`, the two transcribers documented to take them. Not sent, on purpose: `prompt` (not documented for those models) and `delay` (the reference says gpt-realtime-whisper only, the transcription guide says gpt-live-transcribe: the two disagree). Invalid values are refused up front with fixed text; the dry run prints the settings in effect; changing any setting withdraws the voice approval because the digest covers the file as written. The offline stand-ins now refuse what the documented schema refuses (an effort outside the enum, `prompt`, `delay`, both language fields, hints for another transcriber, a bracket in a keyword), and 13 new tests cover defaults, explicit values, invalid settings and the unchanged earlier behaviour. **Proposed settings for the Drew test (the founder's to approve):** voice model `gpt-realtime-2.1` (fallback `gpt-realtime-2.1-mini`), transcriber `gpt-live-transcribe`, text model `gpt-6-luna`, voice `marin`. **Settings confirmed live (the founder's real connection check, 2026-10-27, see the evidence paragraph below):** `gpt-6-luna` with reasoning `none` and a strict JSON schema; `gpt-realtime-2.1` with reasoning `minimal`, `audio/pcmu` in both directions, the voice `marin`, and `gpt-transcribe` with English and keyword hints accepted in a Realtime session. **Still UNVERIFIED model and voice settings:** that `gpt-transcribe` (or any transcriber) handles real 8 kHz telephone speech (the check sent no audio); the `delay` conflict above (not sent); and the model names themselves, which may change.
**OpenAI connection check (built offline, then run by the founder with a real key and passed: see the evidence paragraph below):** `src/founder-voice-check.ts` (command line) with `src/voice-check.ts` (logic), `src/hidden-prompt.ts` and `src/drew-test-opening.ts` is the smallest paid test. **Content:** invented only, no caller audio, no phone, no Twilio; the notice it speaks is Drew's actual test greeting, **with the approved "$25 off a qualifying first standard cleaning" special kept word for word** (once, after the introduction, before the disclosure) and the proposed disclosure that names OpenAI (`DREW_TEST_OPENING`; a proposal for the founder and a lawyer; for the invented-details test only; real customers need a separate wording and a data-terms decision; the file is not read as configuration). **What it does:** two OpenAI text requests built by the same code the phone service uses (an Intake classification with a strict JSON schema and one spoken-wording reply with the Drew persona), then one Realtime connection through the same socket, bridge and adapter the phone service uses with a fake phone: the session settings, the notice, one short line, and a longer line cancelled part-way by its id. **Spending limit:** a ceiling is computed from a dated price table before anything is sent (about $0.14 on `gpt-realtime-2.1`, $0.04 on the mini; an unknown model has no price, so no ceiling and no run); a run needs `--spend`, a `--max-usd` at or above the ceiling and at most $1.00, an interactive terminal, and the ceiling typed back; the run itself is bounded by 75 seconds, 3 spoken lines and 2 text requests, enforced before anything is sent, and a send of caller audio is refused outright. The report estimates spend from the usage OpenAI reports. **Private key entry:** the key is typed at the terminal with nothing shown (raw mode, no echo), held in memory for the run and dropped; it is never printed, logged, written, put in the command line or exported; piping is impossible (a real terminal is required); `--key-from-env` is the one named alternative and still needs the terminal for the confirmation. The report holds fixed codes, numbers, booleans and, for an error, only its type, code and field name. **Key-entry hardening (2026-10-27, after the founder's first attempt reported "no usable key was entered" at the key prompt):** the likely causes were reproduced with fake input and fixed: an Enter left over from the confirmation prompt ended the entry empty (now an Enter with nothing entered is ignored, with a fixed hint); Ctrl+V arrives in a raw console as a control key and pastes nothing (now a hint says to right-click); an escape sequence, including bracketed-paste markers even when split across chunks, was taken as a cancel or as characters (now skipped); and a paste with spaces, quotes, a byte-order mark or zero-width characters was rejected (now cleaned). A bad entry is now reported as its kind and length only, never a character, and up to three tries are allowed in one run. **Not verified on a real Windows console:** all of this was tested with fake terminals and fake keys, so the actual terminal behaviour is still unconfirmed; if it fails again the new message names the kind of problem and the length received. **First paid check, run by the founder (2026-10-27):** `text_structured` passed (TEXT_STRICT_SCHEMA_OK) and `text_wording` passed (TEXT_WORDING_OK), so **`gpt-6-luna` with reasoning `none` and a strict JSON schema is confirmed live** (it settles the text-model unknowns). The Realtime part failed with CONNECTION_FAILED, `opened:false`: the connection never opened, and nothing was spoken. **Why it could not be told:** Node's built-in WebSocket reports a 401, a 404, a closed socket and an unreachable host identically (an empty error event and close code 1006; checked against local listeners), so the status was invisible. **Diagnosis built (offline, local, not run against the service):** `src/realtime-probe.ts` asks the questions that can be answered, with only an HTTP status and a strict error code ever shown: the WebSocket handshake made by hand over node:https (101 means accepted and closed at once; otherwise the status and error code), `GET /v1/models/<model>`, `POST /v1/realtime/client_secrets` (a 10-second Realtime session for the same model, whose secret is never read) and `GET /v1/models` (only the ids of visible voice, live and transcription models). It is shown automatically when the Realtime part fails, and `node src/founder-voice-check.ts --diagnose` runs it alone with no speech and no text request (key typed privately as before). The check now also prints the voice connection's event codes. `realtime-probe.ts` is the third file allowed to import node:https (the pin in `founder-call-gate.test.ts` lists it); its host is fixed to api.openai.com. **Not billed to the best of the documentation (unverified).** **Second finding (the founder ran `--diagnose`):** the hand-made handshake answered 101 and the model lookup and the Realtime permission both answered 200, so the key, the model and the network were all fine, yet the check still failed with `opened:false`. **The defect, reproduced locally and fixed:** the command line made the voice socket as an argument to `runCheck`, so the connection started immediately, but the check ran its two text requests first (seconds) and only then created the bridge. The socket opened, and its "open" event (and any first messages) fired while nobody was listening; the bridge attached its handlers later, never heard "open", waited out its open timeout and reported CONNECTION_FAILED. The mocked tests had passed because mocked text requests finish in microseconds, before the mocked connection opened. Three tests that reproduce it (a socket whose handlers are attached after it opened, one that closed before anyone listened, and a whole check with slow text requests) failed on the old code and pass on the fix. **The fix:** `src/openai-voice-socket.ts` now keeps what happens before the bridge is listening (the open, the newest 100 messages, the close) and delivers it in order as soon as each handler is attached; nothing else about it changed. **Compared with the hand-made handshake,** the WebSocket client also offers a `permessage-deflate` extension and sends browser-style headers (Accept, Accept-Language, Sec-Fetch-Mode, User-Agent, Pragma, Cache-Control, Accept-Encoding); those differences were measured against a local listener and are not the confirmed cause. If the next run still fails with a "Voice connection events" line that has no VOICE_OPEN, the extension offer would be the next suspect; with VOICE_OPEN present, the cause was this defect. **Can prove:** the key and model names work; the session settings are accepted, or which one is rejected; the event names the adapter relies on; fixed text including the "$25" is read back closely enough to pass the adapter's check; a cancel that names a reply stops an out-of-band reply; the text settings work with a strict schema; roughly what a few seconds of speech cost. **Cannot prove:** anything about a phone call (Twilio's signature form, real 8 kHz audio, the transcriber on real speech since no audio is sent so the transcription model is only checked for being accepted, live interruptions, latency over a line), the consent-before-audio gap, how the call logic behaves, or anything about real customers. **The "$25" fix:** the exact-match check now treats "$25" and "twenty-five dollars" (and "25" and "twenty-five") as the same words; it still fails closed, which is safe but would end a call at the notice, on a comma-grouped amount, a decimal amount such as $199.99 or a spoken-hundreds amount, so the greeting must not use one. (`.gitignore` now also ignores the private `founder-voice.config.json`.) 18 new tests cover the opening, the plan and ceiling, the run against mocks (healthy, wrong words, rejected setting, refused or silent connection, text failures), the key entry, the command line in plan and refused-spend modes only, and the "$25" matching; 1433 tests, 1431 passing, 0 failing, 2 todo (after the key-entry hardening, the Realtime diagnosis, the late-handler fix and the voice-file preparation). **The intermittent test failure, identified:** one run showed a single failure; 11 full runs afterwards were clean. A focused search then caught it once in 12 paired runs of the ten voice-path test files (two run at the same time): `tests/founder-voice-service.test.ts`, the "full call" test, timed out on its 4-second wait for `CALL_STARTED` (the phone-side stand-in echoes markers on a 5 ms poll, so a starved process can miss the wait). It did not repeat in 40 runs of that file alone (8 at a time). It is a timing sensitivity of that test under heavy load, not a defect found in the product code, and it does not touch the connection check (which acknowledges markers directly and is bounded by its own wall clock). Nothing was changed to "fix" it; the test's timeout message now carries the log codes and message kinds, so a recurrence will explain itself.
**EVIDENCE: the real OpenAI connection check passed all eight checks (run by the founder with their own key, 2026-10-27, after the late-handler fix `d76c94a`).** The eight: `text_structured`, `text_wording`, `session_accepted`, `notice_verified` (Drew's actual test greeting, **including the "$25 off a qualifying first standard cleaning" special, was spoken by the real voice and matched the adapter's check**), `speech_verified`, `cancel_by_id` (a cancel that names the reply stopped an out-of-band reply), `audio_flowed` and `cleanup`. **What it settles:** the key, project and models have Realtime access; the session settings in the Settings paragraph above are accepted; the event names and shapes the adapter and bridge rely on are the real ones (session.updated, response.created, the audio deltas, the transcript, response.done); the voice reads fixed text, including the amount, closely enough to pass the fail-closed match; a cancel by reply id works for out-of-band replies; the probe and the WebSocket client reach the service from the founder's network; and a few seconds of speech cost a few cents. **What it does NOT settle (its limits, by design):** it was not a phone call. No caller audio was sent, so the transcriber, voice detection, interruption by a live caller, the bridge's played-time estimate from Twilio marks and the consent-before-audio gap are untested against the service; Twilio's stream signature form, `{{To}}`/Parameter handling, real 8 kHz mu-law audio over a phone line and latency over a line are untested; the call logic ran against no real service; nothing about real customers. The founder did not report the exact spend or whether the notice matched character for character or only in spoken form, so neither is recorded here. **The first phone test is therefore the first real evidence on all of those, and it is limited to invented details.**
**Private files for the phone test (prepared 2026-10-27, git-ignored, not committed):** `node src/founder-voice-prepare.ts` (new, `src/founder-voice-prepare.ts`, 8 tests) wrote `founder-voice-call.config.json`, a separate copy of the existing call config with its own approval id (`founder-voice-001`), the business's special kept exactly as in the source, the default disclosure replaced by the one that names OpenAI and says this is a test line, and a NEW random secret path on the same tunnel host and port; every other value (number, caps, ports, database, kill file, expiry, skill) is copied. The existing `founder-call.config.json`, its digest, its running server and the live Twilio Bin were not touched, so they are the rollback. `founder-voice.config.json` already existed from the connection check and was left as it was. **Rollback:** stop the voice server (Ctrl+C), start the existing server with the existing config and its approved digest, and point the number back at the existing TwiML Bin. The two servers share the same ports and database, so only one runs at a time. **Activation order (each step the founder's, one at a time):** (1) run the voice entry's dry run and read the two digests, the gate lines and the stream TwiML; (2) approve the digests and set the environment in the founder's own terminal; (3) stop the old server and start the voice entry with `--serve`; (4) start the tunnel; (5) create the new TwiML Bin from the printed stream TwiML and point the test number at it; (6) place the call with invented details.
**FIRST PHONE CALL on the OpenAI route (founder, 2026-10-27, two calls 11 seconds apart): it said "Hello" and hung up.** **Evidence:** Twilio logged both calls as Completed, exactly 5 seconds each, with no error event (the only error in the log was the earlier ConversationRelay 64102 from the dead tunnel); no record was saved; the server window had been started with a console filter that hid every `[founder-voice]` line, so the server's own diagnostics were lost (the code also logged nothing about why the bridge or adapter ended a call). **Root cause, reproduced offline BEFORE any change:** `relay-handler.ts` waits a fixed 5000 ms for the carrier's `setup` and then closes the carrier (`SETUP_TIMEOUT`). ConversationRelay sends `setup` at once, but the OpenAI route sends it only AFTER the consent notice has been heard, and Drew's notice takes about 28 seconds to say, so the call logic hung up on a notice still being said, exactly 5 seconds in. The earlier tests missed it because their phone stand-in acknowledged audio at once and their notices were short. A new test with a phone that plays in real time and a notice longer than 5 seconds failed at 5.06 s with the old code and passes now. **Fix:** `RelayDeps.setupTimeoutMs` (default 5000, so ConversationRelay is unchanged; 1000 to 300000 allowed), set by the voice service to the notice's length at 70 ms per character plus 30 seconds (about 59 seconds for Drew's notice); the bridge keeps its own tighter protections (the voice must open within 8 s, audio must be confirmed played within its length plus 3 s, a 10-minute ceiling). **A second defect found while inspecting early-close paths, reproduced offline and fixed (fail closed):** a voice message larger than the bridge's 256 KB limit was dropped silently, yet the notice still counted as played (reproduced: 3 s of audio sent, the call logic told the call had started); a dropped or invalid audio piece now makes its reply count as NOT played (the call ends rather than proceed without consent), and the limits are raised to 8 MiB (bridge and voice socket) because audio pieces are of unknown size. **Durable diagnostics (new):** `src/call-diag.ts` (fixed codes plus numbers, booleans and short plain tokens only; a code must look like CODE_NAME and every other value is dropped, not truncated) with one scope per call (call number and milliseconds since the call began). The bridge, adapter, voice socket, WebSocket server (`onRefuse`: HTTP status only) and voice service emit codes for: stream accepted and the notice wait; phone start; voice open (and its connect time); announcement; notice requested; first audio out; each reply's end and its release (acked, timeout, cleared, immediate); the notice heard or not; every dropped message; error events from the voice service (type, code and field name only); the kinds of event the voice service sent (counts); and the EXACT path that ends a call (`BRIDGE_END` with path = phone_stop, phone_closed, voice_closed, adapter_hangup, adapter_close, open_timeout, max_call, bad_start or voice_refused_audio, the close cause and code, and what the call had done), with the reason a notice failed (status, transcript differs, no transcript, not sent). The voice entry appends every line to `founder-voice.log` (git-ignored by `*.log`) and echoes it, so no console filter is needed; serve mode no longer prints the digests, the TwiML or the address at all (the dry run still does). The handler's own log codes (`CALL_STARTED`, `SETUP_TIMEOUT` with its wait, and the rest) go to the same file. **Tests:** 21 new tests in `tests/call-diagnostics.test.ts` and 2 in the service tests (path by path, privacy sweep with canary text, fail-closed cases, voice socket details, server refusals, the entry's output and file), 1456 tests, 1454 passing, 0 failing, 2 todo. **Not yet proven:** the retest on the real phone; whether the real voice service sends audio pieces larger than 256 KB (the diagnostics will count them); real latency over a line. One full-suite run in two showed a single failure whose name was not captured and 12 paired runs of the changed voice-path files were clean: the same unlocated timing sensitivity noted earlier.
**Remaining before the founder can enter the key and place the call (each separately approved):** (the connection check is done: see the evidence paragraph above); the voice settings and the voice call config are prepared; the disclosure wording needs a legal check before any real use; approve the two digests from the dry run; set `INTAKE_ALLOW_LIVE_CALLS`, `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` and `OPENAI_API_KEY` in the founder's own terminal; stop the old server (it runs old code on the same ports) and start the voice entry with `--serve`; start the tunnel; create a TwiML Bin from the printed stream TwiML (the literal-number form) and point the test number at it, keeping the old Bin as the rollback; place the call. The optional paid Intake check on 20 to 30 invented transcripts is still plan step 6. **Risks accepted on the first live call:** every unverified live-service fact under "Kept open" below, no dollar ceiling, and the open gaps (consent-before-audio, the unknown-number notice, the interrupted read-back, the `$25` exact-match risk, the masker gaps, the unwired Processing handoff, the name not read back). **Still not built:** the public endpoint, the TwiML Bin and the number change (the founder's acts); the key and the endpoint each need the founder's separate approval; 6 a live, paid check of Intake extraction on 20 to 30 invented transcripts; 7 one founder-placed spike call with invented details; 8 Drew scenario acceptance with a rollback rehearsal; 9 update the records. Steps 5 (the real connection) to 9 each need separate founder approval; 5 (the connection), 6, 7 and 8 are live or paid. **Kept open (not decided or not known):** (a) **consent-before-audio, narrowed but open:** the bridge keeps the caller's microphone closed to OpenAI until the notice has been played, but the caller's answer to the consent question (including a no) is still audio OpenAI hears before the server can check it (a test states this); closing it means collecting consent on the Twilio side first, which the call logic does not yet accept; also an unknown number still hears the notice before it is refused (a test records it); (b) the exact OpenAI model, product and telephony route (search results show several names and a note that one product needs SRTP); (c) whether Realtime is eligible for zero data retention (the default abuse-log retention is up to 30 days); (d) cost (list audio prices found were $32 and $64 per million input and output tokens, a third-party estimate is $0.06 to $0.11 a minute, the Twilio media charge is unknown); (e) whether the model reads fixed text and digit strings exactly, and the exact-match check now accepts "$25" as "twenty-five dollars" (offline) but still fails closed on comma-grouped, decimal and spoken-hundreds amounts, and has not been tried against the real voice;
