(f) how barge-in, truncation, refusal and empty responses are reported, and whether played time is available; (g) real latency, since replies speak only after the full text exists; (h) whether the disclosure wording must name a second vendor; (i) every event and field name in the adapter, the bridge and both simulators (the schema alignment above is against documentation summaries and the stand-ins, never the service; still unverified: that G.711 works on the WebSocket in practice, that a request's instructions replace the session's, that the error for a refused cancel or delete names the request in `error.event_id`, the transcription model name, and the current model name; OpenAI also documents a second product, GPT-Live, with different events, which has not been evaluated) (including Media Streams `start`, `media`, `mark` and `clear`, whether a mark's echo means "played", the audio-format values, and the stream parameter that carries the dialed number), which follow documentation read on 2026-10-27 and have not been checked against the real service.
- **Step 1, done (commit `15c0862`, pushed):** the carrier's wire format sits behind a `CallTransport` interface (`src/call-transport.ts`); the Twilio ConversationRelay adapter is the only implementation; behaviour is unchanged, the 139 existing call tests pass unmodified, and 6 new transport tests were added. **Step 2, done offline (mocks only, never run against the real service):** `src/openai-provider.ts` implements the existing `ModelProvider` for Intake extraction (strict structured outputs, `store: false`, no tools or sampling parameters, no default model; a refusal, incomplete, filtered, failed or missing-status answer becomes empty text so the gateway sends the item to a person; errors are reduced to a class name and status); `tests/openai-provider.test.ts` covers valid, invalid and missing fields, failures, timeouts, retries, gates, prompt injection, masking regressions on invented transcripts, a static guard that it has no network, key or SDK access, and that no entry point imports it. It is not wired into any entry point: switching needs the composition root to pass it, a key, a cost ceiling and a check of the request and response shapes against OpenAI's current reference (not confirmed). It imports `answerSchema` from the Claude adapter, to be moved when that adapter is retired.
- **Step 3, done offline (local; the adapter and simulator are unwired code and tests, never run against the real service):** `src/realtime-transport.ts` runs the call logic over a model of OpenAI Realtime. The voice service never replies on its own and is asked to say exact words, out of band; what it said is checked against what was asked, and a reply that fails, makes no sound or says other words is reported to the call logic as not played; each caller transcript item is deleted from the voice service at once; the consent notice is spoken first, cannot be talked over, and nothing said before it has played is taken; no audio is ever sent. `tests/helpers/realtime-sim.ts` stands in for the service and the phone layer, and 35 workflow and adapter tests cover consent, interruptions, silence, failed sends, fixed read-backs, masking boundaries and the existing call limits. The call logic gained `serve(transport)` and one new event, `reply_failed`, which the Twilio carrier never emits; the 145 existing phone and transport tests pass unmodified. **Found while building it (open):** exact-match checking can falsely fail on formatted text such as "$25"; an unknown number hears the notice before it is refused; replies are spoken only after the full text exists; a played-time may not be available from the real phone layer. **Fixed in the same change, the unsent read-back gap:** a read-back whose words could not be sent is no longer confirmed by a yes. The next answer is ignored, the read-back is sent again and counts against the same two read-backs, and the dialogue history no longer claims it was said. New log codes: `READBACK_NOT_SENT` (the words could not be sent), `REPLY_NOT_PLAYED` (the voice service reports a reply was not played as sent) and `READBACK_NOT_HEARD`. **Still open:** a read-back that was sent but talked over part-way can still be confirmed by a yes, because the interruption path does not invalidate it.
- **Masking fixes (2026-10-27; `pii-screen.ts` and `contact-capture.ts`; invented examples only).** (1) Spoken repeats: a number said with `double`, `triple` or `treble` before a digit word ("double zero") is masked whole; before this, the digits after "double zero" were left in the clear. (2) Mixed numbers: spoken digit words, written digits and repeat words in one run (for example "five five five 0 1 0 oh one nine nine") are masked whole, as one contact detail, when the run holds at least one spoken word and stands for 5 or more digits; before this such a number could pass unmasked and unrefused, or be masked in pieces, and a hyphen-joined one was masked as an identifier so the callback number was missed. The rule now runs before the written-phone and identifier rules; digits written alone keep their own rules. `phoneDigits` reads the same numbers (repeat words expand to digits) and drops a spoken "oh" that ends a run and makes a number starting 2 to 9 eleven digits long ("... nine nine oh well"); a written 0, a leading 1 and a leading zero are kept, and the read-back to the caller settles any doubt. Existing behaviour was compared before and after on 202 and 320 invented utterances (pure words, pure numerals, mixed runs, ordinary speech): pure-word runs, pure-numeral runs and ordinary speech were unchanged; the only other difference is that placeholder numbers can swap when one utterance holds both a spoken or mixed number and a written phone number. Not live-tested: the running server still has the old masker.
- **Remaining privacy gaps (2026-10-27).** (a) Single written digits grouped with commas ("5 5 5, 0 1 0, 0 1 9 9") are masked as an identifier, not a contact detail, so the callback number is not captured. (b) Unusual digit words ("niner", "won", the letter O) are not recognised, so a number is masked only up to them. (c) "One eight hundred ..." leaves "one eight hundred" in the clear and the phone missing. (d) Short tails such as an extension ("double 0 4") and a repeat word before an address ("double oh seven elm street" leaves "double") are left in place. (e) A filler "oh" in the middle of a number can still be absorbed. (f) Names not in the approved list, said in lower case with no introduction ("this is jordan lee calling"), are not masked (a todo test). (g) A title-cased transcript is over-masked ("I Need A Regular Cleaning"), which would starve extraction (a todo test). (h) With OpenAI Realtime, raw audio would reach OpenAI unmasked; accepted for invented-detail founder tests only. (i) The masker and the adapter have never run on a real OpenAI transcript or response.
- Snapshot 2026-10-27 (a dated automated-test figure, not maintained): the last full run of `npm test` in `poc/intake-runner` was 1456 tests: **1454 passing, 0 failing, 2 todo** (the two todo tests are the known masker gaps f and g above; they fail by design, are reported, and are not counted as failures). Mock and stand-in tests only.
- **Contact capture** (name, callback number, service address) is implemented with focused privacy tests and is covered by the 1261 above. It is opt-in (`FOUNDER_CONTACT_KEY` at start). Contact values live only in the AES-256-GCM contact vault (30-day retention, restricted command-line lookup); the dashboard cannot reach it.
  - *Name.* Found by a rule-based heuristic (the first one or two names the masker recognises). A lower-case name that nothing introduces is not captured, so the assistant would keep asking. **The name is not read back**; the main read-back yes is not delivery-checked.
  - *Address: caller confirmation, never postal validation.* The assistant reads the captured address back and the caller says yes or corrects it (up to three tries; a refusal or no answer leaves it unconfirmed). A yes means only that the caller agreed with what was heard. **It never means the address exists, is deliverable, or was checked against any address data.** The record's `address_status` is `'none'` (no address held), `'unverified'` (found or held but not confirmed: never read back, refused, unanswered, cut off, or the contact failed to store) or `'caller_confirmed'` (read back and confirmed, and the vault holds it as confirmed). `caller_confirmed` is written only when the confirmed contact was stored for that call and the record itself is confirmed. The text of the address is whatever the speech and masker captured.
  - *The vault witness.* `eligibleForProcessing(record, witness)` passes a `caller_confirmed` record only if the encrypted contact for the same tenant and call answers `'confirmed'` through the vault's `addressState()`, which returns a state and never the address. No witness, a missing or expired contact, the wrong tenant, a tampered row, the wrong key or any error means not eligible. `unverified` never qualifies, whatever a witness says. If the record write fails after the vault write, an orphan contact row remains until its 30-day expiry.
  - *Dashboard wording conflict: fixed locally (uncommitted), under the founder's written authorization.* `prototype/intake-work-items/app.js` now says "An address was given and the caller confirmed it on the phone. It is not shown here. It was not checked against any address data." for a `caller_confirmed` call, and "An address was given but it is not confirmed. It is not shown here. A person needs to check it with the caller." for every other call with an address. It tells them apart by the owner view's own `Address` detail, so the owner view, the `/api/owner` shape, the routes, the layout and the stored records are unchanged and no address value is shown. **Duplicate address line (left as is):** a confirmed call's "Other details" list still shows the owner view's own line "Address: Confirmed by the caller on the phone. Not checked against any address data." as well as the new panel, so the same fact appears twice; the two agree.
  - *Rollback risk.* The record file format (schema version 2) is unchanged, but the previous commit's code refuses a records database that holds any `caller_confirmed` record, so the owner page and API would fail for that tenant. Before activating the new code, back up `founder-call.sqlite`; do not roll back without first exporting and removing any `caller_confirmed` rows, or restoring the backup. (Verified by running the old code against a copy.)
  - *Dashboard and contact.* A confirmed call whose contact failed to store is shown as "did not confirm the details", which is safe but imprecise.
- **Also implemented in code and tests (none of it exercised on a live call):**
  - **The opening** (section 2a): configurable business and agent introduction, disclosure and an optional approved special.
  - **The services cleaning skill** (`poc/intake-runner/examples/skill-cleaning-services.json`): Standard, Deep and Move-in or move-out cleaning, each asking only its own missing details (property type, bedrooms, bathrooms, pets, extras), with business-configured `choices` offered when clarification helps, a first-cleaning question, and a commercial route that sends the request to a person (`human_review`) instead of continuing. Date and time window stay separate fields. A move-out property is never assumed empty or pet-free.
  - **First-cleaning answers** count only when they answer the active question: a bare yes or no needs a cleanly delivered question, explicit wording counts anywhere, the newest answer wins, and unattributed values are dropped. Delivery cannot be proved (Twilio sends no playback acknowledgement; a successful send is not the same as played).
  - **SIMULATION pricing fixture** (`examples/pricing-fixture-drew-cleaning.json`, `src/pricing-fixture.ts`): cents-based, labelled SIMULATION, human-pricing reason codes; not connected to any call. Pricing and booking remain simulation-only until the business approves live rules. The approved Drew test greeting is "$25 off a qualifying first standard cleaning"; the fixture's one rule is Standard cleaning for a house or apartment up to 2 bedrooms and 2 bathrooms, $199.99 less a $25.00 first-clean discount, priced only after the caller confirms the details and that it is their first cleaning. The greeting does not state those terms and they are not otherwise configured. **The fixture is not connected to the phone and is not approved for quotes to real customers.**
  - **Handoff preservation**: `call-intake.ts` returns the original sealed handoff and the phone handler keeps it in memory (never logged, stored or sent), offering it to an optional `confirmedHandoff` seam after a confirmed, saved, handed-off record.
  - **Diagnostics** (numbers and fixed words only): `REPLY_SENT`, `REPLY_EMPTY`, `REPLY_INTERRUPTED`, `REPLY_CUT`, `REPLY_SEND_FAILED`, `REPLY_RETRY`, `REPLY_FALLBACK_SENT`, `REPLY_FALLBACK_FAILED`, `REPLY_STALLED`, `REPLY_REFUSED` and `CALL_CLOSED` (who closed, close code, time since the last words and reply, counts of refused writes and ignored frames). They show whether words were produced and written to the socket; they cannot show that the phone provider spoke them, what the caller said, or why a peer closed.
- **Unwired: the Processing handoff.** The phone path still ends at a saved record. The preserved handoff is offered only to an optional seam that `founder-call-entry.ts` does not pass; `pipeline.ts` has no entry for an existing handoff; `eligibleForProcessing` (and its vault witness) has no consumer in `src`; and the seal is in-process only. No quote, calendar, booking or Processing run is connected to a real call.
- **Open: the silent response (mitigated locally, not validated).** The first Drew call showed two empty model replies, zero refused writes and a normal peer close (see the evidence and the silence fix above). The fix is committed (`4b8932b`) but not loaded by the running server, and the cause of the empty output is not established. Two earlier calls (before the diagnostics existed) and a later call closed from the peer side before consent remain unexplained. It stays open until a call on a restarted build with the fix, and the new numbers, are read. See `docs/research/CLEANING_INTAKE_RESEARCH.md` section 0.
- **Not verified:** the read-back (including the address read-back), hang-up and owner screens on a real call; speech recognition of digits, names and addresses in the field; the Twilio caller-ID attestation signal (not read anywhere in the code).

## 5a. Next goal: one complete simulated cleaning quote-to-booking test

**Proposed, not yet approved as a task.** The next milestone is a single end-to-end run in SIMULATION (section 3a) of the chain in section 2: verified contact → service → simulated quote → approval → availability → booking → QC → the existing dashboard, using mock approved-rate fixtures and a mock calendar and invented personas only. It is built as a sequence of separately approved tasks (section 1b), in the order of section 3, and every dashboard addition follows section 1a. It does not make cleaning LIVE and does not quote or book anyone real. Two earlier code blockers for that test are fixed in code and tests (local, not seen live): a captured address no longer leaves `address_status` as `none`, and an address the caller confirms on the phone is recorded as `caller_confirmed` (confirmation by the caller, never postal validation) with the vault witness required by the eligibility gate. Business gaps are separate and need the founder: the live cleaning skill still has one category; a services skill (standard, deep, move-in/move-out, with choices, a first-cleaning question and a commercial human-review route) and a SIMULATION pricing fixture exist as examples and have not been applied or approved for live use, so a quote cannot yet tell the cleans apart on a real call. Prerequisites still open: diagnosing the silent response (section 5), the Intake-to-Processing bridge (section 3), the founder's cleaning categories and fixture rates (section 6), and the decision to commit the current work (section 6, item 7).

## 5a-1. Drew residential call-to-booking specification (DRAFT for founder approval, 2026-10-27)

The visual specification is saved at [`docs/DREW_CALL_TO_BOOKING_SPEC.html`](DREW_CALL_TO_BOOKING_SPEC.html) (open it in a browser). It covers the draft greeting (no "test line" or "made-up details" wording; Drew's introduction, the existing $25 special, the AI/OpenAI privacy disclosure and the consent step kept), the exact questions in order, remembered answers, skip and retry rules, the read-backs, the SIMULATION pricing that exists (a test fixture, no approved rates), calendar rules, customer approval and verified booking, a proposed native Realtime design with controlled server tools, the Intake, Processing, QC, Reporting link on the preserved dashboard, and a built, missing and undecided summary with a proposed build order. **Founder decisions preserved in it (2026-10-27): (1) native Realtime conversation with controlled server tools is the direction (the tool design is still open); (2) Drew is residential-only (the current configuration still has a Commercial category that routes to a person; it is to be reconciled in a later approved task); (3) the $25 first-standard-clean discount is already defined (the greeting special and the simulation fixture discount are that one offer; the fixture base amount and size limits are test data, not approved rates).** It also holds a read-only review of how answers enter current-call state (its section 5A): the contact items (name, phone, address) count only when a masking rule recognises them and are not bound to the question asked, so they can stay missing and be re-asked without limit (call 1: 14 repeated asks, no read-back reached; saved record: no address ever recognised); a name can be a pet or place; a number split across turns never completes; talked-over questions are counted as asked; a masker refusal or a thrown text-model error ends the call (call 2 apology). Current-call state (cleared at hang-up) is distinct from future long-term customer memory (missing, undecided) and from the still-unwired Intake-to-Processing hand-off (its section 5B). The rest of the document is a draft: no business rule in it is invented (undecided items are marked), and approving it starts no build (section 1b). Documentation only: no code, live configuration, Twilio setting, key use, restart or call.

## 5a-2. Current-call state and contact capture checkpoint (2026-10-27; code and tests; NOT live, NOT the native Realtime conversation)

**This is a checkpoint toward native Realtime conversation, not its completion.** Founder approvals recorded for this checkpoint: the caller hears their own name in the read-back (caller only; never in a text-model history or a log), and the voice is told captured and missing field names (labels only, no values).

**Built (local code and tests):** `src/call-state.ts` (server-validated contact state, kept apart from privacy masking): the caller's next line is taken as the answer to the contact question just asked (name, callback number, service address) and validated by the server whether or not a masking rule would recognise it; a lower-case or single name, an address with no street suffix and a number said in pieces are captured; a later explicit name, number or address replaces the earlier one (correction); the masker `learn()`s what was captured so it is masked in that line and later ones; a masker-refused contact answer no longer ends the call (a placeholder is all a model reads). Five contact tools (`collect_name`, `collect_phone`, `collect_address`, `correct_contact`, `call_status`) with a dispatcher that refuses unknown tools (no pricing, booking or hand-off tool exists) and returns labels only. In the call handler: a question counts as asked only when it was heard; an item is asked once plus two clarifications (`MAX_CLARIFICATIONS = 2`), then the call ends with a fixed line as a safe incomplete outcome (saved unconfirmed, reason `incomplete_call`, never handed off); a dialogue-model error is retried once, then the server speaks its fixed words, and only three failed turns in a row end the call (error name and status number logged); the dialogue and voice instructions carry a `STATUS captured / still missing` line of field names before the NEXT line; the read-back says "Your name: ..." to the caller. **Tests:** `tests/call-state.test.ts` (new) and new regression tests in `tests/founder-call.test.ts` for the reported failures (repeated address, name correction, split phone, interrupted questions via the existing delivery tests, transient model errors, the bound, no handoff or price/booking words); suite 1472 tests, 1470 passing, 0 failing, 2 todo (the two known masker gaps). Several older tests were rewritten because their behaviour changed on purpose (unlimited re-asking, immediate apology on one model error, no name in the read-back, "asked before" wording for an ask that was never heard).

**Still open, not done in this checkpoint:** (1) **native Realtime tool calling on the live transport**: the voice still only speaks the exact words the server gives it; enabling `tools` and `response.function_call_arguments` handling means changing the rule that the voice never replies on its own, which needs its own approved task; the tool layer above is ready for it. (2) **Residential-only Drew**: the configured Commercial category still routes to a person; replacing it with a fixed "residential only" response needs a configuration change and wording decision. (3) S7: one masker refusal of a non-contact line still ends the call. (4) The name read-back is a changed privacy contract for the caller-facing read-back only; real-customer use still needs the data-terms and legal decisions in section 6. (5) Pricing, calendar, booking, customer approval of quotes, the Intake to Processing hand-off wiring and dashboard additions are unchanged and unbuilt. Not run live: no call, key, restart or Twilio change was made.

## 5a-3. Native OpenAI Realtime conversation for residential Intake (2026-10-27; code and tests against an offline model of the service; NOT live, NOT tried with OpenAI)

**What it is.** An opt-in mode (voice config `conversation: "native"`; the default stays `scripted`, and the private voice config is unchanged, so nothing live changes). In native mode the voice converses in its OWN words for the directive the call logic gives it, instead of reading a reply written by the text model. The server still decides WHEN the voice speaks (automatic replies stay off), WHAT it is to do (the same `NEXT` and `STATUS` lines, in a brief built by the server) and what every tool call means. Fixed lines (the consent notice, read-backs, the address question, goodbyes, the incomplete and apology lines) are still spoken word for word with every tool switched off.

**Built.** `realtime-transport.ts` `native` option (session `tools`, `tool_choice`, a per-reply brief, tool-call events, `function_call_output` answers, up to two tool rounds, a total time bound, interruption with the words spoken so far, queued native replies answered as failed when dropped); `CallTransport.converse` and `toolResult` and a `tool` event (`call-transport.ts`); `relay-handler.ts` uses `converse` when the carrier has it (the same retry-once, fixed-words fallback, bounded clarification, heard-only ask counting and limits as before), validates EVERY tool call through the session (`call-state.ts`: unknown tool, bad arguments, before consent or after the end, or too many calls are refused with a fixed code; five refusals end the call with the fixed apology), answers the voice with labels and an outcome word only (never a value), and keeps only a fixed marker of the voice's own words; the masker learns what a tool captured. The tools are exactly `collect_name`, `collect_phone`, `collect_address`, `correct_contact`, `call_status`: **no pricing, booking, approval, calendar or Processing tool exists**, and the hand-off still happens only through the existing confirmed-and-ready seam. **Tests:** `tests/native-conversation.test.ts` (15: the brief and tools, scripted mode unchanged, consent decline, remembered answers, split numbers, correction, interruption, the clarification bound and incomplete outcome, invalid tools including pricing/booking/hand-off names, five refusals, a silent voice, a slow voice, privacy across requests/logs/files, limits, stray tool calls), gate tests for the setting, and a service test; the offline Realtime stand-in and its schema rules now model tools and `function_call_output`. Suite 1489 tests, 1487 passing, 0 failing, 2 todo (one run in three showed a single timing failure in an existing voice-service test that passes alone: the unlocated timing sensitivity already noted).

**Contract changes (stated, each needs the founder's acceptance before any live native call).** (1) In native mode the voice service's conversation KEEPS the caller's turns for the call (the scripted mode deletes each transcript item at once); the voice cannot converse without them, and OpenAI already hears the audio (the disclosure says so). The text models and Intake still read only masked lines; the raw name, number and address reach no log, record, Intake request, brief or tool answer (tested). (2) The voice itself, not the text model, words every non-fixed question, so the persona, the "no price/no promise" rules and the NEXT line are enforced by instruction, not by a server check of the words. (3) Voice config gains an optional `conversation` key, which changes the voice digest only when set.

**Residential-only Drew was implemented next (section 5a-4).** **Remaining live-test blockers.** Nothing here has talked to the real service: the tool event and field names (`response.function_call_arguments.done`, `conversation.item.create` with `function_call_output`, `tool_choice`) follow documentation read on 2026-10-27 and are unverified; whether the real model calls the tools reliably, answers in its own words within the NEXT line, stays silent when it should, and how tool rounds add latency are unmeasured. To try it: the founder sets `conversation: "native"` in the private voice config (a new digest, a restart and a single approved test call, each separately). Also still open: consent-before-audio; the interrupted read-back; **residential-only Drew** (the Commercial category still routes to a person; a fixed "residential only" response needs the founder's wording); S7 (a masker refusal of a non-contact line ends the call); native replies are not checked word for word (a spoken price would not be caught by code, only by the instructions); the specification's pricing, calendar, booking, approval, hand-off wiring and dashboard additions remain unbuilt.

## 5a-4. Residential-only Drew routing (2026-10-27; code and tests; NOT live; the private configuration is unchanged)

**Founder decision implemented:** Drew Cleaning does not offer commercial cleaning. **Built:** `RelayDeps.residential` (relay-handler.ts) and `residentialRouting` (founder-voice-service.ts), which turn the skill's person-routed (`route: "human"`) category into a *declined* category: it is never named to a caller (the clarifying question lists only the residential services); when Intake classifies a request into it, the caller is asked the fixed question, in the founder's exact words, "Drew Cleaning cleans houses, apartments, condos and townhouses. We don’t offer commercial cleaning. Is your request for a residential property?", and nothing else is asked, read back, priced, booked or handed on until they answer. A residential answer carries on (the words that made it look commercial, such as "office" in "home office", are neutralised for Intake from then on); a commercial answer ends the call with a fixed line that promises nothing ("Thank you. Drew Cleaning only cleans residential properties, so I can't help with this request. Goodbye."); an answer that stays unclear after one more try ends the same way; a hang-up before any answer is saved the same way. A declined request is saved UNCONFIRMED (needs-human, not handed off, reasons `not_confirmed` and `commercial_declined`), is never read back, no contact is stored, and a request in that category can never be saved as confirmed (defence in depth). The answer is read by fixed rules (`residentialAnswer`: plain yes/no, residential or commercial words, and contradictions such as "no, it's my house" or "yes, it's my office" are unclear). **Tests:** residential, commercial and ambiguous requests (5 handler tests plus a rule table and the service wiring); suite 1496 tests, 1494 passing, 0 failing, 2 todo. Native mode was not activated and works with this unchanged (the question is a fixed line).

**Still open:** the private Drew call config still contains the Commercial category (it is what the routing keys on, so it must stay for this to work); the old Claude/ConversationRelay route (`founder-call-entry.ts`) does not use the routing (rollback route only); Intake extraction failing on the very turn a commercial word appears delays the question to the next turn (the call only listens meanwhile and a commercial request is never read back); **the decline wording was APPROVED by the founder (2026-10-27): "Thank you. Drew Cleaning only cleans residential properties, so I can't help with this request. Goodbye."** (it promises no follow-up); whether a declined caller should be offered anything else (for example another business) is undecided; the specification's pricing, calendar, booking, approval, Processing hand-off and dashboard additions remain unbuilt.

## 5a-5. GPT-Live (full-duplex) voice connection: comparison with the current connection and the smallest next step (2026-10-27; DOCUMENTATION ONLY: nothing built, no key used, nothing live changed)

**Founder decision recorded (2026-10-27):** the residential decline wording is approved as written (section 5a-4).

The full comparison, with sources, is in section 14 of [`docs/DREW_CALL_TO_BOOKING_SPEC.html`](DREW_CALL_TO_BOOKING_SPEC.html). **Summary.** OpenAI's GPT-Live (`gpt-live-1`) is a different API from the Realtime API the founder test uses now: a different endpoint (`wss://api.openai.com/v1/live/sessions`), a strict `session.start` message, different event names, **no turn detection and no per-reply response events** (the voice listens and speaks at the same time), and tools only through "delegation" to a backend model. Twilio's official route is Twilio Agent Connect's `GPTLiveProvider` (Python and TypeScript packages) or a Media Streams tutorial that uses the same `<Connect><Stream>` we use; OpenAI says a Realtime integration is not automatically compatible with GPT-Live. **Reusable unchanged:** the Twilio side (TwiML, secret path, signature, Media Streams audio in mu-law 8 kHz), the bridge's audio and playback tracking, the whole call logic above the transport (consent handling, masker, Intake, current-call state and validators, contact tools, vault, residential routing, limits, records, dashboard, the four-agent boundaries), the persona and rules text, the OpenAI text model as the delegation model. **Must change:** the wire dialect (endpoint, handshake, event names), the turn model (the server can no longer trigger each reply or see each turn end; it must infer turn ends and reply ends), verbatim fixed lines (GPT-Live does not promise exact words), where consent is collected, and how tools are declared and answered. **Smallest next step (offline, no key):** a GPT-Live *dialect adapter* at the existing link seam plus an offline GPT-Live stand-in and tests, behind a new voice-config `engine` setting that defaults to the current engine; it changes none of the call logic. It needs three founder decisions first (verbatim fixed lines and consent, who controls turns, access and cost). Replacing our stack with Agent Connect is **not** recommended: it would take over turn-taking and audio and bypass the consent, masking and vault control points, and needs installs.

## 5a-6. Twilio GPT-Live source review and the proposed consent and read-back design (2026-10-27; DOCUMENTATION ONLY: nothing built, no key used, nothing live changed)

Full detail, with sources, is in section 15 of [`docs/DREW_CALL_TO_BOOKING_SPEC.html`](DREW_CALL_TO_BOOKING_SPEC.html). **Founder decisions preserved:** GPT-Live keeps conversational control (full duplex; the old server-driven speaking loop is not recreated), and Drew is residential-only (the approved question and decline line, section 5a-4).

**Verified (read in vendor source or pages on 2026-10-27; never run; read through a summarising fetch tool).** Twilio Agent Connect's TypeScript GPT-Live provider has *no* consent or disclosure (its TwiML builder emits only `<Connect><Stream>`; the only opening is a paraphrased `welcomeInstruction`), *no* `mark`/`clear` or playback tracking (it relies on GPT-Live handling interruption), and a tool path that only looks a tool up by name (unknown names refused, no schema check, no approve or deny hook, no call state passed). Close is `session.close` then up to 5 s for `session.closed`; hang-up is closing the WebSocket. HTTP routes validate the Twilio signature; its WebSocket has no secret path, rate or size limits or replay protection. So it neither implements nor bypasses our safeguards: it lacks them, and is a protocol reference only (it also needs installs). Twilio docs: later TwiML after `<Connect><Stream>` resumes only when the server closes the WebSocket; `<Gather input="speech">` posts `SpeechResult` to `action` and its nested `<Say>` can be interrupted.

**Unread.** The Python repository; the base-class methods the summaries said were elsewhere (message handlers, call cleanup, the default WebSocket URL); OpenAI's Connections, audio-format and Telephony guides; pricing and limits; any real behavior of the service.

**Proposal (not built).** (1) *Consent before audio:* Twilio says the exact notice (`<Say>` outside a `<Gather input="speech">`, so it cannot be talked over), a signed `/consent` webhook applies our yes-and-not-no rule and records the CallSid in a consent ledger, and only then returns `<Connect><Stream>`; the stream setup refuses a CallSid with no ledger entry. This is independent of GPT-Live and closes the consent-before-audio gap. (2) *Read-back:* GPT-Live converses and reports through the validated contact tools; a gated `begin_confirmation` tool succeeds only when consent, Intake, contacts and the residential question are all settled; the server then closes the stream and Twilio's resumed TwiML says the exact read-back and gathers yes or no (address next, then thanks and hang-up); a no reopens GPT-Live with the masked history seeded. Costs: Twilio's voice for those lines, raw values in TwiML (possibly in Twilio's logs), a resume case for a repeated CallSid. (3) *Build order, each step separately approved:* the Twilio consent webhook and ledger first; then the GPT-Live dialect adapter with an offline stand-in behind a voice-config `engine` setting; then a live-call orchestrator (extracting record saving out of `finish()`); then the Twilio confirmation leg.

**Unresolved assumptions.** That Twilio resumes TwiML (or calls `<Connect action>`) when we close a Stream (the Stream page says later TwiML resumes; the Connect page does not say `action` fires for a Stream); that delegated tools receive the caller's exact words; that speaker changes separate turns when speech overlaps; that `session.start` `input` seeding is enough context after a restart; key access to `gpt-live-1`, latency and pricing; the disclosure wording for GPT-Live's storage settings.

## 5a-7. First live memory test on the OpenAI route: findings, privacy-safe contact diagnostics and phone-capture fixes (2026-10-28; code and tests; NOT retested live)

**What the live call showed (recorded evidence, call of 2026-10-28 17:14:59 to 17:19:02 UTC, server PID 9960, commit `45622cf`, scripted mode).** The call ended as the safe incomplete outcome: `INCOMPLETE {clarifications:2, lines:21}`, saved unconfirmed (`needs-human`, reasons `not_confirmed` and `incomplete_call`), nothing handed off. About nine questions went on the cleaning details, then eleven asks and nine answers went on contact capture (two captured, three partial, four not captured), and the call ran out of asks. The log held counts and codes only, so it could not say which item failed. **That is the diagnostic gap this step closes.**

**Dashboard reading of that record (read-only; nothing changed).** (1) *Morning is absent from the owner's call card:* the record holds "Morning."; the owner view puts the FIRST date-or-time field in "Requested time" (the preferred date, "October 28th.") and lists the rest as details, and the list is capped at eight, so "Time window", "Extras" and "First cleaning with us" were cut off. This is a display limit in the owner view, not a capture failure. It is a **separate fix that needs the founder's written authorisation** (the prototype dashboard and its allow-list are protected). (2) *"Last professional clean" is withheld:* the saved value was "[CONTACT_1] months ago." (a mask token), which the owner view renders as "(withheld)". Offline, none of ten ordinary phrasings of a duration ("eighteen months ago", "18 months ago", "about six months ago" and others) is masked, so **the cause is NOT reproduced**; it needs the new diagnostics on the next call. (3) *A standard cleaning shows deep-clean and move-out details (last professional clean, priority areas, home empty):* these three fields are required only by the Deep cleaning and Move-in or move-out categories, so they were asked while Intake had classified the request as one of those, and the final category was Standard cleaning. Inference only: the category flipped during the call (the log does not record the category). Their short bare values ("Yes.", "The two, the bedrooms.") look like answers to asked questions rather than volunteered detail, but the evidence cannot prove it. Separate fix, not made: category stability and which fields a final category keeps.

**Diagnostics added (never a value, a word of the caller's or a transcript; every value is a fixed code or a count, and the existing log policy test still passes).** `CONTACT_ANSWER` now carries `field` (NAME, PHONE or ADDRESS), `outcome`, a fixed `reason` (CAPTURED, PIECE, RESTART, CORRECTED_LAST4, NO_DIGITS, TOO_MANY, NOT_A_NAME, NO_STREET_NUMBER, NOT_AN_ADDRESS, INACTIVE or NOT_HEARD), for the phone `digits` (this line) and `held` (after it), and `n` (asks so far). `ASK` carries `item` (NAME, PHONE, ADDRESS or CONTENT) and `n`. `INCOMPLETE` carries `item`, flags for which contact items were held (`name`, `phone`, `address` as 0 or 1) and `phoneDigits` (digits held of a number heard in pieces).

**Phone-capture defects reproduced offline and fixed (only these).** (a) *Repeated prefix:* "two one two five five five" then "five five five zero one four two" joined to thirteen digits and was taken as the number; pieces that do not make a ten digit number (or eleven with a leading 1) are now dropped and the new piece starts over (reason RESTART), so the caller is asked again instead of being read a wrong number. A whole line of ten to fifteen digits is still taken as said. (b) *Stray digit word:* a spoken "oh" between pieces made an eleven digit number; it now restarts the same way. (c) *Twelve joined digits* were accepted; now they restart. (d) *"The last four are ..." correction* was lost silently when a number was already held; it now replaces the last four digits (reason CORRECTED_LAST4) and is masked. Name and address capture, corrections of a whole number or name, and call isolation were checked and needed no change.

**Tests.** Focused: 222 pass (call-state, native, founder-call, diagnostics, voice service). Full suite once: 1502 tests, 1500 pass, 0 fail, 2 known masker todo. New tests cover split numbers, repeated prefixes, stray digit words, the last-four correction, the fixed reasons and the log contents.

**Still open (separate fixes, none approved):** the owner view's eight-detail cap hiding the time window; category stability (deep-clean and move-out questions on a standard cleaning); the withheld duration value (cause unknown); the "anything else?" wording (the text model's wording of the Extras question; no fixed line exists); which greeting is approved (the live opening is the earlier test-line wording, not the spec's draft). **Next single retest:** one live call on the same route, scripted mode, with invented details, giving the phone number in pieces once and repeating a prefix once; the new log lines will then say which item failed and why.

## 5a-8. Service address collected in parts (2026-10-28; code and tests; NOT retested live; phone, name and category behaviour unchanged)

**Why.** The two calls after the diagnostics step (server PID 35708, commit `469b619`) both ended with the address missing: the caller said a house number ("742") with no street name, the capture rule refused it (`NOT_AN_ADDRESS`, twice), and the question was repeated until the ask limit ended the call as the safe incomplete outcome. The dashboard shows the request saved unconfirmed with its cleaning details; it does not establish why address capture failed, and this step does not claim to. The recorded diagnostics did show the reason code, and the rule was reproduced offline.

**What changed (address collection only).** A house number heard on its own (digits or number words: "742", "seven four two", "forty two") or a street name heard on its own ("Evergreen Terrace", "Evergreen"; no particular suffix is required) is now kept for the rest of the call, never treated as a complete address, and joined to the other part when it arrives, in either order. A different number replaces the held one; a correction, a refusal path or "that is wrong" drops any held part. The ask for the address names only the missing part in one fixed line (`NEXT ASK Service address (the house number is already heard: ask only for the street name)`), and in the address-correction step the fixed lines "Thank you. What is the street name?" or "Thank you. What is the house number?" are used (at most three times, then the existing limits apply). Diagnostics gained the fixed reasons `NEED_STREET`, `NEED_NUMBER` and `JOINED`. **The caller still confirms the whole joined address aloud** (the existing read-back and yes or no step, unchanged); nothing is looked up, invented or called validated.

**Number-only addresses are rejected.** "742", "seven four two", "forty two", "apartment 4" and "4 B" are not addresses. A defect found on the way and fixed with it: "one moment" (a number word plus any word) was accepted as an address; a street word may no longer be a word of conversation or a unit designator. Still accepted: "742 Evergreen", "forty two Oak Lane", "Maple Grove Apartments unit 4", "PO Box 12".

**Privacy, limits and the dashboard preserved.** A held part is taught to the masker under the contact-detail type (so it is masked and never read by a model or logged) and the joined address under the address type; a part never counts as the address through the masker's fallback. The vault, the clarification limits (one ask plus two clarifications; a part heard is progress, credited at most three times per call, the same rule as a phone piece), the incomplete outcome and the dashboard's allow-list are unchanged; no log, model request, record or dashboard text holds any part of an address (tested).

**Tests.** 183 focused tests pass (call-state and founder-call), covering spoken and written numbers, either order, separate components, corrections, a refusal in the correction step, noise, an interrupted question, an incomplete address (ends bounded, unconfirmed, never read back, nothing stored), the native tools, the directive wording and call isolation. Full suite once: 1516 tests, 1514 pass, 0 fail, 2 known masker todo.

**Not changed and still open (separate, none approved):** the first-round name rejections (a run-on line such as a name followed by digits, and "Mr." with its full stop); category stability (deep-clean and move-out questions on a standard cleaning); the owner view's eight-detail cap that hides the time window; logging the content field and category on every ask; the withheld "Last professional clean" value; the "anything else?" wording; which greeting is approved. **Next retest:** one live call, scripted mode, invented details, giving only the house number first and then the street name when asked.

## 5a-9. One validated cleaning-answer state, field-labelled answers and field/category diagnostics (2026-10-28; code and tests; NOT retested live; contact capture, category rules and the dashboard unchanged)

**Why.** In the latest live call (server PID 46868) the call ended `INCOMPLETE` on a cleaning-detail question (asked three times; the log did not say which) while the name and address were held, and the saved record, built by a separate final Intake run over the same 23 lines, listed nothing missing and had every Standard field filled. Each turn re-read the whole conversation from nothing and the record was a different reading from the one the call had steered by. This is the inference from that record, not a proven cause: the offline reproduction below shows the mechanism and fixes it; the next live call will say whether it was the cause.

**What changed.** New `src/cleaning-state.ts` (pure): one cleaning-answer state per call, shared by the question choice and the saved record.
- **Field-labelled answers.** When a cleaning question was cleanly delivered, the caller's next line is bound to that field if it plausibly answers it (a count needs a number and not another room's count; a frequency, a date and a time of day each need their own words; a yes or no needs a yes or no or a description) and is sent to Intake on a `Field: answer` line, as the first-cleaning answer always was. A vague reply ("maybe", "hmm", "I don't know"), a refusal, or an answer about another field ("just this one time" to an extras question) is **ambiguous**: nothing is stored and the question is asked again within the usual limits. Free-text fields have no checkable shape and are not bound; Intake reads them exactly as before.
- **Accepted answers persist.** What an Intake pass finds (and is plausible) is kept for the call. A later pass that omits it does not un-complete it: the field is not asked again, and the read-back and the saved record put it back (then held for a person, never handed off, because the pass itself did not produce it).
- **Corrections and contradictions.** A different value read from the newest caller line replaces the accepted one only with correction wording ("actually", "sorry", "I meant", "make that" ...). Without it, a decisive difference (a count, yes or no, frequency, date or time) is a **conflict**: the stale value is taken out of the record and out of what Intake reads, the field is listed first among the missing, and the caller is asked again; their answer replaces it. A different reading of older lines is ignored. Nothing marks a required field as found without the caller's answer having been found by Intake or given to the question that was asked.
- **The record agrees with the ending.** The incomplete ending saves the very pass whose missing list ended the call (log `FINAL_FROM_LAST_PASS`), so the field that ran out of asks is in the record's `missing_fields` with `incomplete_call`.
- **Category changes keep answers.** Accepted answers are keyed by field, not category: after a change, only what the new category still requires and has not been answered is asked. (Category choice is unchanged: Intake's keyword and model rules.)

**Diagnostics (fixed codes only; never a caller's word).** `ASK` carries `field` and `cat`; `INCOMPLETE` carries `field`, `cat`, `miss` and `m1` to `m3` (the first missing labels); new lines `FIELD_ANSWER` (BOUND, AMBIGUOUS or UNCHECKED), `FIELD_CORRECTED`, `FIELD_CONFLICT`, `FIELDS_RESTORED`, `CATEGORY` (from and to) and `FINAL_FROM_LAST_PASS`. Codes are capitals and underscores built from configured field and category names.

**Privacy and contracts preserved.** Stored values are the caller's masked words (the lines Intake already reads), one line, at most 80 characters, with no label-looking colon so a stored answer cannot forge another field's label; the state is disposed with the call. Required-field checks, the first-cleaning guard, the clarification limits (one ask plus two clarifications), contact capture, the vault, the owner-view allow-list and the dashboard are unchanged.

**Tests.** `tests/cleaning-state.test.ts` (9, pure) and 7 handler-level tests through the real scripted path with a fake extraction model that can omit fields, force readings and force categories: volunteered answers skipped and labelled lines sent; vague and off-topic replies; extraction omitting an earlier answer; an explicit correction; a genuine contradiction answered and left unanswered; a category change; the incomplete ending's labels against the saved record. Full suite once: 1532 tests, 1530 pass, 0 fail, 2 known masker todo.

**Must pass before one live retest** (all must be true in the log and record of that call): a volunteered detail is never asked; the log shows each ask's field and category; the call reaches `READBACK`, then `ADDRESS_CONFIRMED`, with no `INCOMPLETE`; the saved record is confirmed with `missing_fields` empty and `address_status` caller_confirmed, and its fields match what was read back; the vault holds name, number and address. If the call ends incomplete again the log now names the field, the category and the missing labels, and the record lists the same field. Still open and separate: the first-round name rejections; the owner view's eight-detail cap hiding the time window; GPT-Live (paused).

## 5a-10. Confirmation wait after verified playback, phone fragments that are not digits, mismatched cleaning answers (2026-10-28; code and tests; NOT retested live)

**Why (the 3:02 PM call, record #13, server PID 28360).** The read-back was 44 seconds of speech; the confirmation timer was a fixed 20 seconds from the moment it was SENT, so the call was hung up as unconfirmed with about 35 seconds of it still unplayed, the caller could not answer, and the address confirmation never came. Separately, the phone question received what is almost certainly a date ("October 22": exactly two digits, then 4 and 4, captured as a ten digit number), the digits "22" were taught to the masker as a contact detail, and the date was saved as "October [CONTACT_1]" (shown "(withheld)"): the withholding happened in masking, was stored as a token, and the dashboard only rendered it.

**Fix 1: the silence wait starts when the read-back has finished playing.** The voice route's transport gains `playbackDone(maxMs)`, resolving `played`, `cut` (the caller talked over it, it failed, or the call ended) or `timeout`. The voice service only releases a reply's completion after the phone provider has acknowledged the audio, so "nothing current and nothing queued" means verified playback. The call logic arms its `confirmWaitMs` (20 s in production) only then. The wait for playback is bounded twice (the transport and the handler itself, `playbackMaxMs`, default 90 s); on a bound it logs `PLAYBACK_SETTLED TIMEOUT` and the silence wait runs, so a carrier that never reports cannot hold a call open. A carrier that cannot tell (ConversationRelay) is given the estimated speech time on top of the wait. A caller who speaks clears the wait as before. Log: `PLAYBACK_SETTLED {outcome: PLAYED | CUT | TIMEOUT}`.

**Fix 2: dates, times and durations are not phone digits.** A line with a month, weekday, part of the day, duration unit, tens word ("twenty"), ordinal ("22nd"), clock time or am/pm is refused as a phone piece (reason `NOT_DIGITS`): nothing is taken, the held pieces stay, the question is asked again within the usual limits, and the line stays an ordinary line for Intake, so "October 22" is no longer masked. **Privacy preserved, not weakened:** an ACCEPTED phone piece is still taught to the masker in full (still masked in that line and later); a REFUSED line's runs of five or more digits are still taught (so a long number hidden in a date sentence stays protected); only short runs (a day, an hour) in refused lines are left alone. Known remaining effect: if a real phone piece happens to equal a date's digits, a later mention of that date is masked (over-masking, never under-masking).

**Fix 3: clearly mismatched cleaning answers are rejected.** A date, a time of day or a frequency is not an answer to a yes/no question (pets, extras, home empty); a date or time is not a count (and counts above 20 are not room counts); a frequency or time is not a date, and so on. Such a reply is not stored (and not fed to Intake as a labelled answer) and the question is asked again; the same checks apply to what an Intake pass extracts.

**Investigated (both items were then resolved on the same day: see section 5a-11).** (a) *Unsupported optional fields:* the log shows no ask for "Last professional clean" or "Home empty" in the 3:02 PM call, yet the record holds "A month" and "Yes". Intake fills every skill field it can from any wording, while the category's required list only drives what is asked; the read-back then reads every found field, which made it long and carried a misreading. The labelled-answer lines are not the origin (earlier calls before them had the same). No value is invented or removed here. Options for the founder: read back and save only required, asked or volunteered fields, or leave it and accept misreadings. (b) *First-cleaning reversal:* the record says "No" and a bare yes or no counts as the first-cleaning answer whenever that question was the one asked; but the QUESTION is worded by the text model with no fixed words, so a differently phrased question ("have you used us before?") would reverse what a plain no means. Not provable from the log (the wording is not logged). Smallest fix proposed: one fixed, founder-approved first-cleaning question spoken as a fixed line. Required-field rules are unchanged.

**Tests.** `tests/playback-confirmation.test.ts` (4, through the voice route and the realtime simulator: a read-back longer than the wait, silence after it, an interruption, a playback that never completes); 6 more in `tests/founder-call.test.ts` through the scripted path (a date said to the phone question, split phones with a time and a duration between pieces, long digits in a refused line, wrong-field answers, read-back against the saved record and vault, a carrier that never reports) plus 2 todo for the two open decisions; unit tests for the phone guard, the masker behaviour and the mismatch checks. Final full suite: 1549 tests, 1545 pass, 0 fail, 4 todo (the 2 known masker gaps and the 2 above). One earlier full run had a single failure in `call-diagnostics` ("the default limit accepts audio pieces far larger than the old one"), a test that sends an 800 KB piece and checks it after a fixed 60 ms pause: it is not touched by this change, passed alone, passed with every voice-route file together (144 tests) and passed in the final run, so it is load-sensitive timing in the test, not a defect found here (left as is).

**Unresolved, separate:** the owner view's eight-detail cap; calendar and booking (a past date, October 22 against a call on October 28, is accepted as text: nothing checks dates); the two decisions above; read-back length; GPT-Live (paused).

**Readiness for one retest (all must hold):** the server restarted from this commit (the log shows the new `PLAYBACK_SETTLED` line on the read-back); a read-back of any length is followed by the full silence wait after it ends; a date or time said to the phone question is refused (`NOT_DIGITS`) and asked again; the phone read back is exactly the digits said; the call reaches `READBACK`, `PLAYBACK_SETTLED PLAYED`, a spoken yes, `ADDRESS_READBACK`, `ADDRESS_CONFIRMED`; the saved record is confirmed with `missing_fields` empty and its details equal to what was read back; no detail was asked that the caller had already given.

## 5a-11. The fixed first-cleaning question and optional fields kept only with the caller's evidence (2026-10-28; code and tests; NOT retested live)

**Founder approval recorded (2026-10-28).** The first-cleaning question is exactly: **"Is this your first cleaning with Drew Cleaning?"** Yes means first cleaning; no means returning customer. These two decisions resolve the two open items of section 5a-10.

**The fixed question.** The voice route now speaks that question as a fixed line, word for word, instead of letting the text model word it (`firstClean.question`, built by `firstCleanQuestion(business_name)` in the voice service; the older ConversationRelay entry is unchanged). A repeat ask, or one after the caller talked over it, uses the same words; a plain yes or no counts as the answer only after this very question was cleanly delivered (the existing rule); explicit wording ("it is my first cleaning", "a returning customer", "not my first") still counts anywhere and the newest wins, which is how corrections work. Log: `FIRST_CLEAN_ASKED`. Tested both answers, a correction before the read-back, a correction at the read-back, and a question talked over.

**Optional fields.** A field the category does NOT require and that has a known topic (last professional clean, home empty, priority areas, number of rooms) is kept, read back and saved only with evidence from the caller: an answer bound to that question in context (a plausible reply to it; being asked alone is not evidence, and a vague reply is not an answer), or a caller line about the topic that contains the value, or a value that itself names the topic ("six months ago", "the house is empty"). "Yes" said to another question, or "once a month" said as a frequency, cannot populate home empty or last professional clean; nor can "I clean it myself once a month" (the word clean alone is not about a professional clean). Required fields, and fields with no known topic, are never policed, and nothing is invented: an unsupported optional value is taken out (not found, and not reported missing). Wiring: the call logic takes `requiredFields(tenant, category)` (`requiredFieldsOf` in `cleaning-state.ts`); the voice service supplies it. Log: `FIELD_UNSUPPORTED`, `FIELDS_RESTORED` (with `dropped`).

**What the caller's yes means for an edited item.** When the server put an answer back or took one out, the item's sealed handoff no longer describes it, so it is never handed off. If the caller confirmed the read-back (which showed exactly the edited item), the record is saved CONFIRMED and held for a person (`needs-human`, reason `human_review`, not handed off), the existing state used for person-routed requests; with no yes it stays unconfirmed as before. (Section 5a-10 saved such a record as unconfirmed; this replaces that.) Processing hand-off is not wired in any case.

**Tests.** 8 call-level tests through the scripted path (exact question and both answers, corrections, talked over, unrelated answers cannot populate the two fields, volunteered ones are kept, required fields are not policed) and 5 unit tests of the evidence rule. Full suite: 1560 tests, 1558 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo. The 2 todo are the long-known masker gaps, unrelated to this step and reported, not failures: a lower-case name that is not on the approved list and is said with no introduction is not masked, and a fully title-cased transcript is over-masked. Neither was touched; nothing here depends on repeating a run.

**Remaining and unresolved (separate):** the owner view's eight-detail cap; calendar and booking (a past date is accepted as text); read-back length (now only required, asked-in-context or volunteered fields are read, which shortens it, but it is not capped); the fixed question is spoken by the voice route only; GPT-Live (paused). **Readiness for one straightforward completion test:** the server restarted from this commit; a standard residential cleaning requested in one sentence (house, two bedrooms, two bathrooms); every question answered plainly and once; the first-cleaning question heard in exactly the approved words; a ten digit phone number said in two parts; the address as a house number, then a street name; the read-back heard to its end; yes, then yes to the address. The log must then show `FIRST_CLEAN_ASKED`, `READBACK`, `PLAYBACK_SETTLED` with outcome PLAYED, `ADDRESS_READBACK`, `ADDRESS_CONFIRMED`, no `INCOMPLETE`, no `CONFIRM_TIMEOUT`; the record is confirmed with `missing_fields` empty, details equal to the read-back, and home empty and last professional clean absent unless you said them.

## 5a-12. Phone pieces merged once, the item being completed kept, digit-count guidance, complete-address validation on every path, and "I am" (2026-10-28; code and tests; NOT retested live)

**Why (the 4:02 PM call, record #14, server PID 39264, commit `eae7c68`).** The saved record matched the dashboard (house, two bedrooms, two bathrooms, no pets, one time, no extras, first cleaning yes, morning, October 28 intact; unconfirmed, held for a person; the optional-field and fixed-question fixes held live). The call ended incomplete on the phone: pieces 3, 3, a line with no digits, then 7, 6, 7 and 4 digits, each over-long piece RESTARTED (replacing, never duplicating or retaining, the held digits), so a caller who restated the repeated exchange could never finish. The address was asked once (a house number alone), then the rotation put the phone back and the street name was never asked; and the phone question after a partial answer was only "ask it a different way". Offline the whole sequence was reproduced through the scripted path, and three more defects were found: a street name said to another question could fill the address with no house number (through the masker's own detection), a whole address said to the phone question was not recognised, and the last fix (5a-10) refused any phone line containing the word "am" ("I am at 212 ...").

**Fix 1: phone pieces.** A new piece is appended when that makes a whole number (ten digits, or eleven with a leading 1: legitimate repeated digits such as "five five five" twice land here); otherwise, when it starts by repeating at least three digits of the END of what is held and exactly ONE reading makes a whole number, the repeated digits are said once (reason `MERGED`: 212555 then 5550142 is 2125550142); two readings are ambiguous. A piece equal to six or more digits already held is a restatement (`SAME`, nothing changes). Anything that does not fit (more digits than a number can have, no unambiguous overlap) is `AMBIGUOUS`: the held digits are forgotten and the caller is asked for the whole number again, from the start; a whole number said (ten to fifteen digits) always replaces everything, which is how a correction works. No digit is ever guessed; the digits of every piece, accepted or not, are still taught to the masker.

**Fix 2: the field being completed is kept.** While a phone number or an address is partly heard it is asked next, before any other missing item, until it is whole, the caller declines ("I'd rather not give my number": the pieces are forgotten, log `CONTACT_DECLINED`, and the item goes back to the ordinary rotation), or its existing limit ends the call (still one ask plus two clarifications, with the existing three credits per call for pieces). The phone question after a partial answer now tells the voice exactly how many digits are still needed: `(part of the number is already heard: ask only for the remaining 7 digits)`, or, after an ambiguous fit, `(the pieces heard did not fit together: ask for the whole number again, from the start, digit by digit)`. Only COUNTS are in the instruction and the logs (`ASK` carries `need`); no held digit is ever in a model instruction or a log.

**Fix 3: complete-address validation on every path.** The masker's own detection (which feeds the address when the caller said it unprompted) now fills the address only when it is complete (a street number plus a street name, the same rule as every other capture path). A street name said to another question is no longer an address and the held house number stays held.

**Fix 4: "I am".** Only a number with am or pm, or a clock time, is a time ("10 am", "ten pm", "nine thirty a.m.", "9:30"); the word "am" alone is conversation.

**Confirmation preserved.** The whole phone number is read back (digit by digit) and the whole address is read back for a yes, as before; corrections, the clarification limits and masking are unchanged. A merged or unambiguous number is only ever the digits the caller said.

**Tests.** Unit (merge, restatement, repeated digits, ambiguity, whole-number correction, counts, "I am", clock times, fallback, selection) and through the scripted path: the reproduced live sequence (it now finishes, with the ask order phone, phone, phone, phone, address, address, the counts 7, 4, 4, the whole number and the whole address read back and confirmed, the record confirmed), repeated digits, an ambiguous fit, a decline, a number never finished (ends incomplete on the phone, nothing stored), "I am", and a street said to another question. Full suite: 1572 tests, 1570 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo (the two old masker gaps).

**Remaining uncertainty.** (1) The voice's wording of the phone question is still written by the text model from the instruction; that it follows "ask only for the remaining N digits" is not verified live. (2) What the live caller actually said is inferred from counts; an overlap shorter than three digits is deliberately not merged and costs one repeat. (3) A whole address said to the phone question is not recognised as an address, and its house number may be taken as a phone piece (rare; not changed). (4) The playback verification is tested against the offline model of the voice service, not against OpenAI. (5) The two optional-field and confirmed-for-review behaviours of 5a-11 are unchanged. Separate and open: the owner view's eight-detail cap; calendar and booking; GPT-Live (paused).

**Readiness for one straightforward completion retest.** The server restarted from this commit. Standard residential request in one sentence; every question answered plainly and once; the first-cleaning question in the exact words; a ten digit number in two or three pieces (a repeated exchange is fine); the address as a house number, then the street name. The log must show `READBACK`, `PLAYBACK_SETTLED` PLAYED, `ADDRESS_READBACK`, `ADDRESS_CONFIRMED`, no `INCOMPLETE`, no `CONFIRM_TIMEOUT`; phone answers ending `CAPTURED` or `MERGED` with ten held; the record confirmed with `missing_fields` empty.

## 5a-13. The contact settle window, progress that is not a failed attempt, and the fixed date and time questions (2026-10-28; code and tests; NOT retested live)

**Why (the 5:15 PM call, record #16, server PID 18968, commit `0911bd9`, code of `6470b86`).** The caller said the full ten digit number twice. The log shows the first telling arrived as FOUR fragments of 3, 1, 1 and 4 digits (nine in all), each landing about a second after Drew began a new question, so each question was talked over (two were cut off with no word spoken) and each interruption split the speech further. The three credits per call for pieces were used up by the first three fragments, so the next asks counted; the one-digit question was ask 2, the "whole number again" question was ask 3, and the first 3-digit fragment of the second telling arrived just as the limit was reached: the call ended on a fragment of a number still being said, with the other seven digits not yet arrived. A final successful capture can never trigger the limit (the limit is checked only on the item asked next, and a captured number is no longer missing); the limit hit a partial turn. The date and time answers were saved as "October 28." and "Morning."; the log cannot show whether Drew's own date question mentioned a weekday (the model wrote the words and they are not logged).

**The settle window.** After a turn that ADDED to a phone number or an address that is not whole yet, the call waits until the caller has been quiet for `contactSettleMs` (1.5 seconds in the voice service) before it asks again, measured from the newest sign of speech (an interim result, an utterance or a talk-over). When a later utterance is already waiting, or arrives meanwhile, the question for this turn is cancelled (nothing is said), the same item stays the one being answered, and the next fragment is handled as a continuation: fragments of one number are merged instead of each being answered. **Maximum collection duration:** one wait lasts at most `contactCollectMaxMs` (8 seconds in the voice service), however much speech keeps arriving, and at most six turns in a row may skip their question for more speech; then the call asks. The wait applies to a partly given address (a house number then a street name) in the same way. Interruption handling is unchanged: a caller who talks over a question being spoken still stops it, and that question does not count as asked. Logs: `CONTACT_SETTLE` with outcome MORE, QUIET or MAX (and the skip count); no text or digit.

**Progress is not a failed attempt.** A turn that adds digits (or the missing part of an address) is progress: the question it answered is forgiven (once per question, up to twenty per call, which one number cannot exceed), and **the call is never ended on a turn that just added digits**. A repeat that adds nothing (the same house number said again, the same digits restated) is not progress. Hesitations and anything else that adds nothing still count, so the existing limit (one ask plus two clarifications) still ends a call that makes no progress; the ambiguity and correction safeguards of 5a-12 are unchanged.

**Fixed questions (founder-approved, 2026-10-28).** The date and the time of day are asked in exactly these words, as fixed lines the text model never words: **"What date would you prefer for the cleaning?"** and **"What time of day would you prefer?"** (with the first-cleaning question of 5a-11, three fixed cleaning questions). They offer no weekday, no availability and no booking confirmation; a repeat or a re-ask after a talk-over uses the same words. They apply only to skills that have those two fields. Log: `FIXED_QUESTION_ASKED`.

**Tests.** Through the scripted path with real timers: the live fragments 3, 1, 1, 4 sent back to back give ONE question asking for the single digit still needed; the live sequence with a pause, a talk-over, a restatement as one line, the address in two bursts, the full read-back (the whole number, the whole address), the confirmed record equal to the read-back and the vault's number; silence before the question (it waits the window); speech that never stops (bounded by the maximum); ten one-digit pieces (not ten failures) versus hesitations (still bounded); the address in two bursts; the two fixed questions in exact words, never worded by the model, repeated in the same words after a talk-over; settings validated. Full suite: 1582 tests, 1580 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo (the old masker gaps).

**Remaining uncertainty before one retest.** (1) The settle values (1.5 s quiet, 8 s maximum) are engineering choices from one call; they are not proven against real speech, and a longer pause inside a number would still be asked about (once, with the count of digits needed). (2) Whether the real voice service delivers interim results (the signal the wait extends on) as these tests assume is modelled, not verified; without them the wait still works from final utterances alone. (3) The restated number is taken whole only when it arrives as one line; the transcriber splitting it again is handled by merging, but a digit it drops cannot be recovered (the call then asks for the whole number again). (4) The wording of the other, model-written questions is still not logged. (5) The offline voice-service model, not OpenAI, is what the playback and interruption tests run against. **Readiness:** restart from this commit; the same straightforward call as in 5a-12; the log should additionally show `CONTACT_SETTLE` lines, at most one phone question per burst, and `FIXED_QUESTION_ASKED` twice.

## 5a-14. The first whole-path completion on the 2026-10-28 builds, and the post-preview timing fix (2026-10-28; code and tests; NOT retested live)

**The live result (7:54 to 7:57 PM Central, server PID 4204, commit `b8c7e0a`; record #17).** One verified Intake completion **with friction**. M1 is NOT complete. Evidence is the server log (fixed codes and counts only), the saved record and the owner API; the caller-side transcription is the founder's and is not in the repository, and the spoken words are not logged.
- **Backend:** READBACK, ADDRESS_READBACK, ADDRESS_CONFIRMED, CONFIRMED (21 turns). Record: Standard cleaning, status ready, confirmation confirmed, address caller-confirmed, no reasons, no missing fields, nothing held for human review; the encrypted contact row exists for the call (30-day expiry); the home-empty, last-professional-clean and priority-areas items are absent. The owner API returns the record (17 calls); it is hidden only by the "Needs follow-up" filter, which is correct because it needs none. `handed_off` is a flag only: the Processing hand-off is not wired.
- **Friction, in order:** (1) the phone number arrived as fragments (1, then 2, 2 and 1 digits, then 4); the questions "nine digits remaining" and "four remaining" were spoken over the next fragments; (2) the first address answer was refused (NOT_AN_ADDRESS) and the address was asked a second time; (3) the job read-back was played in full (corrected in 5a-15), then a line was judged unclear, the short re-prompt was cut at 0 ms acknowledged by the caller's yes, and that yes was accepted; (4) the spoken "Correct" to the address read-back was ignored (ADDRESS_ANSWER_IGNORED) and the address was read back again before the second yes was taken.

**Cause of (1), reproduced and fixed here.** The settle window of 5a-13 was applied BEFORE the Intake preview (about three seconds). Fragments that arrived after the quiet window but during the preview were queued while the question already chosen for the earlier fragment was spoken over them. **Fix:** immediately before anything is spoken (a question, a clarification or the listen line), the handler looks again. A later utterance already waiting makes the question stale: it is not said, the item stays the one being answered, and the waiting line is handled next. While a number or an address is still being given, resumed speech (an interim result) after the preview holds the question until the caller has been quiet for the window again. The total wait is bounded by the same maximum as before (one budget per turn, measured from the start of the turn) and at most six turns in a row may skip their question. Log: `CONTACT_SETTLE` with `stage: AFTER_PREVIEW` and outcome MORE (cancelled) or MAX. **Tests:** four delayed-preview tests through the scripted path with real timers and a model whose Intake run takes 400 to 700 ms (fragments arriving during the preview, interim results resuming past its end, a bounded wait under never-ending speech, an ordinary question made stale by a later line); three of the four fail without the fix. Full suite: 1586 tests, 1584 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo (the old masker gaps).

**A test-only CI finding.** The GitHub run of the first push failed once in `founder-voice-service.test.ts` ("settings: with none configured the documented defaults reach the wire"), the test that failed once before with no detail: the stand-in refused a `response.cancel` of a reply that had already finished (a benign race of a caller sound against the end of the reply; the bridge tests already tolerate exactly this refusal). The assertion now allows that one refusal and no other. Other tests in that file assert an empty refusal list without the allowance and could in principle race the same way; they have not failed.

**Unresolved, reported and NOT changed.**
- **(2) The first address answer.** The caller-side transcription gives the first address as a house number and street (synthetic equivalent: "742 Evergreen Terrace"). Every rendering tried offline is accepted by the parser (digits or number words, hyphenated, with a lead-in, a filler, a city, a zip, an apartment, a trailing word, upper case): none reproduces NOT_AN_ADDRESS. That code is returned only when the line contains digits but is neither a whole address nor a lone house number or street name (for example a late tail of the telephone number, or a number run of more than six digits). So the backend did not necessarily receive the text the caller-side transcription shows (the transcriber may have split, merged or mis-heard it). The cause is **unknown** and no change is made to address parsing.
- **(3) and (4) Confirmation timing (corrected in 5a-15).** The address question was cut with 6,000 of 7,450 ms acknowledged; the "81 characters" was an estimate from that figure (an assumed 70 ms per character), not a measurement, and an earlier remark that it matched the end of the question is withdrawn. The rule that ignored the answer worked from that estimate. The two confirmations are also **inconsistent**: the address answer needs the whole question to have been reported as played, while a yes to the JOB read-back after a CUT playback was accepted with no coverage check (only a failed send blocks it). Neither rule is changed here.
- **Proposal (approved and built in 5a-15): a playback-aware boundary.** An affirmation is accepted only when the transport's own playback record shows that every detail and the confirmation question were heard: the voice route reports what was played for the response item (audio played-out position and the item's transcript), the server treats the question as heard when that report covers the last detail and the question sentence, a yes before that point is held (not discarded) and the read-back is repeated or the question re-asked in full, and the rule applies equally to the job read-back and the address read-back. Estimated character counts alone must not loosen it. Not yet verified: whether the live OpenAI route reports the played-out position and transcript at the fidelity this needs.

## 5a-15. One confirmation flow for the job read-back and the address question, decided by measured playback (2026-10-29; code and tests; NOT retested live)
