**What the evidence is (and is not).** Four things are different and are kept apart: (1) *generated by the provider* (the voice service made the audio and a transcript of the words it generated; it generates faster than real time, so this says only what was said); (2) *sent* (the bridge adds up the milliseconds of audio it handed to the phone and puts a mark after each chunk, about every half second); (3) *acknowledged* (the phone echoes a mark once it has played the audio before it; live, the 32.7 second job read-back was fully acknowledged about 27 seconds after generation ended, so the echo follows real playout); (4) *estimated heard position* (the old conversion of acknowledged milliseconds to characters at 70 ms per character, which this change stops using for confirmation). **The strongest claim any of this supports is "played to the caller's line". It does not show that the person heard, understood or attended to it** (handset, noise, inattention are invisible to the server), and the phone provider's acknowledgement semantics are still documentation plus one live call.

**Corrections to 5a-14 (documentation only).** (a) The job read-back at 7:57 PM was **played in full** (32,700 of 32,700 ms acknowledged), not cut. What was cut, at 0 ms acknowledged (800 ms sent), was the 50-character short re-prompt "Sorry, I did not catch that. Please say yes or no.", sent because a line after the read-back was judged unclear (the log records no words); the caller's yes cut it and was accepted. (b) The address question was cut with **6,000 of 7,450 ms acknowledged**; the "81 characters" in the log was that figure divided by an assumed 70 ms per character (about 85) and trimmed to a word boundary: an estimate, not a measurement. The earlier statement that 81 characters "fits" the end of the question was a coincidence of a synthetic address and is withdrawn. (c) The estimate also failed in the loosening direction: the real pace was about 81 to 84 ms per character, so an interruption in roughly the last 14 percent of a clip would have counted as heard in full.

**Built.** (1) **Measured verdict.** The bridge reports, with each finished reply, the milliseconds of audio sent and acknowledged; the voice adapter counts a reply as played only if something was sent and **all** of it was acknowledged, the words match, and it was not cut or lost. There is no tolerance: one mark or one millisecond short is not played. The verdict and the two numbers are logged (`CONFIRM_UNIT`, `PLAYBACK_SETTLED`, `CONFIRM_HELD`: fixed codes and milliseconds only). (2) **One flow.** The speech just sent for a confirmation (the job read-back, the address question, or a repeat of the question) is a unit. A yes confirms only if, **when the caller's line arrived**, that unit had been played in full; a unit that finishes playing later does not rescue an earlier yes. (3) **Wording.** "Please say yes or no." now comes before the final "Is that correct?" in the job read-back, the address question and the repeat prompts, so the last words spoken are the question; the repeat of the question alone is "Please say yes or no. Is that correct?". (4) **An early yes** confirms nothing and does not become valid later: it is logged (`CONFIRM_HELD` with kind and reason CUT, LOST, TIMEOUT or EARLY), and a fresh yes is required after verified playback of what is spoken again. If the details themselves were heard and only a repeat of the question was cut, only the question is asked again (at most twice); otherwise the read-back is said again (the existing limit of two) or the address question again (the existing limit). (5) **A no, a correction or an unclear line** is processed as before, whether or not the unit was heard; only a yes needs the whole unit. (6) **Estimated-character gates removed** from the voice route's confirmation. For a carrier that cannot verify playback (the text relay) a sent unit counts as heard until the provider reports it talked over, and then the provider's reported words must cover the unit. (7) Bounded outcomes unchanged: after the limits the call ends unconfirmed and held for a person, never confirmed.

**Tests.** The live call's measured timings (32,700/32,700; 7,450/6,000; 7,550/7,550; 800/0; 3,900/3,900) as verdict cases; one millisecond short; nothing sent; unmeasured; the same verdict for any speech pace (10 to 387 characters at 40 to 120 ms per character); through the bridge and phone stand-ins: a fully acknowledged reply, the last mark never returned, late echoes of marks after a clear (they change nothing and the reply is not played), a lost send, marks that never return (bounded, `timeout` for a short caller bound); through the call logic: the live job sequence (details heard, unclear line, yes over the repeated question: held, only the question again, a fresh yes goes on), the live address sequence (yes over the question: held, asked again in full, the old yes not valid later, a fresh yes confirms and saves), a yes over the details (held, said again, no fresh yes ends unconfirmed), speeds 2 and 45 ms per character, a read-back one mark short, a no or correction while not heard, repeated early yes answers ending unconfirmed, and the text-relay path. Full suite: 1601 tests (1599 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo, the old masker gaps).

**Not changed.** The unexplained first address answer (5a-14) is not touched. Whether the live phone provider's acknowledgements behave as modelled for the shorter replies, and whether the voice service's cancel reaches the real service in time, are unverified until the combined retest.

## 5a-16. The first verified clean M1 Intake pass, and the M1 acceptance rule (2026-10-29; live result recorded from the log, the saved record and the owner API; documentation only)

**The result (Oct 29, 10:28 AM Central, server PID 11496, commit `6250a1b` (the code of 5a-14 and 5a-15); record #18).** The **first verified clean M1 Intake pass**. It is the first of the three completions M1 now needs (below). It is **not** M1 complete, and it does not change the label of the earlier completion: the 7:57 PM call of 5a-14 stays recorded **"with friction"**. What this call shows is read from fixed-code logs, the saved record and the owner API only; the caller-side transcription is the founder's and is not in the repository, and no spoken word is logged, so agreement between the spoken read-back and the saved details is by construction and by the dashboard's agreement with the record, not a comparison of words.

- **Backend.** The log shows the whole path once: consent, nine cleaning questions (property type, bedrooms, bathrooms, pets, frequency, extras, then the fixed first-cleaning, date and time questions in their approved words), name, phone, address, `READBACK`, `ADDRESS_READBACK`, `ADDRESS_CONFIRMED`, `CONTACT_STORED`, `CONFIRMED`, `RECORD_NEW`, then the goodbye played in full before the hang-up. Every cleaning question was asked once (n=1); no `INCOMPLETE`, `CONFIRM_HELD`, `READBACK_NOT_HEARD` or `ADDRESS_ANSWER_IGNORED`. Record #18: Standard cleaning, status ready, confirmation confirmed, address status caller-confirmed, no reasons, no missing fields, nothing held for a person. Saved fields: house, two bedrooms, two bathrooms, pets no, one time, no extras, first cleaning yes, preferred date "October 29th.", time window "Morning."; home empty, last professional clean and priority areas absent (nothing in the call supported them).
- **Contact and privacy.** One encrypted contact row exists for the call (30-day expiry; counts only were read). The name, number and address were never read in this review. Logs hold fixed codes and counts and millisecond figures only. The owner API entry holds no digit string and no street word; its address line reads "Confirmed by the caller on the phone. Not checked against any address data."
- **Dashboard (founder's screenshot, checked against the owner API).** The 10:28 AM entry reads "Confirmed by the caller", requested time "October 29th." (a request as said, not a booking), Pets "No", time window "Morning", address confirmed with no value shown. The owner API returns it as the newest of 18 entries with `needsFollowUp` false; the summary shows 18 received in total.
- **Phone-number fragments.** The number arrived as fragments of 3, 3 and 4 digits within about five seconds; the settle window and the post-preview re-check (`CONTACT_SETTLE` with `stage: AFTER_PREVIEW`, outcome MORE) cancelled the stale question, no question was spoken over the caller, and the number was captured whole on the third fragment. The address arrived as a number then a street name and was joined (`NEED_STREET`, `JOINED`) with one cancelled question. **No `NOT_AN_ADDRESS` occurred this time.**
- **Measured playback (5a-15).** Every reply, including the consent notice, was fully acknowledged by the phone (`how: acked`, acknowledged milliseconds equal to sent). The job read-back: 35,750 of 35,750 ms (`CONFIRM_UNIT` kind JOB, outcome PLAYED); the address question: 9,900 of 9,900 ms (kind ADDRESS, PLAYED); the caller's yes to each came only after its unit had been played in full. **Limit, unchanged:** acknowledged means played to the caller's line, not that the person heard or understood it.
- **Not shown by this call.** The early-yes hold (`CONFIRM_HELD`) and the question-only repeat were not exercised live (no early yes was given); they are proven offline only. Availability and booking are not part of this verification: the stored date is the caller's words, nothing checked whether it is available or in the past, nothing was booked, and `handed_off` is a flag (the Processing hand-off is still unwired).

**Unresolved, preserved.** (1) **The first address answer of the 7:57 PM call** (`NOT_AN_ADDRESS`): no existing evidence explains it. The founder's caller-side transcription of that call shows an address beginning "Address: 742" and a house number and street; that text is not in the repository, and the backend text for that turn is not logged. Every synthetic rendering tried offline (5a-14) is accepted by the parser, so the backend may not have received the text the transcription shows. Nothing was changed. The 10:28 AM call's clean address does **not** explain it; it only shows the usual path works. (2) Whether the real phone and voice service behave as modelled when a caller answers early (the early-yes hold).

### M1 acceptance (founder decision, 2026-10-29)

**M1 is accepted when three consecutive verified clean completions have been seen live, this 10:28 AM call being the first.** Two more are needed, one per script below. The other exit criteria stand: every defect any call found has a regression test, the status board keeps live and offline results apart, and the founder accepts M1 in writing.

**"Verified clean"** means all of: the whole path completes (consent, the cleaning questions, name, number, address, read-back, address confirmation, a confirmed and ready record that the owner API shows as confirmed by the caller); every unit of confirmation speech was played in full before the yes that was taken; the log shows **no unintended repetition** (below) and no unexplained refusal; the owner API entry equals the record and shows no contact value; logs hold only fixed codes and counts. **Reading to confirm with the founder:** a call that is not clean breaks the run; the run then restarts from zero after the defect has a regression test, and the 10:28 AM call would no longer count unless the founder says otherwise.

**Expected safe clarification versus unintended repetition.**
- *Expected and safe (allowed in a clean call, but only where the script causes it):* a read-back said again **after a correction** (counts toward the limit of two read-backs); a unit said again **after an early yes** (`CONFIRM_HELD` with a reason of CUT or EARLY, then the read-back or the question again, then a fresh yes); one question cancelled by the settle window before a fragmented number or address (`CONTACT_SETTLE` MORE) with a single question after the burst.
- *Unintended (makes the call not clean):* any cleaning question asked a second time without a cause; the number or the address asked again after it was captured, or asked once per fragment; a repeat of a unit that was played in full and answered; a `CONFIRM_HELD` without an early yes; `READBACK_NOT_HEARD` or `ADDRESS_ANSWER_IGNORED` when the caller waited; `INCOMPLETE`; a goodbye cut off; a refusal such as `NOT_AN_ADDRESS` for an address said plainly; a yes taken before its unit was fully played.

**The two remaining scripts (invented details; each is one call; nothing is authorized by writing them down).**
1. **Early yes.** Give a plain request (as on 10:28 AM). While the **job read-back** is playing, say "yes" over it once. Expect `CONFIRM_HELD` (kind JOB, reason CUT) with fewer acknowledged than sent milliseconds, `READBACK` a second time, nothing confirmed, no address question yet. Let the second read-back play to the end, then say "yes". At the **address question**, say "yes" over it once; expect `CONFIRM_HELD` (kind ADDRESS), the address question again in full; let it finish and then say "yes". Expect `ADDRESS_CONFIRMED`, a confirmed ready record, exactly two job read-backs and two address questions. A third repetition of either, or a confirmation before the fresh yes, is a failure.
2. **Correction.** Give a plain request, answering one question with a value to be corrected later (for example, say two bathrooms). Let the job read-back play to the end and say "no", then say "change the bathrooms to three" (or the equivalent). Expect the correction question, a fresh pass, the read-back **once more** with the corrected value (the safe repeat), then "yes" after it is played in full, the address confirmed, and a record that shows three bathrooms and equals the second read-back. Failure: the old value surviving, a second correction question without cause, a third read-back, or a confirmation of the first read-back.

**Next single test (proposal, not authorized): script 1, the early yes.** It is the newest code and has not yet been seen live. Script 2 follows only after script 1 is verified.

## 5a-17. Name-collection robustness: an UNRESOLVED M1 acceptance item (2026-10-29; documentation only; nothing changed, nothing started)

**Status: OPEN. This is not a closed defect and it is not a diagnosed one.** It is recorded as an unresolved item that M1 acceptance must settle (resolved by the review below and any founder-approved change, or explicitly waived by the founder in writing) before M1 is accepted and before any customer-readiness claim.

**Evidence (Oct 29, 11:04 AM call, record #19; server PID 11496, commit `6250a1b`).** The call completed the whole path and was saved confirmed and ready. The first answer to the name question was refused (`CONTACT_ANSWER` field NAME, reason `NOT_A_NAME`), the number and the address were then collected, and the name was asked again and captured on the second asking. The log shows that the refused line was a real, separate caller utterance after the name question had been played in full (all 19 caller utterances of the call map one to one onto answers; no interrupts), and that it was checked as a name because the name was the field being asked. The words of that utterance are not logged, and the wording of the model-written first name question is not logged either (it was 36 characters; the second asking was 41). The founder's chat transcription showed no first name question; **the cause is unexplained, and the discrepancy between the transcription and the backend is not attributed to either side.** The investigation of that one call was **closed as inconclusive** by the founder: completion verified, first-name refusal unexplained. The 10:28 AM clean pass is preserved. Consecutive verified clean passes: 1; verified clean in total: 1; whole-path completions in total: 3 (7:57 PM with friction, 10:28 AM, 11:04 AM). The 11:04 AM call neither extended nor reset the run.

**Offline observations (not caused by that call, and not a finding about it).** With synthetic lines, the name rule accepts plain replies ("Dan", "it's Dan", "my name is Dan", "Yes, Dan", "call me Dan") and **refuses** "you can put it under Dan" and "put it under Dan" (lead-ins it does not know); it also **accepts** lines that are not names ("Hold On", "Excuse Me", "Pardon", "Dan Speaking"). These show that the rule has gaps in both directions. They do not say what the caller said at 11:04 AM. **No validation was changed and no diagnostic was added.**

**Proposed bounded offline review (NOT started; to follow the early-yes test; needs the founder's go-ahead).** Scope: the name step only, offline, synthetic data, no live call, no key, no restart. (1) Build a table of natural name replies and corrections (lead-ins such as "put it under" and "you can call me", fillers and hesitations, spelled-out names, one and two part names, nicknames, a name restated after a refusal, "no, it's Dan not Don", a name given while the question is being answered late, refusals, non-name answers such as dates or yes and no), with the expected outcome of each stated first. (2) Run them through the real name step and the real call flow (the ask, the refusal and the later re-ask, the read-back of the name, a correction after the read-back) and record accepted, refused and re-asked, against the expectation. (3) Report the mismatches only, with evidence. (4) Regression tests only for mismatches the table demonstrates. (5) **No change to validation without the founder's separate approval of the specific change**; a decision on any change, and on whether the item can close, is the founder's. The review would not use the log of the 11:04 AM call to guess what was said.

## 5a-18. A mask placeholder is never an answer to a job question: the Pets value of the 12:05 PM call (2026-10-29; code and tests; NOT retested live)

**What happened (record #21, Oct 29, 12:05 PM, commit `6250a1b`).** The saved Pets value was the address's mask placeholder, and the record was confirmed and ready. **Record #21 is kept as it is, as a historical confirmed record that contains an invalid Pets value; it was not altered, and no repair is proposed.** The owner view withholds the placeholder (the dashboard shows Pets as withheld) and shows no value; the read-back most likely said "Pets: a detail you gave." (the read-back builder's wording for a placeholder; the audio is not available to verify). No contact value is recorded here. **Record #18 (10:28 AM) is a separate matter: its Pets wording was closed as inconclusive with no defect demonstrated** (the chain from question to owner view was consistent, and the provider's own transcript matched the requested read-back).

**The path (reproduced offline on the real scripted path).** (1) The extraction model returned the placeholder as the Pets value; why it did so, and why the first pets answer was judged ambiguous, is not known (the words and the model's output are not logged). (2) The grounding check only requires a value to appear in the masked text, and the placeholder does, so it passed. (3) The answer tracker rejects such a value as an implausible pets answer, so it was never *accepted*, but it was also never *removed*. (4) The overlay restores only an answer that had been accepted; none had been, so the placeholder stayed. (5) The read-back spoke it as "a detail you gave", the caller confirmed, and the record kept the raw value.

**The fix (`cleaning-state.ts`; offline; separately authorized 2026-10-29).** A value that is **only mask placeholders and punctuation** (for example `[ADDRESS_1]`) is never accepted for a field that is not an address or a location field, and the overlay **holds** such a field on every path, including the final pass: not found, listed as missing and asked first, with the ambiguous-field reason, so the record cannot be ready while it is unresolved. The caller's next answer is then taken in the ordinary way, and an answer already accepted still wins over a later placeholder. It uses the existing hold-and-clarify path and adds no state. Address and location fields keep their placeholder (that is what the masker is meant to put there). Masking, corrections, names and the read-back wording are unchanged.

**Explicitly outside this fix's safety claim.** A value that **mixes** a placeholder with other words (for example "no [PERSON_1]") is not held, and **no safety claim is made for it**. Through the scripted path the grounding check already rejects such a value unless the masked text contains it verbatim, so the hazard was **not demonstrated** end to end; a unit test records that the overlay leaves such a value alone. Widening the rule needs its own evidence and approval.

**Tests.** Unit (`tests/cleaning-state.test.ts`, 6 new): what counts as placeholder-only; the overlay holds it and the record cannot be ready; merge ignores it and an accepted answer stands over a later placeholder; address and location fields exempt; legitimate answers (a plain no, a short descriptive answer) and a correction undisturbed; the mixed value left alone (marked as a known hazard outside the fix). Scripted path (`tests/founder-call.test.ts`, 4 new): **the exact reproduced failure** (the first pets answer never accepted, a placeholder returns: Pets is asked again, the real answer saved, no "a detail you gave", confirmed ready with the real value); an unresolved placeholder-only required field never reaches a confirmed ready record and is never read back; legitimate pet answers pass through; masking and the address unchanged. The first two scripted tests fail without the fix. Full suite: 1611 tests, 1609 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo (the old masker gaps).

**Readiness.** The running voice server does **not** have this change until the founder restarts it. This task is separate from the early-yes test (a live call) and from the name-collection review (offline, no change proposed). Live, the absence of a placeholder on a call proves little because the model's behaviour varies.

## 5a-19. Scripted question table and durable first-cleaning (2026-10-29; code and offline tests; NOT retested live)

**Why.** The 18:44 to 18:49Z PID 50320 call (record #24, unconfirmed) showed five consecutive 42-character replies for five different fields after the phone restate. The wording was not logged, so **the live cause is NOT established**. What was demonstrated offline is only that a text model whose wording stops following the selected field can stall the scripted path (the asks repeat, the bounds hold, no read-back). The founder approved (2026-10-29) removing that dependency for required questions, as a scripted-mode change that does not cancel the later native-conversation decision.

**What changed (`scripted-questions.ts` new; `relay-handler.ts`; `founder-voice-service.ts`; `dialogue-model.ts` exports one helper).** With scripted turn control (a carrier that does not converse natively) every question for a required item, and the category question, is a fixed sentence chosen by the field the code selected and the situation (nothing heard, no digits, N digits still needed, start again, street or house number missing). The text model words only the opening, the "listen" turn and the read-back stays the fixed builder. The date, time, first-cleaning and residential questions keep their approved wording. No generic repeat apology; a repeat is the same sentence. The category question is built from the configured residential category names (the declined one is never named). Business names are not in the table (the first-cleaning question takes it from the persona). A required field without an entry gets one plain generic sentence and the id `GENERIC`; the live skill has none (a test asserts it). The log carries only the field code and the template id (`SCRIPTED_ASK`). Native Realtime stays opt-in and inactive (a native carrier never uses the table); GPT-Live stays paused.

**Durable first-cleaning (`guardFirstClean`).** The caller's own newest attributable answer is put back when a later Intake pass omits it, reads something unclear, or reads a different value; only the caller's own newer answer replaces it (corrections work). The item is marked edited (its sealed handoff no longer describes it), so a confirmed request is held for a person, as the other server-side restores are.

**Tests (`tests/founder-call.test.ts`, 16 new passing, 4 reported-gap todo; two `founder-voice-service` tests updated because they asserted model-worded asks).** Whole conversations through the real scripted path with a caller that answers the sentence it hears, delayed and non-cooperative extraction (omitted, stale, flipped, unclear values), checking the final record and the read-back: table integrity; a text model that always asks for the phone number (never consulted for a required item); omitted counts and pets; House/house and 2/two; compound counts; none/nothing; one correction each of bedrooms, pets and first-cleaning (the existing rule allows one correction round per call); durable first-cleaning in three modes; split phone and address with the situation-specific sentences; privacy of the logs; interrupted asks; each category's own required fields; the category question. The wrong-field, durable and privacy tests fail without the changes. Full suite: 1631 tests, 1625 pass, 0 fail, 6 todo (the two masker gaps and the four below).

**Reported (RESOLVED by 5a-20, 2026-10-29: the founder approved the meanings and the fix).** (1) **Bare "none", "no preference", "never" or "no" to a free-text field (Priority areas, Last professional clean; Deep cleaning only)** is not bound to the question: the call state binds an answer only for count, yes/no, frequency, date and time fields, and Intake sees the caller's lines, not the question, so the answer is read only if the extraction model happens to map it, and otherwise the question is asked again. Shown with four todo tests. The smallest candidate fix (not made) is to bind a bare decline phrase to a free-text field in `cleaning-state.ts` `bind`, as already done for yes/no fields. (2) **Interrupted asks are not counted as asked, by design**, so a caller who talks over every question is bounded only by the 30-turn and 8-minute limits (shown: the call ended at the turn cap with the fixed goodbye, saved unconfirmed, never handed on). (3) The name check accepted a non-name phrase in one scripted run; name validation is the open item 5a-17 and was not touched. (4) A second "no" at the read-back ends the call unconfirmed (one correction round); unchanged.

**Readiness.** The running voice server does NOT have this change until the founder restarts it. Nothing live was touched: no key, restart, configuration, Twilio or call.

## 5a-20. Refusals, uncertainty and the two Deep-cleaning free-text fields (2026-10-29; code and offline tests; NOT retested live)

**What was measured first (scratch run, read-only).** With an extractor that copies the caller's bare line into the field, a COMPLETE reply of "I'd rather not answer" or "not sure" was accepted for Priority areas and Last professional clean, read back as said, and the record was confirmed, ready and handed off (a refusal satisfied a required field). With an extractor that does not copy it, a plain "none", "no preference", "no" or "never" was never tied to its question (free-text fields were not bound), so the question was asked three times and the call ended incomplete: valid answers lost.

**Approved meanings (founder, 2026-10-29).** Priority areas: no, none, no preference (and the plain variants nope, nothing, no preferences) is an answer, stored in the caller's own words. Last professional clean: never (also "not ever", "we have never had one") is an answer; no, none, no preference there does not say when, so it is asked again with the same sentence. Nothing is inferred: no date or cleaning history is invented.

**Refusal and uncertainty (any non-address job field).** Only when the COMPLETE reply is a refusal or an "I don't know" ("I'd rather not answer", "I prefer not to say", "I don't know", "not sure", "no comment", "pass", "maybe" and the like), or, for the Last professional clean field, a reply that is not an answer to it, is a value for a field with nothing accepted held (not found, missing, ambiguous_field, asked again, never read back or saved as an answer). A longer answer that merely contains those words ("we don't know the old cleaner but it was in March", "no preference for the kitchen") is an ordinary answer, and "no", "none", "nothing" and "no pets" are never treated as refusals except where the field's own meaning above says so. An accepted answer is never overruled by a later refusal, and an explicit correction still replaces it. Clarification stays bounded by the existing ask limit: after it the call ends incomplete, unconfirmed, needs a person, the field listed as missing, never handed on (shown for every refusal case). **No "declined" state was added; a refusal never satisfies a required field.**

**Where (`cleaning-state.ts` only; no contract or call-logic change).** `bind` accepts a valid reply for the two fields as a labelled "Field: answer" line made from the already masked caller words (the context Intake reads, and the deterministic label extractor already has labels for both fields); `plausible` and the overlay use the same meanings. Restored fields still mark the item edited, so a confirmed record is held for a person (a test covers it for these fields); first-cleaning durability is unchanged.

**Tests.** `tests/cleaning-state.test.ts` (+5: meanings, the whole-reply rule with legitimate negatives and containing phrases, bind, hold, keep/accepted-over-refusal/correction) and `tests/founder-call.test.ts` (27 whole-path tests, replacing the four todo tests): eleven replies for the two fields under mapped and unmapped extraction with the final record, status, handoff, missing list and read-back; a clarification that resolves in two asks; a valid answer omitted by a later pass (restored, held for a person); an explicit correction at the read-back; privacy (the labelled line is masked wording only, no caller word in a log); refusals for Pets. 17 of the 27 fail against the previous code.

**Remaining gaps (reported, not changed).** (1) No path for a caller to WITHDRAW an accepted answer ("forget that"): it stays until a correction supplies a value. (2) Interrupted asks are still bounded only by the turn and time caps (5a-19). (3) A reply that merely contains `don't know` can still be rejected by the older substring test in bind/merge for count and yes/no fields; Intake's own value is then kept by the overlay (unchanged). (4) Name validation (5a-17) untouched. (5) Not loaded by the running server; no live evidence.

**The review (PID 15304, build `dff8a83`; read from fixed-code logs, the saved record and the owner API only; no contact value read).** The call (21:47:56Z to 21:52:32Z) was saved as record #25: Standard cleaning, **confirmed, ready, handed off, nothing missing, address caller-confirmed, 20 turns**; one encrypted contact stored (`CONTACT_STORED`); the owner entry reads "Confirmed by the caller", needs no follow-up, and agrees with the record. Every logged step after the verdict is consistent. **The verdict is NOT explained.** The founder's caller-side chat transcription shows that first reply as "It has two"; run offline through the actual classifier and the actual scripted path those words are **BOUND** (why OK) and the question is asked once, and the masker leaves them unchanged. The chat transcription is not the system's input (which is not logged), and **a different verdict does not prove different words reached the system**; the logs show only that it was a single caller line. The next measurement is the reason code below.

What would you like help with?"). That is the only turn in the scripted path where the text model still words a line on its own. **A fixed listen line is PROPOSED SEPARATELY and is NOT implemented**; its wording needs the founder's approval.
- **Name.** The name question was asked from the table and played; the first answer was refused (`NOT_A_NAME`); a second utterance already waiting was taken (`CAPTURED`) with no re-ask spoken; the contact was stored and the job read-back is built with it. ## 5a-23. The PID 47344 call: an address loop on a hyphenated house number (2026-10-29; log review, offline reproduction, code and tests; NOT retested live)

**What the live call showed (PID 47344, build `c0c271e`, 22:43:30Z to 22:47:23Z; record #26; fixed codes, the saved record and the owner API only; no contact value read).** The early-yes script was **not reached**: the call ended before any read-back. Held, with the tests that guard each: every required question was a table or fixed sentence and none was model-worded (`SCRIPTED TABLE`, `SCRIPTED WRONG-FIELD WORDING`, `SCRIPTED CATEGORY PATHS`); the property, bedrooms (asked once), bathrooms, pets, frequency, extras, first-cleaning, date and time answers were each accepted once with a reason code on the new `FIELD_ANSWER` (`SCRIPTED DIAGNOSTICS`); the name was captured on the first answer; the phone number was captured from three pieces (3, 3, 4 digits) with the settle window behaving (`SCRIPTED SPLIT CONTACTS`); every reply played in full, no interruption, no preview over budget this time. **Failed:** the address. The first answers came back `NEED_NUMBER`, `NEED_NUMBER`, `NEED_NUMBER`, then `SAME` (n=1), then `NO_STREET_NUMBER` (n=2), while the question asked was "What is the house number?" each time (template `ADDRESS_NUMBER`, n=1, then 2, then 3); the caller ended the call 5 seconds after the third such question. Saved unconfirmed, needs a person, reason not_confirmed, address status none, no contact stored; the owner entry reads "Not confirmed by the caller" with a follow-up action (26 entries in total). One call used of three on that server. Not counted.

**First proven state failure (offline, through the actual scripted path).** In `call-state.ts` a house number said **as a hyphenated number word** ("forty-two", "twenty-one", the form speech-to-text writes) was not recognised as a number: said alone it was classified as a **street name**. A street piece then replaces the street piece already held, so the number was never held, nothing could join, and the house-number question was asked again. Reproduced with the same reason codes in the same order and the same ask counters as the live log (`NEED_NUMBER` ×3, `SAME` n=1, `NO_STREET_NUMBER` n=2, only the `ADDRESS_NUMBER` template after the first ask). **What is NOT proven:** the caller's words were not logged, so it is not shown that "forty-two" was what was said or written by the transcriber on the live call; a similar synthetic scenario reproducing the logged codes does not prove the live cause. The defect in the parser is proven; its being the live cause is the best-fitting explanation of the logged sequence, unconfirmed. (The property-type answer of this call was saved as the word "Health", which suggests the transcription can differ from what was said; noted, unexplained, not changed.)

**The repair (`call-state.ts`, one line).** A number word may be two number words joined by a hyphen. A hyphenated street name ("Mary-Anne Street", "Fort-Lee") is still a street; a whole address is unchanged; a house number alone is still not an address. After it the same scenario gives `NEED_STREET` then `JOINED` with the question matching what is held (the street name).

**Tests.** `tests/founder-call.test.ts` (+11 `SCRIPTED ADDRESS`, whole path): the hyphenated number first then the street name; the street name first then the hyphenated number; the logged sequence cannot recur (a number repeated is `SAME` and the street name is asked for); working forms unchanged (whole address in digits, hyphenated, spaced number words; digits then street; spaced words then street; street then digits); a correction at the address confirmation with a hyphenated number (the corrected address is stored, the old one never repeated); bounded retries (a caller who only repeats the number ends incomplete: unconfirmed, held for a person, nothing handed on, no address stored, at most 4 address questions); privacy (no address word in any log). `tests/call-state.test.ts` (+2): the piece and contact-state rules. 4 of the 11 whole-path tests fail against the previous parser; the 7 working-form and correction tests pass before and after; the phone, confirmation and privacy tests are unchanged. Full suite: 1677 tests, 1675 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo (the old masker gaps).

**Unexplained intermittent failures (kept, cause NOT proven).** In earlier full runs one different timing-sensitive test failed each time (a realtime-bridge interruption test; the voice-service full-call test; a socket-size test in `call-diagnostics`); each passed alone and in the next full run. A load flake is likely, not shown.

**Remaining gaps.** The live words of the address answers; the "Health" transcription; the unexplained AMBIGUOUS bedrooms verdict of the PID 15304 call (now measurable with the reason codes; it did not recur here); the fixed listen line (proposed separately, not implemented); the early-yes script still unexercised; name review (5a-17); withdrawal; the older substring rule; interrupted asks bounded only by the turn and time caps. Not loaded by the running server until the founder restarts it. **Next single verification step:** restart with the helper, a read-only readiness check, then one founder-only early-yes call.

## 5a-24. Property type: an unsupported value is clarified once, then kept for a person (2026-10-29; code and offline tests; NOT retested live)

**The finding (offline acceptance check through the actual scripted path, then the founder's decision).** The configured property choices (house, apartment, condo, townhouse) were used only to word the question; nothing enforced them. A valid house intake, a whole or hyphenated/split address and a supported reply all produced a confirmed, ready, handed-off record, but so did an unsupported reply ("Health", "residential"): read back as said and saved confirmed, ready and handed off. Live evidence: record #25 was saved confirmed, ready and handed off with the value "residential"; record #26 saved the value "Health" (unconfirmed, because of the address loop of 5a-23). The same one-word, seven-character shape of the "Health" answer was logged live. Why the live transcription produced "Health" is not known.

**The rule (founder-approved 2026-10-29; property type ONLY, scripted mode only).**
- A property answer that is one of the configured choices (a whole word or its plural, in the caller's own words, with no choice negated and not two different choices) is accepted as before and nothing changes.
- Otherwise the property type is asked about **once more**, in a fixed sentence that lists the configured choices once: "Just to be sure, is it a house, apartment, condo, or townhouse?" (**founder-approved 2026-10-29**). The clarification is asked only once it was actually delivered, and never twice.
- An unambiguous supported answer to it is accepted (it reaches Intake as a labelled line in the caller's own masked words). Anything else is **kept as the caller's own masked words and never guessed, replaced or normalised** ("Health" is not "house", "residential" is not "house", "condominium" is not "condo"); the record is saved **confirmed but needs-human with the human_review reason, never ready and never handed off**.
- A negated choice ("not a house", "no house", "it isn't a house", "it is a house, not an apartment") is never accepted, even when the extraction returns just "house"; the caller's own answer line to the property question is checked as well as the extracted value. Two choices at once ("house or apartment") are asked about too.
- The final run cannot ask anything: an unsupported value there is kept and held for a person. The native and non-scripted paths do not ask; they hold for a person.
- If the caller's clarification is a choice but the extraction still returned something else, the caller's answer is used, and because the server set it the record is held for a person.

**Where.** `src/property-choice.ts` (new, pure: `classifyPropertyAnswer` → choice / negated / multi / none), `src/relay-handler.ts` (the guard beside the first-cleaning guard, the one-time clarification, the caller's answer lines), `src/scripted-questions.ts` (the fixed clarification sentence, id `JOB_PROPERTY_TYPE_CLARIFY`), `package.json` (the new unit test file). Logs carry fixed codes only (`PROPERTY_CLARIFY`, `PROPERTY_RESOLVED`, `PROPERTY_UNRESOLVED`), never a caller word. First-cleaning durability, the address, phone, privacy and measured-confirmation behaviours are unchanged.

**Tests.** `tests/property-choice.test.ts` (5): choices in the caller's words, unsupported values (nothing guessed), negation, two choices, whole-word matching. `tests/founder-call.test.ts` (9 `SCRIPTED PROPERTY`, whole path): "Health" then "house" and "residential" then "house" (asked once, clarified once, read back and saved as house, ready, handed off, the wrong word never saved); unresolved "Health" twice (kept as said, one clarification only, confirmed, needs-human, human_review, not handed off); a genuine off-list description (the newest kept); a negated choice with the extraction returning "house"; a negated answer that stays negated (kept as "no house") and two choices; volunteered and supported answers untouched (no clarification, ready, handed off); corrections (supported to another choice; unresolved to a choice); the extraction still returning the unsupported word; privacy. 7 of the 9 fail against the previous source; the volunteered and the correction test pass before and after. **A full-suite run found a real regression of mine** (a line inserted between an `if` and its `else` reset the ask history and broke 16 existing tests); it was fixed, and the suite is 1691 tests, 1689 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo (the old masker gaps).

**Remaining gaps.** A negation or an off-list property mentioned in the opening request (volunteered) is not checked against the caller's lines: only the answers to the property question and its clarification are. The clarification wording is founder-approved (2026-10-29). A genuinely unclear speech-to-text result is not distinguished from an honest off-list description (both are kept for a person). The hyphenated-number repair (5a-23), the reason codes (5a-22) and every earlier fix are not loaded by the running server until the founder restarts it. Unexplained intermittent test failures are still unexplained.

## 5a-25. The property rule wherever the answer occurs (2026-10-29; code and offline tests; NOT retested live)

**The offline check (actual scripted path; no live call).** The rule of 5a-24 held for a valid volunteered house (A) and for a volunteered off-list description (B: the one clarification was asked directly, and the caller's "house" was then accepted), but failed in two ways: **(C)** "this is not a house" in the opening request, with the extraction returning just "house", was accepted: read back and saved as "house", confirmed, ready and handed off; **(E)** the caller's answer to the clarification ("it is an apartment") lost to a stale extraction that still returned "house", which was saved ready and handed off. A third case, a later contradiction said to another question, was also not caught. The extraction stand-ins in these checks return the first or newest choice word like a loose model would; they show what the system does if the model does that, not that the live model does.

**The founder's approval (2026-10-29).** The bounded repair for C and E, plus explicit later contradictions, reusing the existing guard and the one-clarification limit; a mere mention of another property must not overwrite the service property; the caller's wording and the existing human-review rules are preserved. **The clarification wording "Just to be sure, is it a house, apartment, condo, or townhouse?" is approved.**

**The repair (`relay-handler.ts`, `property-choice.ts`, `cleaning-state.ts`).** The property guard now judges the caller's own words wherever they were said, not only the answer to the property question: every masked caller line is remembered in order. A choice returned by Intake is accepted unless (1) the caller's answer to the question or to the clarification is negated or names two choices, (2) a LATER line negates that choice and no explicit answer has settled it since ("this is not a house", in the request or in a reply to another question), or (3) the caller's answer to the question or the clarification names a different choice from the one Intake returned (a stale or wrong extraction), unless a correction ("actually", "sorry", "I meant" ...) follows. When the doubt is raised and the clarification has not been asked, the field is held and asked about once; otherwise the caller's newest relevant words are kept as said, the record is saved confirmed but needs-human with the human_review reason, and it is never handed off. **An un-negated mention of another property ("my sister has an apartment") never overwrites anything.** Because the guard's value must not be undone by an older accepted answer, the answer state gained `release(field)` (forget one field's accepted answer, candidate and conflict); it is called only by this guard and only when the guard changes the property field. The classifier gained `propertyMentions` (which choices a line affirms and which it negates) and `propertyChoiceOf`. The clarification is still asked at most once; an explicit correction to another choice is still taken as before.

**Remaining gaps.** An affirmed mention of the wrong property in the request alone ("my sister has an apartment", with nothing about the caller's own property) cannot be told from the service property without asking; a negated mention aimed at someone else's property ("my sister does not have a house") raises the one clarification although it need not. A speech-to-text slip is not told apart from an honest off-list description (both are kept for a person). A caller cannot withdraw an accepted answer other than the property type, which this guard can now release. Not loaded by the running server until the founder restarts it. The intermittent test failures are still unexplained. Other open items are unchanged.

## 5a-30. Address confirmation: recovery from an early affirmation and an honest ending when recovery is exhausted (2026-10-29/30; code and offline tests; NOT retested live)

**Two accounts, kept separate.** **The founder's account (call 2, record #28):** "I answered yes to confirm the address and understood it as confirmed." **The backend's outcome:** the address step ended `ADDRESS_UNCONFIRMED`, address status unverified, and the owner next action "Verify the address with the caller." Neither replaces the other. The first mismatch between them was that the only goodbye said "Your request is recorded", the same line as when an address is confirmed, and nothing told the caller the address had not been confirmed.

**Why `ADDRESS_CONFIRMED` did not fire (from the logs; unchanged).** The measured rule: a yes confirms the address only if the whole question had been acknowledged by the phone when the yes began (`acked_ms` at least `sent_ms`). The first yes began with 6,800 of 7,450 ms acknowledged and the second about a second into the replay with none acknowledged, so each was held (`CONFIRM_HELD`, reason CUT), and the second held answer reached the existing limit, ending the step unconfirmed. **Wording correction (evidence):** audio that was or was not acknowledged does not establish what the caller heard, and `spokenChars` is the provider's transcript length at the interrupt, not measured word-level playback; neither is used here to say what was heard. Earlier sentences in this file that read "97 of 97 characters spoken" as "the whole question was spoken" are corrected by this note.

**The repair (`relay-handler.ts`; the measured rule and the retry limits are NOT changed).** (1) When an affirmation comes before the address question was played in full it is held exactly as before, and the question is now asked again with a **fixed instruction in front: "Please wait until I have finished, then answer."** (**the wording was approved by the founder, 2026-10-29**). (2) A fresh yes after the whole replayed question confirms the address, as before. (3) If the recovery is exhausted (two held or unclear answers, or three corrections: the existing limits) the caller hears the **founder-approved ending: "Your request is recorded, but I could not confirm the address. Someone will need to verify it with you. Goodbye."** Only for the exhausted case; the ordinary ending, the refused-address ending and the job read-back are unchanged. The existing test that no fixed line promises a follow-up now names this one approved line and keeps the guard on every other.

**Downstream gate (verified, not changed).** `eligibleForProcessing` already returns false for any record whose address status is unverified, and for a caller-confirmed record unless the encrypted contact for the same call also says confirmed; it is asserted for the exhausted case here and in the earlier consistency test. **No production code calls it yet** (the hand-off seam is offered to by the call logic but the server does not pass it), so nothing downstream can currently act on any record.

**Tests.** `tests/playback-confirmation.test.ts` (+3, the simulator with measured playback): **record #28 reproduced** (two affirmations held, ordinary ending on the previous code; the instruction, the honest ending, address unverified, a follow-up for the owner, no `ADDRESS_CONFIRMED`, not eligible, no address in the owner view or logs on the new code); early yes then a fresh yes after the whole replay (confirmed, ordinary ending, eligible); an ordinary confirmation (no hold, no instruction). `tests/founder-call.test.ts`: the three-wrong-addresses and two-unclear endings say the approved line; three existing assertions on the replayed question now expect the instruction in front of it. The exhausted test fails against the previous source; the phone, address, split-contact, privacy and measured-confirmation tests are unchanged. Full suite: 1704 tests, 1702 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo (the old masker gaps).

**Remaining M1 gates (the criteria are unchanged).** (1) The founder's counting rulings (does call #27 count; does a non-clean call break the consecutive run). (2) Three consecutive verified clean live completions (1 so far; consecutive 0 read strictly). (3) The early yes on the JOB read-back, never exercised live. (4) This address recovery and ending, never exercised live. (5) The name-collection review (5a-17) resolved or waived in writing. (6) The founder's written acceptance. A regression test exists for every defect whose cause was found.

**One combined live acceptance test (proposed; needs the founder's go-ahead and a restart so `HEAD` loads).** One call: let every ordinary question finish; say one early "Yes." about a third into the JOB read-back, stay silent through the full repeat, then a fresh "Yes."; at the ADDRESS question say an early "Yes." just before it finishes, stay silent while it is asked again (it begins with the wait instruction), let the replay finish, then "Yes.". **Pass:** exactly one `CONFIRM_HELD` (JOB, CUT) and one (ADDRESS, CUT), each with `acked_ms` below `sent_ms`; two `READBACK`, the second PLAYED with `acked_ms` equal to `sent_ms`; two `ADDRESS_READBACK`, the replay's `requestedChars` equal to the first plus 48 (consistent with the instruction, not proof of its words) and PLAYED with `acked_ms` equal to `sent_ms`; `ADDRESS_CONFIRMED`; no `BRIDGE_CLEAR` before the job read-back; confirmed, ready, handed off, address caller-confirmed, one contact stored, nothing missing; the owner entry "Confirmed by the caller" with no follow-up and no contact value; and no repetition other than the two intended. The exhausted ending is covered offline; a live check of it is optional and separate.

**Proposed first Intake to Processing/booking task (NOT authorized; no booking is implemented).** Connect the existing, already tested modules **in SIMULATION only**: pass the confirmed hand-off seam of the call logic to the existing Processing entry (a fixed plan of mock tools), allowed to act **only** on a record for which `eligibleForProcessing` is true with the encrypted-contact witness (so an unverified address can never proceed), using the existing SIMULATION pricing fixture as the first mock tool, and running the existing Intake to Processing to QC to Reporting pipeline against a **mock** calendar and the existing approval contracts, offline, with the live server unchanged. **Simulated booking** (mock availability, a mock calendar, SIMULATION pricing, nothing sent to anyone) is distinct from a **real customer-software connection** (`DRF-02`: per-company profiles, credentials, approvals, real writes), which is not started and needs the founder's separate approval. First step of that task: a read-only design of the seam wiring and its tests, no code.

## 5a-32. M1 accepted for progression (founder decision) and the SIMULATED booking path connected (2026-10-29/30; offline, simulation only; nothing live changed)

**Founder decision (2026-10-29).** M1 is **accepted for progression** using the existing call evidence; **no more Intake acceptance calls**. This is an acceptance for progression, **not** a statement that the M1 criteria were met: the counts stay as ruled in 5a-31 (qualifying clean passes in total 1; actual consecutive streak 0), and every earlier record stays as written. The items below were never exercised or are unresolved; they are recorded as **accepted limitations, not passed tests**.

**Accepted limitations (not passed).** (1) The early yes on the JOB read-back was never exercised live. (2) The address recovery (the wait instruction) and the exhausted-address ending were only tested offline, never live; the running server (PID 22132, build `a9b326c`) does not load them. (3) The name-collection review (5a-17) is unresolved. (4) A caller cannot withdraw an accepted answer. (5) The older substring "don't know" rule remains. (6) Interrupted asks are bounded only by the turn and time caps. (7) Several timing-sensitive tests have failed once each and then passed (realtime-bridge interruption, voice-service full call, call-diagnostics socket size; the full-call test did so again once during this task's first full run, passed on the rerun and alone): the cause is not proven. (8) The unexplained AMBIGUOUS bedrooms verdict (PID 15304), the PID 50320 failure cause and the "Health" transcription are unexplained. (9) The fixed listen-line proposal is not implemented. (10) The voice-character, silence-reprompt and GPT-Live items (`DRF-07`, `DRF-08`, `DRF-09`) stay deferred; GPT-Live stays paused.

**What is now connected (built; tested offline; NOT verified live).** `src/simulated-booking.ts` composes the existing modules, unchanged, into: **saved confirmed Intake record -> `eligibleForProcessing` gate (with the address witness) -> Processing plan [`mock-quote`, `mock-availability`, `mock-booking`] -> QC -> Reporting**, then a saved **booking outcome**.
- **Seam.** `run(handoff, {tenant, callRef})` takes the original sealed hand-off (the object the `confirmedHandoff` seam supplies) and looks the saved record up by tenant and call. The record must exist, belong to the same item and tenant, and pass `eligibleForProcessing`; otherwise the outcome is `blocked` (`NO_RECORD`, `RECORD_MISMATCH`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE`) and **no tool, QC or Reporting step runs**. A copy or forged hand-off is refused by Processing (it accepts only the sealed original). Every tool repeats the eligibility check on its own.
- **Tools** receive only `{step_id, item_id, workspace_id}` and read the saved, masked record. `mock-quote`: the existing SIMULATION pricing fixture; anything it does not price (bedrooms or bathrooms over the limit, extras, not a first clean, unknown answers) is "not performed", so a person must price it. `mock-availability`: a mock calendar matched on the exact Preferred date and Time window answers (no guessing; an unknown phrase is "not performed"); a taken slot is not offered. `mock-booking`: **human-approved and idempotent** on the step id (the existing approval store, approver contexts and QC's read-only approval checks); one booking per item and per slot.
- **Outcome** (`booked`, `awaiting_approval`, `not_booked`, `not_verified`, `blocked`, `failed`) is derived from the pipeline result and is `booked` **only** when QC's verdict is pass and the mock calendar holds the booking QC verified; a tool failure (unknown result) is `not_verified` with an escalation, never `booked`.
- **Tests** (`tests/simulated-booking.test.ts`, 6, registered in `npm test`): one correct booking (waits for approval, QC pass, Reporting counts it once, outcome/calendar/report agree, no answer values in the outcome); a retry books nothing more (calendar called once, Reporting counts repeats); blocked inputs (unverified address, unconfirmed request, held for a person, no witness, vault unconfirmed, witness error, no record, mismatched item, forged hand-off) never reach a tool or Reporting; unpriced or unschedulable requests and a taken slot stop before the approval step; a throwing calendar is escalated and never reported as booked, a declining calendar is reworked; the module has no network, file, process or contact-vault access. Synthetic data only; the witness is a test stub; **no contact key is read**. Removing the gate, or the human-approval flag, makes tests fail (checked).
- **Guard edit (disclosed).** Two existing inventory guards were adjusted without weakening their intent: the approval-store guard is satisfied by deriving the store type from Processing's own configuration (no guard change); the QC inventory guard "one place says which tools need a human" now checks that the function is **defined** in one place (`processing-entry.ts`) instead of mentioning it nowhere else, so a composition root may call it but never redefine it.
- **Full suite:** 1710 tests, 1708 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo (the old masker gaps).

**Still simulated (nothing real).** The calendar and its slots, the pricing fixture (invented numbers), the in-memory approval store, the in-memory saved outcome, the owner's approval (a test approver), the address witness (a stub; production would use the encrypted contact). **Not connected:** the running server does not call this path (the `confirmedHandoff` seam is not wired there), the outcome is not shown on the dashboard or saved to disk, no message or notification is sent, and the live server (PID 22132) and the prototype dashboard are untouched.

**Next requirement for a REAL scheduling integration (`DRF-02`, not started, needs the founder's separate approval).** Per-company profiles and a real calendar adapter with credentials kept outside the repository and idempotency keys; a real date and time-window understanding instead of exact phrases; a price the business has approved (not a fixture); a human approval surface for the booking and a persisted outcome store with retention; wiring the seam in the server (and the production witness from the contact vault) behind an explicit decision; customer notification only with separate approval. Until then every booking is a simulation.

## 5a-34. Shorter, friendlier wording for Drew's existing questions (founder-approved 2026-10-30; wording only; tested offline; NOT loaded by the running server)

**What changed (17 spoken lines; question order, answer meaning, validation and intake state unchanged).**

| Line | Before | After |

| Residential question | {business} cleans houses, apartments, condos and townhouses. We don't offer commercial cleaning. Is your request for a residential property? | Is this cleaning for a home? (the founder's exact words) |
