## 5a-35. City and ZIP code in Drew's private service-address flow (2026-10-30; code and offline tests; NOT loaded by the running server; NOT tested live)

**Where it sits (unchanged).** The service address is still collected with the other contact items, still held only in memory and the encrypted contact vault, and still read back for a yes **after the job is confirmed**. What changed is what makes it whole: with the setting on (`addressCityZip`, enabled for Drew's voice service and only when the contact vault is on) an address is complete only with a **street address, a city and a five-digit ZIP code**. The older ConversationRelay phone service and any call without the vault are unchanged (the setting is off there).

**Behaviour.**
- The first question is "What is the service address, including the city and ZIP code?" (the retry is "Sorry, I missed that." in front of the same question).
- Parts are **kept**: only what is missing is asked for, in this order: the street pieces (the existing street name / house number follow-ups), then "Thank you. What is the city?", then "Thank you. What is the five-digit ZIP code?". The street address is said FIRST; the city and ZIP code may then be said together with it, in either order, or across answers (a city or ZIP code said before any street is not reliably handled: see the correction below); "the city is ..." and "the zip code is ..." replace only that part; a bare word never overwrites a held city.
- A ZIP code is five digits, not all zero, read from the **end** of a line only (a house number or a unit number is never a ZIP code); ZIP+4 is cut to its first five; four or six digits, all zeros or words are **not taken** and the ZIP code is asked again with the fixed reason `BAD_ZIP`.
- The **whole address is read back** for the yes (the ZIP code digit by digit: "1427 Sample Street, Springfield, 9 1 3 6 4"); a no clears all three parts and the existing correction path asks again, naming only what is still missing.
- **Bounded**: the ZIP code (like any item) is asked at most three times before the call ends as a safe incomplete outcome; follow-ups after a no are capped (six in this mode, three before). The measured-confirmation rule, the early-yes hold, the wait instruction and the exhausted-address ending are untouched.
- **Never a postal check and never a service-area check**: the caller confirms the address aloud; `address_status` keeps its meaning.

**Privacy (checked by tests with canary values).** The city, ZIP code and street are raw personal data like the rest of the address: they are taught to the masker so a model never reads them, spoken only to the caller, stored only in the encrypted vault, and never appear in a log, a saved record or the dashboard; logs carry fixed reason codes only (`NEED_CITY`, `NEED_ZIP`, `BAD_ZIP`).

**Wording vs logic.** Three new fixed lines (the city and ZIP follow-ups and the street-address follow-up) and the new opener are wording; the parsing, the part tracking, the whole-address read-back and the setting are logic. Consent, read-back and recovery wording is unchanged.

**Known limits (accepted, not hidden).** A city said before any street ("Springfield 91364" as the first answer) is taken for a street name, so the house number is asked next; a bare number with no street yet is a house number, not a ZIP code; a city that is also a common word may be masked in later lines; the city is not checked against the ZIP code or anywhere else.

**Correction (2026-10-30, documentation and test title only; behaviour and evidence unchanged).** This section, the commit message of `140f6b0` and the test title first said an address may be said "in any order". That overstated what was built and tested. What is supported and tested: a whole address in one answer; the street address first, then the city and the ZIP code in either order or together with it. What is NOT supported: a city or a ZIP code said before any street. Evidence kept: a probe showed "Springfield" then "1427 Sample Street" ends with the city asked again (the first word was held as a street name and discarded when the street arrived), and "Springfield 91364" as a first answer is taken as a street name plus a ZIP code, so the house number is asked next; a lone five-digit number with no street yet is a house number. The read-back shows the whole address, so a wrong reading is not confirmed silently, and the calls stay bounded. No repair is made or planned here; the commit message of `140f6b0` is history and is not rewritten.

**Tests.** `tests/address-city-zip.test.ts` (12: whole and split answers, street first and then the city and ZIP code in either order, spoken ZIP digits, ZIP+4, invalid ZIP codes, house and unit numbers, kept parts, corrections, city rules, spoken form, default off, tool surface, masker privacy, wording); eight whole-call tests in `tests/founder-call.test.ts` (whole answer, split answers, ZIP-only or city-only missing, an invalid ZIP asked again then accepted, a never-valid ZIP ending incomplete, a wrong address corrected, a city corrected mid-collection, a repeated no ending unconfirmed) with no address value in any log or record. Removing the setting or weakening the ZIP rule makes them fail (checked). Existing address and confirmation tests run unchanged with the setting off. Full suite: see the commit.

**Still pending (not started).** Service-area checks, homeowner or renter and company authorization rules, acknowledgements, the speaker adapter and voice integration (`DRF-10`), GPT-Live, and a live check of this flow. Booking stays paused at `2f23a51`. The running server (PID 22132, build `a9b326c`) does not load any of this until the founder restarts it.

## 5a-36. CI is RED on `fbb2a6e`: two separate test failures, PARKED with their evidence (2026-10-30; nothing fixed, nothing rerun to get green)

**CI history, stated plainly (updated 2026-10-31): RED on `fbb2a6e` (Actions run #37) and on `b444df8` (run #38); GREEN on `d1bee6b` (the commit that fixed the race in the speaker adapter's own tests).** A green run does not explain or close failure 1 or failure 2 below: both stay OPEN, and green on one commit says nothing about the intermittent failures in the pattern note. The original report follows. **CI for `fbb2a6e` FAILED.** The earlier commits `2f23a51`, `866bea3` and `140f6b0` passed CI. `fbb2a6e` changed only documentation, two code comments and one test title, so it cannot have changed the runtime of either failing test. The two failures are different tests and are tracked separately.

**1. The CI failure (GitHub Actions, Linux runner).** Source: the run page's annotations (the full log needs a GitHub sign-in).
- Test: `tests/founder-voice-service.test.ts:146`, "full call: the notice, a yes, the cleaning questions, the fixed read-back and a saved confirmed record, all over the stream, with the Drew persona on every spoken line" (299 ms).
- Assertion (line 182): `assert.deepEqual(r.voices[0].refused, [], 'nothing the documented schema would refuse was sent')`; actual `['response.cancel of a reply that is not playing']`, expected `[]`.
- The message comes from the test voice stand-in (`tests/helpers/realtime-wire-sim.ts:158`), which refuses a cancel whose reply is not the one it is playing.

**2. The separate local failure (same tree, a full local run).** `tests/call-diagnostics.test.ts:189`, "the default limit accepts audio pieces far larger than the old one (256 KB)", assertion "a large piece goes through": `2 !== 3`. In that local run the CI-failing test above passed.

**Investigation of failure 1 (read-only, bounded).** Code reading: in the scripted full call the transport sends `response.cancel` only on caller speech while a reply is current, or for a reply already marked to cancel; the simulator refuses it when it has already finished that reply; the transport deliberately tolerates exactly that error (`realtime-transport.ts` ~392) and `tests/realtime-schema.test.ts:83` tests the tolerance. A window exists between the simulator clearing its play state and the transport hearing `response.done`, but by the test's own ordering (`say()` waits for the spoken count, and queue order puts `response.done` before the next caller event) it should not be reachable. **The ordering that happened in CI is NOT established.** A bounded reproduction (10 attempts of that test under CPU load, with test-only tracing in a scratch copy, since deleted) passed 10 of 10 and captured no refusal. **Not demonstrated: that the transport or the simulator is wrong; not demonstrated: that it is a flake.** The same tests (this one, and the call-diagnostics large-piece test) have each failed once before in this project and passed on a rerun; no cause was found.

**Proposed fix (test-only, not applied):** keep a short ordering trace in the simulator (event types, reply ids, play state; no caller values) and attach it to the `refused` assertion so the next failure shows the exact ordering; decide after that.

**Later evidence (2026-10-30/31), same class, still unexplained.** (a) CI run #38 on `b444df8` (the speaker adapter commit) FAILED, but on three NEW whole-service tests of that commit (`founder-voice-service.test.ts`: "whole call" `3 !== 5`, "interruption" timed out waiting for a phone clear, "speaker failure" saved `confirmation: 'unconfirmed'`), not on the `full call` test of failure 1. Cause found and fixed in the tests: they waited for the stand-in source to finish sending (faster than the phone plays), so on a slower runner a yes could arrive before the read-back was acknowledged, and one wait counted the notice's audio as the opening's. They now wait for the transport's playback verdict; three attempts under CPU load passed. (b) A later full local run failed two OTHER existing tests, each of which passed when run alone: `founder-call.test.ts:3359` "DELAYED PREVIEW fragments that arrive while the preview is running cancel the stale question" (`0 !== 1`: no question asked) and `realtime-bridge.test.ts:305` "input: a damaged voice-service message is counted and handed on as unreadable" (`1 !== 2`). Neither file was touched by that change. A third full local run (with the ElevenLabs client tests added) failed two existing bridge tests again: `call-diagnostics.test.ts:77` "a healthy call logs ... the reply's end and its release" (release path `immediate`, expected `acked`: the phone's acknowledgements had already arrived when the reply ended, so the test's assumed ordering flipped) and `realtime-bridge.test.ts:305` (the same test as in (b), `1 !== 2`). Both passed alone three times in a row (56 of 56 each time, the two files together). The new tests (speaker adapter, ElevenLabs client) passed 23 of 23 in three attempts under CPU load, and the bridge code was not changed; the extra real-time tests may add load to a parallel run, which is a guess, not a finding. (c) **A DIFFERENT, now explained failure.** CI run #41 on `aee1a3f` (the inactive ElevenLabs client commit, which added files only) failed `playback-confirmation.test.ts:315` "CONFIRM address exhausted (record #28 reproduced)" at line 350, "no address in any log". Cause DEMONSTRATED: the assertion searched the serialised log entries for the regex `742|evergreen`, and each entry of that test's rig carries an epoch-millisecond timestamp (`at`); a timestamp containing the digits 742 matched. Reproduced locally (1 failure in 12 loaded runs); the match context was `"at":1793408834742`, a timestamp, not an address. It was a false positive in the test, NOT an address in a log. Test-only fix: the timestamp is left out of that search and the street name (or the number followed by the street) is what is searched for. Other tests search logs for the same `742` text (`founder-call.test.ts` lines near 2686, 3964, 3979 and `call-state.test.ts` 206); their log entries carry no timestamp, so they are not known to be affected, and they were not changed.

(d) **First LOCAL reproduction of failure 1 (2026-10-31).** In a full local run of 1768 tests (the speaker-switch commit), `founder-voice-service.test.ts:155` "full call: ..." failed with the SAME assertion as CI run #37: `refused` held `['response.cancel of a reply that is not playing']` instead of `[]`; every other test passed except the two known masker todos. It had passed in the runs before and after it, and in 10 of 10 isolated loaded runs earlier. So the failure is real and intermittent, it appears in a full parallel run (as on the CI runner) and not in isolation, and its cause is **still not established**: no trace was running, and nothing in the change under test touches the transport, the bridge or the simulator. The proposed test-only ordering trace (above, under "Proposed fix") would now be worth adding, because a failing run can be expected again.

**Pattern, not a cause (for failures 1 and 2 and the other intermittent tests above; (c) is explained):** several timing-sensitive existing tests fail intermittently in a full parallel run or on the CI runner and pass alone; the cause (machine load, an ordering assumption in the stand-ins, or both) is not proven for any of them.

**Not done on purpose:** no CI rerun to obtain a green mark, no change to production code or to the failing assertions. Both items stay open until an ordering or a cause is shown.

## 5a-37. OFFLINE speaker adapter for the ElevenLabs candidate (Jack, Flash v2.5, speed 0.95): built and tested against stand-ins only (2026-10-30; NOT wired into any service; NOT a live voice change)

**What exists.** `src/speaker-adapter.ts`: `withSpeaker(voiceSocket, speechSource, settings, options)`, a decorator on the voice socket the bridge already uses. OpenAI keeps LISTENING (caller audio, transcription, speech detection pass through unchanged); every fixed line the call logic asks the voice to say ("Say exactly the following words ...") is answered by the splice from a **speech source**, in the same events the voice service sends (created, mu-law audio pieces, the requested words as the transcript, done). **The bridge, the transport, the call logic and the intake state are unchanged**, so marks, measured playback, the clear on caller speech and the CUT / CONFIRM_HELD rules apply as before. Settings are validated before any call: voice `7EzWGsX10sAS4c9m9cPf`, model `eleven_flash_v2_5` (the only approved model), speed 0.95 (project bound 0.7 to 1.2; the provider's range is not on file), output `ulaw_8000`. **The current speaker path (OpenAI speaks) is untouched and nothing selects the adapter.** The only source it has ever run with is the test stand-in (`tests/helpers/speaker-stand-in.ts`); there is no ElevenLabs client, no network and no key anywhere.

**Behaviour proven (against the stand-ins).** (1) A line is spoken by the source with exactly the requested words and the candidate settings; OpenAI is never asked to speak. (2) **Cancellation:** a cancel stops the source request, answers `cancelled`, and every later piece (arriving, or already queued for delivery) is dropped and counted; a cancel of a reply that already ended gets the voice service's own `response_cancel_not_active` error with the request's event id. (3) **Measured playback through the real bridge:** `x_sent_ms` equals the source audio's length and `x_acked_ms` equals it once the phone has played it; the notice must be played before the caller's audio reaches OpenAI. (4) **Caller talks over a line:** the phone is cleared, the source cancelled, no piece reaches the phone afterwards, the played time is measured. (5) **Early yes:** a yes said while the read-back is playing is held (`CONFIRM_HELD`, acknowledged below sent) and never confirms; a fresh yes after the whole read-back does. (6) **Speaker failure is honest:** a source that cannot start, sends nothing, stalls, errors, is empty or sends bad or oversized audio ends the line as `failed` with no transcript; the call logic reports the line as not heard; the next line works; a source that always fails never produces a confirmed record. (7) **Unchanged intake state:** the same five-line call, spoken by the source, saves a record identical to the baseline call, and the same record results after an interrupted opening and after one failed line. (8) A reply event OpenAI sends anyway is dropped and counted. (9) Privacy: the splice stores and logs no text; diagnostics are fixed codes and numbers; a canary in a line reaches only the source; no network, file, process or key access in the module.

**Tests.** `tests/speaker-adapter.test.ts` (12: eight on the adapter alone, five behind the real bridge and transport) and four whole-service tests in `tests/founder-voice-service.test.ts` (the rig gained an opt-in `speaker` option; absent, it is unchanged). Removing the drop of queued audio, treating a source error as success, or letting OpenAI's reply events through makes them fail (checked); removing the second late-piece guard in the sink changes nothing (the drop at delivery covers it: a redundant guard).

**What remains UNVERIFIED (nothing below was tested).** How ElevenLabs actually behaves: its streaming protocol, latency, real chunk sizes, whether cancelling an in-flight request works as assumed, rate limits, errors; how Jack sounds at 8 kHz phone quality; **pronunciation of digits, house numbers and amounts** (the transcript check is bypassed because the words are exact by construction); whether Jack is available to the API and licensed for this use; cost; and the privacy position (the provider would receive the text of every spoken line, including read-backs with a name, number and address). The disclosure that names OpenAI as the speech provider would need to name the new provider too. **A finding about the existing call logic, not about the adapter:** after a line the caller did not hear (`REPLY_NOT_PLAYED`) the call logic does not speak again by itself; it waits for the caller to speak (the silence reprompt, `DRF-08`, is not built), so a failed line on a real call can leave silence until the caller speaks or a timer ends the call. The same holds today when OpenAI's own reply fails.

**Next requirement for a live connection (each needs the founder's separate approval):** a real ElevenLabs streaming client behind `SpeechSource` (with its key handled outside the repository), a measured cancel and latency check, an audition of digits and numbers at 8 kHz, the disclosure wording, a selection switch in the voice service's composition root (default stays OpenAI), and a restart for the code to load. Booking stays paused at `2f23a51`; GPT-Live stays paused.

## 5a-38. INACTIVE ElevenLabs streaming client behind the speaker adapter (2026-10-31; tested against a mock server only; NOT wired; no key; NOT a live voice change)

**What exists.** `src/elevenlabs-speech-source.ts`: `createElevenLabsSpeechSource({ getKey, fetch, ... })` returns a `SpeechSource` (the seam of 5a-37). For each line it makes ONE request, no retry: `POST https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/text-to-speech/7EzWGsX10sAS4c9m9cPf/stream?output_format=ulaw_8000`, the key only in the `xi-api-key` header, a JSON body with the exact words, `model_id` `eleven_flash_v2_5` and voice settings `{ stability 0.5, similarity_boost 0.75, style 0, use_speaker_boost true, speed 0.95 }` (the provider's documented defaults, which the founder's audition used, plus the chosen speed). The endpoint, query and body fields follow the provider's reference page read on 2026-10-31. The HTTP client is INJECTED (nothing in the file opens a connection), the key is asked for when a line starts and never at setup, and only the approved candidate settings are accepted (anything else throws before a request). **Nothing imports it, it reads no environment variable, and it holds no key.** The current speaker path (OpenAI speaks) names neither it nor the adapter.

**Behaviour proven against the mock server (loopback, no key, no outside call).** (1) The exact request: method, path, only `output_format=ulaw_8000` in the query, body fields, the key in the header and nowhere else. (2) **Audio format and order:** every byte the server streams reaches the sink in order, in as many pieces as arrive, then exactly one end; through the adapter and the real bridge the provider's bytes become the line's audio, `x_sent_ms` and `x_acked_ms` are the audio's length (one second for 8,000 bytes), and OpenAI is never asked to speak. (3) **Cancellation:** a cancel aborts the request (the server sees the connection drop); nothing reaches the sink afterwards; a second cancel does nothing. (4) **Late audio:** a stream that ignores the abort and keeps delivering is dropped after a cancel, after a failure and after the byte limit (the third piece that breaks the limit is not delivered). (5) **Timeouts:** no headers within the bound fails the line once and drops the request; headers then silence (`NO_AUDIO`) or some audio then silence (`STALLED`) are the adapter's bounds, and each cancels the provider request. (6) **Provider errors:** 400, 401, 403, 404, 422, 429, 500, 502 and 503 each fail the line once with no audio, recording only the status; a success that is not audio (JSON, HTML), a reset before the headers, a drop mid-stream (the delivered pieces stay delivered, one fail, no end), an empty body (the adapter fails the line `EMPTY`), a refused connection and an over-long stream all fail honestly. The provider's error text and the key never reach a diagnostic or an error (the mock's error bodies begin with canary strings). (7) No key: the line cannot start, with a fixed message, no request, and the adapter reports a failed start.

**Tests.** `tests/elevenlabs-speech-source.test.ts` (11). Putting the key in the URL, dropping the late-audio guard, removing the abort, or reading the provider's error body into a diagnostic makes them fail (checked).

**Unverified (the mock is my model of the provider).** The real content type of the audio and its chunk framing; that aborting a request stops the provider's work; what 401, 429 and 5xx actually return; that every voice setting may be sent together; latency to the first byte; Jack's availability to this account and license; cost; and pronunciation of digits, house numbers and amounts. The privacy position stands as in 5a-37 (the provider would receive the text of every spoken line). The provider's `enable_logging` default is on and zero retention is documented as enterprise-only; the client does not change it.

**Still needed before any live use (each a separate approval):** a switch in the voice service's composition root that selects the adapter and this client (default stays OpenAI); the key from the secret store; a first guarded live check of latency, cancellation and the content type; an audition of digits at 8 kHz through this exact path; the disclosure wording; a restart for the code to load. Booking stays paused at `2f23a51`; GPT-Live stays paused. The two parked CI investigations (5a-36) remain open and unresolved.

## 5a-40. First-test FALLBACK and bounded call ending for the ElevenLabs speaker (2026-10-31; code and offline tests; NOT enabled; supersedes the failure behaviour in 5a-37 to 5a-39)

**The rule (founder-defined for the first test).** OpenAI may take over a line **only if zero Jack audio has been handed on**. There is **no hold buffer and no estimate of playout time**. (1) The source fails with zero audio handed on (it cannot start, no key, an HTTP error or non-audio answer, no first audio in time, a first piece that is unusable, an empty body, or a line too long for the adapter): OpenAI is asked to speak the **same line**, and its events are relayed to the bridge under the **same reply id**, so the call logic sees one reply. The failed source request is stopped, and anything the source still delivers is dropped. The next line goes to the source again. (2) The source fails **after any audio was handed on**, or the **fallback fails** (OpenAI cannot be asked, errors, sends no audio in time, stalls, or completes empty): the line is answered `failed` with **no transcript** (so it never counts as heard), the call logic is told (`speaker_down`), and the call **ends within a defined time**.

**Ending the call.** The adapter sends `x_speaker_down` to the bridge; the transport turns it into a `speaker_down` event; the call logic logs `SPEAKER_DOWN`, marks the call incomplete, **saves what was collected as an INCOMPLETE and UNCONFIRMED record (never handed on)**, and hangs up by the existing path. `endDelayMs` (default 300 ms) after the failure the adapter also closes the voice socket itself as a backstop, so the call ends even if nothing else does. **Bounds (defaults, tunable, latency unmeasured):** source first audio 4000 ms, gap 2500 ms; fallback first audio 4000 ms, gap 2500 ms, total 30000 ms; end delay 300 ms. When both voices are down nothing can explain it aloud: the caller hears the line close, and the dashboard shows an incomplete, unconfirmed call.

**Protections kept (all tested).** Consent: the notice falls back too, the caller's audio stays closed to OpenAI until the OpenAI-spoken notice is fully acknowledged, and a notice that cannot be spoken ends the call. Measured confirmation: a fallback line is one reply measured by the real bridge, so an early yes during it is held (`CONFIRM_HELD`) and only a fresh yes after the whole line confirms; a fallback line carries OpenAI's own transcript, so the right-words check applies to it. Cancellation: a cancel while the source works never starts the fallback; a cancel after OpenAI was asked is applied to OpenAI's reply (remembered until OpenAI names it) and its late audio is dropped and counted. Late audio: pieces the failed source still delivers are dropped. Unexpected OpenAI reply events are still dropped.

**Tests.** `tests/speaker-adapter.test.ts` (18; fallback cases, no fallback after audio, fallback failure classes, cancellation during the switch, late audio, notice fallback and a fallback line played and cut behind the real bridge, the call ending behind the bridge), `tests/elevenlabs-speech-source.test.ts` and `tests/founder-voice-speaker.test.ts` updated to the new behaviour, and five whole-service tests (fallback leaves the baseline record; failure after audio saves an incomplete unconfirmed record and ends the call; a failed fallback does the same; an early yes during a fallback read-back is held; the consent notice falls back). Mutation-checked (fallback after audio, no `speaker_down`, no drop of queued audio, completing a fallback with no audio, no orphan cancel, no incomplete mark).

**Known limits, not hidden.** With a provider that hangs without answering, each line waits for the source's first-audio bound before OpenAI speaks it (no per-call stickiness was added); a line cut off by a failure after audio started is not repeated (the call ends instead); latency, real provider behaviour and pronunciation are unverified as before. Nothing is enabled: no private-config change, no key, no restart. Booking stays paused at `2f23a51`; GPT-Live stays paused; the parked CI investigations (5a-36) remain open.

## 5a-41. First live activation attempts for the Jack test: call evidence, the current blocker and test coverage (2026-10-31 to 2026-11-01; records only, no code change)

**What the live attempts showed (each from the provider's call log, the tunnel log and the server log of that attempt; nothing here is from the local test below).**
1. **Dead quick tunnel.** The call ended with no answer after 0 seconds and Twilio error 11100 (invalid URL): the tunnel's address no longer resolved. Cause: a quick tunnel dies when its connection is lost, and a new start gets a new random address.
2. **New address, server not listening.** The TwiML Bin was fetched (HTTP 200) and Twilio then reported error 31920 (stream WebSocket handshake error); the tunnel log showed the origin refusing the connection. Cause: no voice server was running at that moment.
3. **Server listening, wrong Twilio token in the server window.** The server logged `WS_HANDSHAKE_REFUSED` with status 403 (twice, on two attempts). A 403 means the path and headers were fine and only the signature check failed. A read-only check of the token the running server held (it reads only that one variable from the server process and makes one read-only request to Twilio for the account; the value is never printed) returned **rejected (401)**, and the value was not 32 characters. After the token was re-entered correctly the same check returned **success**. The earlier check of the token typed in a different window proved nothing about the server's token.
4. **Tunnel process ended.** The tunnel log shows a normal termination signal; no cloudflared process remained. A new quick tunnel was started detached from any PowerShell window, the private config's `public_wss_url` host and the TwiML Bin Stream URL were updated to it (secret path unchanged, config backed up first), and the server was restarted so its accepted signature URLs follow the new address. Verified afterwards by hash comparison: the saved Stream URL equals the config's, the rest of the TwiML is unchanged, the tunnel reached the server, the server's token was accepted.
5. **Latest call (the first to get past the handshake).** The server logged `CALL_STREAM_ACCEPTED`, then 4 ms later `VOICE_KEY_MISSING`, then the call closed (0 audio, about 20 ms after accept). **This is the live proof that Twilio's real signature, the saved address, the tunnel and the token all work together.** It is also the current blocker.

**Current blocker (proven).** Drew's voice socket accepts an OpenAI key only if it matches `^[A-Za-z0-9_.-]{8,300}$`; anything else is treated as "no key" and the call hangs up at once. The running server held a **non-empty** OpenAI key (presence only; its value was not read), so the key has a shape that fails that rule (the likely causes are stray whitespace, a line break or quotes from pasting; **unproven**, because the value was deliberately not read). The start gate did not catch it because it only checks `typeof string` and length of at least 8. **Smallest fix (founder-run, not done by me):** re-enter the key in the server window as clean plain text with `set-voice-keys.ps1` (a private-folder helper that prompts privately, applies Drew's rule to the OpenAI key and the same header-safe rule to the ElevenLabs key, contacts no provider and prints only pass or fail), then restart through the start helper and re-verify. No call until the read-only checks pass.

**Local tests versus live results (kept apart on purpose).** A local test with a throwaway server on a loopback port, a dummy token and synthetic values showed that the server accepts a handshake signed over the wss address, its https form, or either with a trailing slash, and refuses a different host, a different token, no signature (403) and a wrong path (404). A second local test with synthetic key-shaped values showed which shapes make the voice socket report `VOICE_KEY_MISSING` (trailing or leading space, line break, quotes, an internal space, shorter than 8 or longer than 300, empty or unset) and that a plain key-shaped value passes. **These are reproductions with fake secrets; they do not show what Twilio signed or what key the server held.** The live facts are items 1 to 5 above.

**Existing regression coverage, and gaps (nothing was added or weakened).** Covered: `tests/ws-server.test.ts` (signature as HMAC-SHA1 of the URL for either form and refusal of anything else; a valid signed handshake accepted; wrong or missing signature 403, wrong path 404, bad key or version 400; signature forms with and without a trailing slash and with a port; the lockout), `tests/founder-call-gate.test.ts` (the exact signed-URL candidates and that the entry hands them to the server), `tests/founder-voice-service.test.ts` (the whole service behind the real relay server using those candidates), `tests/openai-voice-socket.test.ts` (an unset, empty, short, space-containing, line-break, quote-containing, 301-character, non-string or throwing key makes no connection and reports `VOICE_KEY_MISSING` then `VOICE_CLOSED` once) and `tests/call-diagnostics.test.ts` (the voice side closing makes the bridge end the call). **Gaps, reported not fixed:** (1) the start checks in `founder-voice-entry.ts` (Twilio token, OpenAI key and ElevenLabs key present when a string of at least 8 characters) are weaker than the call-time rule, and the gate tests feed that gate plain booleans, so nothing tests that a key the call would reject is caught at start; (2) the ElevenLabs client enforces only "not empty", so there is no test or rule for an ElevenLabs key whose shape would break the request header; (3) the signature tests compute the expected value with the same HMAC code as the implementation, so there is no independent vector from the provider's documentation in the suite (the live `CALL_STREAM_ACCEPTED` above is the independent evidence, and it is a log line, not a test); (4) the signed URLs are fixed when the server starts, so a config host change needs a restart, which is documented but untested; (5) a dying quick tunnel is an operations fact with no test. Aligning the start gate with the call-time rule is a possible later change; it is **not** built and needs its own approval.

**Preserved.** Booking stays paused at `2f23a51`; GPT-Live stays paused; wording and city/ZIP checkpoints (`866bea3`, `140f6b0`) and the Jack preference (`8c69764`) are untouched; the working private configs hold their last verified values (the call config's address and the voice config's speaker block as approved; backups taken before each change). No restart, provider request or call was made by this record. The parked CI investigations (5a-36) remain open.

## 5a-42. First completed Jack test call: evidence, the founder's observations and the approved conversation goals (2026-11-01; records only, no code, config or call)

**What the log of the first completed ElevenLabs/Jack call showed (privacy-safe log, no personal text).** The blocker in 5a-41 is cleared: the keys were re-entered and passed the format checks, the running server held a Twilio token Twilio accepted, and the next call completed. All 20 spoken lines, the consent notice included, were spoken by ElevenLabs/Jack (`via SOURCE`); there was no fallback, no speaker failure and no provider error; every response was acknowledged by the phone. Start-to-first-audio was 202 ms at the least, 232 ms median and 484 ms at the most (the long notice); the OpenAI voice connection took 1219 ms. The consent notice built from the live config is 509 characters (equal to the logged length), contains the approved disclosure word for word and names both providers, and was played in full before any caller audio was released. Measured confirmation behaved as designed: the job read-back (378 characters) and the address read-back played in full, no early yes was held, the confirmed record was saved and the call ended after its closing line.

**The founder's observations from that call (recorded as said; not all proven by the log).**
1. **The property question was asked twice.** The log is consistent: the first answer was free text, the property-type guard held it, and one clarification was asked after the bedroom question (the single allowed re-ask; no third ask). Whether either answer should have counted as a supported choice cannot be judged from a log without the words.
2. **City and ZIP follow-ups.** The log shows the first address answer needing a city, two more pieces arriving while the system waited (classed not usable and a bad ZIP), then one city question and one ZIP question. The founder's observation is that a correctly said city or ZIP was asked for again; the log is consistent with the later pieces being dropped, but the cause is **not established** and nothing here proves the caller's city and ZIP were captured correctly.
3. **Unverified, said plainly:** whether the recorded **phone number and address match what the caller said** (the log shows a 10-digit phone captured and a joined address, and contacts are stored encrypted, so a match was not checked), and the **audible wording and pronunciation** of Jack's lines (the log's transcript length equals the characters requested, not an independent transcription of what was spoken).

**Conversation goals approved by the founder (a record of intent: nothing is designed or built, and each needs its own approval before any change).**
- **Occasional natural acknowledgements:** a short acknowledgement now and then, so the call sounds less mechanical, never on every turn.
- **Calmer turn-taking and interruptions:** calmer handling of pauses, overlap and the caller talking over a line.
- **Retain a correctly captured city and ZIP:** once the city or ZIP has been captured correctly, keep it and ask only for what is still missing, instead of asking again.

**Protections that any future change must preserve (unchanged today).** Confirmation is measured on what the phone actually played; an early yes during a read-back is held and never confirms; the consent notice must be fully played and acknowledged before the caller's audio reaches OpenAI; nothing unconfirmed is handed on; caller details are kept encrypted at rest and never appear in logs, diagnostics, the repository or this record; the fallback rule (OpenAI speaks a line only if zero Jack audio was handed on) and the bounded call ending stay. An acknowledgement must not count as a confirmation, must not repeat caller details aloud, and must not lengthen or shorten a measured read-back or a fixed line. Retaining city and ZIP must keep the readback and address confirmation exactly as they are.

**Not done here.** No code, config, restart, provider request or call. The earlier gaps in 5a-41 (start gate weaker than the call-time key rule, no ElevenLabs key rule, no independent signature vector) are unchanged. Booking stays paused at `2f23a51`; GPT-Live stays paused; checkpoints `866bea3`, `140f6b0` and `8c69764` are untouched; the parked CI investigations (5a-36) remain open.

## 5a-43. The repeated property question: reproduced through the real handler and fixed (2026-11-01; code and offline tests; no private config, restart, provider request or call)

**Reproduced (not only mirrored).** Through the real relay handler (the `founder-call.test.ts` property rig with its extractor stand-in), a caller who said "my home" in the request and then answered the property question with "It's a house." was asked the one clarification (`JOB_PROPERTY_TYPE_CLARIFY`) when the extraction returned the grounded non-choice word "home". The classifier was not at fault: it classes "It's a house." as a clean choice in every spelling tried. **Cause (proven offline):** `guardProperty` trusted only the extraction. When the extraction was a non-choice string it asked the clarification even though the caller's own answer to the question was one unambiguous configured choice. **Not proven for the live call:** what the live extraction returned (logs hold counts only, by design), and why the question came after the bedroom answer; the live call is consistent with this cause, not shown to be caused by it.

**Fix (smallest).** In `src/relay-handler.ts` `guardProperty`, a new `clearAnswer` rule: nothing is asked when (a) the clarification has not been asked, (b) the extraction is a value that is not a configured choice, (c) the caller's own answer to the question classifies as exactly one choice, and (d) **no later line mentions any configured choice, affirmed or negated** (so no negation, second choice or contradiction can be hiding). The value is then the caller's own words, as on the clarification path. Because the server set the value, the sealed handoff is dropped as before: **the record is confirmed but held for a person and not handed off** (this is the existing protection, kept; it means a clear answer saves a spoken question but not an automatic handoff in this case, which is a trade-off for the founder to review).

**Preserved (all tested).** A negated answer ("not a house"), two choices ("house or apartment") and an unsupported answer ("Health") are still asked about once; a later line that mentions a choice ("it is not a house", "my sister has an apartment") still raises the one clarification; the one-clarification limit, human review for unresolved answers, the read-back, the address confirmation and measured playback, and the rule that no caller words appear in logs are untouched. The 18 existing and new property tests pass.

**Tests and checks.** Two new tests in `tests/founder-call.test.ts`: a clear answer is retained for the extractions "home" and "residential" (asked once, no clarification, read back and saved as the caller's words, held for a person, no caller words in the logs), and the clear-answer rule never overrides a doubt. Mutation-checked: with the rule switched off, with the later-mention protection removed, and with any answer line accepted instead of only a clear choice, the new tests fail. Full suite: 1780 tests, 1778 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo. **Unverified:** the behaviour on a real call with the live model (a call is needed), and the audible wording. Booking stays paused at `2f23a51`; GPT-Live stays paused; checkpoints `866bea3`, `140f6b0` and `8c69764` untouched; the parked CI investigations (5a-36) remain open. The server must be restarted by the founder for this code to load; none was done.

## 5a-45. The goal: ONE COMPLETE Drew test call (not merely a Jobber connection); the proposed flow and the standalone Jobber connector's status (2026-11-01; records only; the flow is PROPOSED and nothing in it is built unless it says BUILT)

**(SUPERSEDED by 5a-47 and 5a-51: the flow below was PROPOSED when written; it has since been built and tested offline. The statement "nothing built or wired" is out of date. Nothing in it has run live.)**

**The goal (founder, 2026-11-01).** One complete, honest Drew test call, from the first ring to two verified text messages, using only the founder's Jobber DEVELOPER TEST ACCOUNT and founder-approved test phone numbers. Connecting Jobber is a means, not the goal. Jobber (the founder's developer test account) is now the scheduling system under test; Housecall Pro was investigated first (no account was created, no key obtained) and stays an unexplored alternative; nothing was built for it.

**The proposed flow (PROPOSED; none of it is wired to the voice server).**
1. Consent notice, unchanged (fully played and acknowledged before the caller's audio reaches OpenAI). The incoming **caller ID is retained from the start of the call** as a callback-number candidate and kept for the whole call; it is never logged in full.
2. **Name.** 3. **Address, city and ZIP** (ask only for what is missing; keep a correctly captured city or ZIP). 4. **Preferred day and time.** 5. **Bedrooms and bathrooms.**
6. **Quote: a HARDCODED test price of $199 for exactly 2 bedrooms and 2 bathrooms** (a standard cleaning). Any other combination gets no number ("a person will confirm"); nothing is guessed, rounded or estimated. The $399 and $499 combinations and the $25 discount rule stay UNDECIDED and are not used; the simulated fixture ($199.99 / $174.99) still disagrees and is unchanged.
7. **A supported Jobber time:** only a time Jobber's own data shows as free is offered. If none is supported, the caller is told a person will confirm. Availability is never invented.
8. **Customer agreement** to the offered time and price (yes, no or call me back).
9. **Callback number:** the retained caller ID is read back (last four digits) and verified; if it is missing, blocked or anonymous, a number is asked for.
10. **Text permission:** asked and recorded as a yes or no only after the number is verified; no text is sent without it.
11. **Measured confirmation, unchanged:** a read-back of the details, confirmed only when the phone played all of it; an early yes is held and never confirms.
12. **Create the TEST booking in the Jobber Developer Test Account and verify it by a separate read-back** (existing human-approved, idempotent booking step; "booked" is claimed only after QC passes). If any step fails, the caller hears that the request is recorded and a person will confirm.
13. **Send matching booking details by text to the approved CUSTOMER test number and the approved OWNER test number, then verify BOTH deliveries** (delivery status from Twilio; a failure is reported, never assumed).
**Success for the complete call (all must be shown):** exactly one booking in the test account whose read-back matches what was confirmed; both texts delivered with details that match the booking; no duplicate booking or text on a retry; the consent, measured-confirmation and privacy protections intact; no caller detail, token or number in any log, record or document.

**Jobber connector, AS FIRST WRITTEN (superseded by 5a-46: it is now CONNECTED, read-only): LOCALLY TESTED, NOT CONNECTED.** Built as a standalone read-only connector (`src/jobber-connector.ts`, `src/jobber-dpapi.ts`, `src/jobber-connect-main.ts`, `tests/jobber-connector.test.ts`, and the entry `jobber-enter-credentials.ps1` in the founder's private folder outside the repository). It implements Jobber's documented OAuth 2.0 authorization-code flow with a random state (single use, 10 minutes) and PKCE (S256), a local-only callback listener, a form-encoded token exchange, refresh-token rotation (the new refresh token saved before use, one refresh at a time), queries only (a mutation or subscription is refused before anything is sent), Windows DPAPI storage of credentials and tokens outside the repository, and a check of the connected account by exact name (and pinned id) before any data is read. Tested against a local stand-in for Jobber with made-up secrets: 14 tests, mutation-checked five ways; one existing containment guard (the inventory of files that open a network listener) was updated to name the connector's callback listener. **Not connected:** no credentials were entered, no authorization was done and no Jobber API call has been made; the connector's files had not been committed when this was written, and nothing imports them (the voice server, call logic and the simulated booking checkpoint are unchanged). **Verified in Jobber's official docs:** the authorization and token flow, PKCE, rotation, the 60-minute access token, the GraphQL endpoint and version header (active version 2026-09-25 in the changelog), rate limits. **Unverified at that time (the callback question is answered in 5a-46: the field stays blank):** that Jobber accepts an http localhost callback URL, the scheduled-items schema and any availability query, whether job creation works in the test account, and the scopes needed to write.

**CORRECTED in 5a-46: the OAuth Callback URL field in the Jobber app stays BLANK** (Jobber said localhost is supported automatically and must not be listed); the connector itself sends `http://localhost:8765/jobber/callback`. The steps that followed were done: credentials entered, authorized, one read-only check run. Then, one step at a time: enter the credentials privately, authorize (the connector refuses to store tokens unless the account is the expected test account), and run one read-only account and schedule check (counts only).

**Remaining dependencies (none started unless stated).** (1) Write scopes and a re-authorization if creating a client and job in the test account is approved, plus a verified read-back path. (2) A rule for real availability, probably business hours minus Jobber's scheduled items, if Jobber exposes no availability query (unverified). (3) A narrow, audited way to read the encrypted contact (name, phone, address) for the booking; the booking tools are barred from it today (a privacy decision). (4) Passing the caller's number into the call (a TwiML Bin parameter; an untested Twilio change). (5) Twilio SMS: whether the Drew number can send texts, business-texting registration, the two approved test numbers, the consent and message wording, and a way to verify delivery; none verified. (6) The hardcoded quote rule. (7) Wiring the call logic's `confirmedHandoff` seam and the in-call availability check, with latency and failure handling. (8) The earlier priorities still open: the property-question fix is built and CI-passed but not yet loaded by the running server or live-tested; city/ZIP retention; acknowledgements; calmer turn-taking; read-back accuracy; a shorter test-only opening (requested, not drafted).

**Preserved.** Booking stays at the simulated checkpoint `2f23a51`; GPT-Live stays paused; checkpoints `866bea3`, `140f6b0` and `8c69764` untouched; the parked CI investigations (5a-36) remain open. Only the Developer Test Account and approved test numbers; no real customer; no secret in any record. No restart, booking, text or call was made for this record.

## 5a-46. The Jobber connector is CONNECTED (read-only) and a test-account-only booking adapter is built and tested locally; nothing was booked (2026-11-01; code and offline tests; no restart, text, call or booking)

**(Status as of 2026-11-01.)** Write scopes were later saved in the Jobber app and the founder re-authorized (founder report, not re-checked here). **Which scopes the stored Jobber token actually holds is UNVERIFIED** (the token was not inspected), and no write has ever run against Jobber: `jobber-booking-ledger.enc` does not exist in the private folder.

**Connected, read-only.** The founder's Jobber app was authorized through the connector (Microsoft Edge opened the approval page; the connector stored tokens only after the account name matched the expected Developer Test Account exactly, then pinned its id). Tokens and credentials are protected with Windows DPAPI outside the repository; no secret was printed or recorded. **The OAuth Callback URL field in the Jobber app stays BLANK:** Jobber said localhost is supported automatically and must not be listed (this corrects the instruction recorded in 5a-45); the connector itself sends `http://localhost:8765/jobber/callback` as the redirect address. **Launcher fix:** the first launch ran PowerShell with `-Command 'Start-Process $args[0]' <url>`, where the argument is appended to the command text instead of arriving in `$args`, so no tab opened and nothing failed; the new `src/jobber-browser.ts` starts `msedge.exe` directly with the link as one argument (clipboard fallback, fixed reason codes, the link never printed), with its own tests.

**The actual read-only API result.** The account check passed (expected name, pinned id). The schedule check read **0 scheduled items in the next 14 days** (the test account is empty); API version 2026-09-25 answered; query cost 154 of 9,993 available. **Schema facts read from the live API (introspection only):** `scheduledItems` takes a required filter `ScheduledItemsFilterAttributes` with `occursWithin: DateRange(startAt, endAt)`; items expose `startAt`, `endAt`, `duration`, `title` and come in six kinds (Assessment, Event, InvoiceReminder, QuoteReminder, Task, Visit). Write operations exist (`clientCreate`, `propertyCreate`, `jobCreate`, `visitCreate` and others); `jobCreate` needs a property id, an invoicing block, and takes `timeframe.startAt` (a date), `scheduling.startTime` and `endTime` (ISO 8601 clock times) and line items.

**The booking adapter (BUILT, tested locally, NEVER run against Jobber).** `src/jobber-booking.ts` plus a small change to `src/jobber-connector.ts`. **Read-only stays the default:** a write needs the context to switch writes on for exactly one expected test account, the entry point never does, and it offers no booking command. `mutate` can send only `clientCreate` and `jobCreate` (a mutation with an alias, a directive, a second operation or any other field is refused before anything is sent) and re-verifies the account (name and pinned id) before every write. **Gates:** the request must carry three literal approvals (a record that passed `eligibleForProcessing`, a person approved the booking, the caller agreed to the price and time) and strict patterns (names, a 10-digit number, a five-digit ZIP, a future same-day visit of at most eight hours, a price in whole cents). **Duplicate prevention:** a ledger of the key's progress (ids only, protected, written BEFORE each write), a marker in the job's title and instructions that Jobber is asked about before any new job, and a lock per key. A finished key is only re-verified; a half-finished one reuses its client and property; a key left pending with no job to be found is NOT retried (a person decides). **Independent read-back:** after the write the job is read by id through a separate query path and compared (client name and phone, address, marker and title, exactly one visit, its start and end as instants, the price, the job status); anything different ends as `uncertain` with the field NAMES only, never as a claimed booking. Jobber's own reminders, follow-ups and team notification are switched off and the test client's number is not SMS-enabled (the texts of this project are sent by Twilio later, on their own gate). No name, phone, address or token reaches a log, a result or the ledger. The simulated booking checkpoint (`2f23a51`, `simulated-booking.ts`) and the voice server are untouched and import none of this.

**Tests.** 10 new booking tests against a local stand-in for Jobber, plus the 14 connector tests; **mutation-checked 11 ways** (writes default on, allowlist ignored, account not re-checked before a write, ledger ignored, marker search skipped, pending key retried, read-back mismatch ignored, visit time not compared, approval gate not enforced, no lock per key, Jobber notifications on): each makes a test fail. Two first-round survivors exposed real test gaps (the write path's own account check; the ledger alone preventing a repeat) and were closed with new tests.

**UNVERIFIED (it needs the first approved test write).** Whether the account reads `startTime` and `endTime` in its own time zone (the read-back compares instants, so a different reading ends as `uncertain`); whether `jobs(searchTerm:)` finds the marker in a title; whether these exact inputs create a job in the Developer Test Account; whether the line item makes the job total equal the price; whether Clients and Jobs write scopes are enough (properties are created through the client). **Founder step pending:** change only Clients and Jobs to read and write (Scheduled Items stays read-only), save, and re-authorize; until then every write is refused by Jobber and by the connector.

**Not done and still true.** No Jobber booking, text, call, restart or commit; booking in the voice path stays the simulated checkpoint; the contact-vault read path, the real availability rule, the caller-ID parameter, Twilio SMS and the call-path wiring remain open (5a-45); the earlier conversation priorities are unchanged. Booking stays paused at the simulated checkpoint `2f23a51`; GPT-Live stays paused; checkpoints `866bea3`, `140f6b0` and `8c69764` are untouched; the parked CI investigations (5a-36) remain open.

## 5a-47. The complete fast Drew test call is BUILT and tested locally (offline; not loaded by the server, not enabled in any private config, no call, no Jobber booking, no text) (2026-11-02)

**(Parts superseded.)** The flow described here asked the caller to say yes first and sent two texts by default; 5a-48 removed the yes prompt, 5a-50 aligned the texts and consent to the submitted campaign, and 5a-51 made texts OFF by default. The list "Remaining before the live test" below is out of date: the fast-test config has been applied, the approved numbers saved, the SMS capability check run and the owner consent recorded (the setup status reports it; the evidence content was not inspected).

**What exists (all offline, all tested against stand-ins).** A short deterministic call flow with no language model in the loop (`src/fast-call-handler.ts`) on the existing carrier interface: the carrier's opening and the caller's yes, then name, address with city and ZIP (parts are kept; only the missing part is asked, once), day and time, bedrooms and bathrooms, **$199 for exactly two bedrooms and two bathrooms** (any other size is not booked), a time that Jobber's own calendar supports, agreement, the **verified caller ID as the callback number** (the caller is asked for a number only if it is declined), text permission, the measured read-back, the Jobber test booking with an independent read-back, then **two texts with matching details, each delivery checked**. Supporting modules: deterministic answer parsers (`fast-parsers.ts`), availability from the founder's stated hours and cleaning duration minus Jobber's scheduled items (`fast-availability.ts`, plus `readBusyIntervals` in the connector), Twilio helpers for the caller ID, texts to approved numbers only and delivery status (`twilio-messaging.ts`), the wiring (`founder-voice-fast.ts`, the optional `fast_test` section of the voice config in `founder-voice-gate.ts`, a switch in `founder-voice-service.ts` and the entry), private setup (`fast-test-setup-main.ts`, `fast-test-config.ts`, `fast-test-config-main.ts`), and `addressParts()` in `call-state.ts`.

**Protections kept, and how they were shown.** Measured confirmation: the quote and time, the callback number, text permission and the read-back are units that count only after the carrier's playback verdict says the whole line played; a yes that arrives early is held (`CONFIRM_HELD`), the unit is replayed after the approved instruction and only a fresh yes counts; a unit that is cut or unreported is replayed twice at most, then the call ends unconfirmed; a carrier that cannot measure playback is refused. The opening's yes is required: anything but a plain yes ends the call with nothing collected. Privacy: a name, address, number or text is held in memory for the call and cleared at its end, goes only to Jobber, Twilio and the caller's ear, and never reaches a log, an outcome or a record. Controlled test: only a caller whose verified caller ID is on the approved list gets past the opening (anyone else hears one line and nothing is collected); texts go only to the two approved numbers and the customer's only with the caller's permission; a booking needs the founder's standing test approval; the Jobber test account is re-verified by name and id before every write. No price, time or availability is invented: a time is offered only when the hours and duration are configured, the calendar was read completely and the time is free. **Tests:** 16 new handler tests, 3 joined tests (a stand-in for Twilio's stream, the real server and bridge with measured playback, the real handler, connector, booking adapter and Twilio helpers against local stand-ins for Jobber and Twilio), parser, availability, Twilio-helper and setup tests; mutation-checked 16 ways (each defect makes a test fail). Full suite 1845 tests, 1843 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo.

**(SUPERSEDED by 5a-48: the opening is now the founder's greeting and has no yes prompt.)** **The opening, and the disclosure question (reported separately).** For the controlled test the opening became, exactly: "Hello. This is the automated A I assistant for Drew Cleaning. This is a test call. Please say yes to continue, or hang up now." The long disclosure (it said speech is turned into text, that OpenAI hears and may keep it for up to thirty days, that ElevenLabs turns the replies into speech and may keep the words, that the call is not recorded, and that details are saved for testing) and the special are removed, at the founder's request, and the voice gate no longer requires the ElevenLabs-naming notice **only when the fast test section is present**. What still applies, not legal advice and for the founder and counsel to decide: the notice still says the caller is talking to an automated assistant; nothing may be collected without the caller's yes; the controlled test is limited to approved test callers with invented details. **Before any real customer, a disclosure covering the automated assistant, the processors that hear or speak, retention, and the text-message consent wording must be restored.** This was not applied to any private config.

**Test hours and cleaning duration (founder to supply; nothing is defaulted).** The flow needs the Jobber account's time zone, the open days and hours of the TEST, how long one standard cleaning takes, the two-letter state, and the standing approval of test bookings. Without hours and a duration nothing is ever offered and the call ends with "a person will call you". A private script (`fast-test-apply-config.ps1`, in the founder's private folder) asks for exactly these, shows the exact opening and the changes, backs up both configs and applies on a typed APPLY; it has only been dry-run on copies with sample answers.

**Text messages, and what is NOT verified.** The two texts are plain ASCII, one segment each: the customer's "{business}: your standard cleaning is booked for {Day Mon D at H:MM AM/PM}. Price $199. Reply STOP to opt out." and the owner's "{business} booking: {first name} {last initial}, {when}, {city}, 2bd/2ba, $199, job {number}." **Unverified:** whether the Drew number can send texts at all, and US business-texting registration. A read-only check was prepared (`fast-test-sms-check.ps1`, prompts for the token privately and asks Twilio only read-only questions) but not run: reading the token from the running server's memory was refused, and it was not worked around. The two approved test numbers are saved only through a private prompt (`fast-test-enter-numbers.ps1`, DPAPI-protected, never in the repository).

**Timing (an estimate, unmeasured).** The scripted lines come to about 860 characters, 47 to 56 seconds of speech at the pace measured on the first Jack call; with nine short caller turns and a few seconds for the calendar, the booking and the texts the happy path is about 1.2 to 1.9 minutes; a repeated question or a slow caller moves it toward the 2 to 3 minute target. Real latency of Jobber, Twilio, OpenAI and ElevenLabs is unmeasured.

**Remaining before the live test (all founder-gated).** (1) Apply the fast-test config (hours, duration, time zone, state, standing approval), which changes both digests; (2) save the two approved test numbers privately; (3) run the SMS capability and registration check, and register for business texting if needed; (4) re-authorize if the write scopes were changed, and set `JOBBER_ALLOW_WRITES=test-account` in the server window with the keys and token entered cleanly; (5) restart the server through the helper (the running one does not have this code); (6) a read-only dry run and one guarded call from the approved test number. Unverified until then: the real STT behaviour on these answers, the time-zone reading of Jobber's visit times, marker search in Jobber, that Twilio texts are delivered, and the audible result. Booking in the long flow stays the simulated checkpoint `2f23a51`; GPT-Live stays paused; checkpoints `866bea3`, `140f6b0`, `8c69764` and `c3c7a7b` are untouched; the parked CI investigations (5a-36) remain open. Nothing here is committed.

## 5a-48. The controlled Drew test opening is now the founder's greeting, with no yes prompt, and Jack answers honestly if asked whether he is an A I (offline; not loaded by the server, no call, no booking, no text, no restart) (2026-11-02)

**The exact opening the caller hears:** "Thanks for calling Drew Cleaning. I'm Jack. What's your name?" (straight apostrophes; nothing is added before or after it). It asks the first question itself. The earlier "Please say yes to continue" prompt and the consent step are **removed**: nothing is spoken after the caller verification, and the first thing the caller says is taken as the answer to the greeting's question (a bare "yes" or an off-topic line is not a name, and the name is asked for again; silence is reprompted once and then ends the call).

**Kept, unchanged and tested:** the approved-caller restriction (a caller not on the approved list hears one short line and nothing is read, booked or texted); measured confirmation (offer, callback number, text permission and read-back count only after the carrier's playback verdict; an early yes is held and the unit replayed after the approved instruction); texts only to the approved test numbers after a verified booking; no caller detail, line text or secret in diagnostics.

**Honesty.** At any step, including over a line that is still playing, a caller who asks whether this is a person, a robot, a bot, a computer or an A I is told: "Yes, I'm an A I assistant for Drew Cleaning, not a person. This is a test call." The question in progress is then asked again (a confirmation unit after the approved instruction), and the question never counts as a yes, a no or an answer. Names such as "Bot Johnson" or "Robin Lee" are not mistaken for the question. No scripted line claims to be a person.

**Config.** `opening.greeting` is a new optional key (10 to 200 plain characters, validated). `consentNoticeFor` returns it verbatim; `twimlFor` refuses it (the voice route only). The voice gate allows it **only together with the `fast_test` section**; without `fast_test` it is refused. The fast-test config helper writes exactly this greeting (no disclosure, no special, no agent name field) and its dry run prints the exact opening and the honesty behaviour. Both private configs are part of the approval digests, so the start helper recomputes them after the helper is applied (not yet applied).

**Disclosure caveat (not legal advice).** The greeting itself does not say that Jack is an A I; it is said truthfully on request. This is acceptable only for the founder-run test with approved callers. Some places require a disclosure before or at the start of an automated call and require recording or retention notices; before any real customer, restore the full disclosure (A I, speech processing, retention, no recording) and have it reviewed.

**Verified (offline).** Handler tests (17), joined tests through the real bridge with measured playback (3), config helper tests (6, including the gate refusing a greeting without `fast_test`), parser tests for `isAiQuestion`, opening tests for the greeting. Mutation checks: removing the AI-question handling, weakening the answer, or asking the name again after the greeting each fails the suite; all 18 mutations of the fast flow are killed. Full suite: 1849 tests, 1847 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo.

**Not verified:** how the ElevenLabs voice sounds on this greeting, latency, and any live call. Nothing was committed or pushed when this was written; the code was committed locally later (see 5a-52).

## 5a-51. Voice + real Jobber test mode with ALL REAL TEXTS DISABLED (the default) is built and tested offline (nothing applied, restarted, called, booked, sent or published) (2026-11-02)

## 5a-53. The first LIVE calls of the fast flow: result FAIL; the wrong-date booking in Jobber; the offline fixes; what is still to be shown live (2026-11-03; records only; the fixes are uncommitted, unpushed and NOT loaded by the running server)

**Scope and sources.** The founder's live test calls on 2026-11-03 (Central time), read from `founder-voice.log` (fixed codes and counts only; the log holds no caller words), Twilio's call record and debugger (read-only), the TwiML Bin (read-only) and one read-only query of the Jobber Developer Test Account through the app's own connector. No personal detail, number, address, host or key is recorded here. **Overall result of the last live test: FAIL** (CL-1 is not met; see the table).

**What happened, in order.**
1. **12:58 PM: a call failed at once.** Twilio recorded "No Answer", 0 seconds, error 11100 "Invalid URL" after fetching the TwiML Bin successfully (HTTP 200). The Bin's stream address was well-formed and matched the saved config, but its free Cloudflare quick tunnel had died (its name no longer resolved). VERIFIED LIVE (Twilio record, DNS and tunnel checks). **Fixed by infrastructure steps, each approved separately:** a new quick tunnel, the host updated in the private call config (backup first) and in the Bin (read back and verified by fingerprint), and a server restart. Quick tunnels get a new host at every start and expire; a permanent named tunnel would end this and is an open founder decision.
2. **About 1:35 PM and 1:36 PM: two calls reached the fast handler** (the approved caller was recognised). Call 1: the caller hung up after 28 seconds. Call 2: ended after 66 seconds as `SIZE_UNCLEAR`. No booking. The address question was asked 3 and 6 times (the follow-up for the missing city or ZIP started while the caller was still speaking; the caller's speech cut those prompts after 0 to 718 ms). ElevenLabs answered all 20 speech requests (the HTTP 400 seen on 2026-11-02 did not recur; its cause stays **UNVERIFIED**, it followed a key re-entry).
3. **2:27 PM and 2:32 PM (the server restarted at 2:26 PM): call A reached a verified booking; call B did not.** Call A: `JOBBER_WRITE_SENT` for `clientCreate` then `jobCreate`, `JOBBER_BOOKING_CREATED` (2 writes), `FAST_BOOKING booked_verified`, `FAST_TEXTS_SKIPPED`, `FAST_END BOOKED`. Call B: the read-back played three times with verdict "cut", then `UNCONFIRMED`; nothing booked (the cause is **UNVERIFIED**; the caller speaking over the line is the likely reason).

**The Jobber wrong-date evidence (VERIFIED LIVE, read-only query 2026-11-03).** One ledger entry (state created); **job #1**, status **upcoming**, total **$199**, created 2:28 PM Central. Its visit and its job window are **Monday, October 4, 2027, 9:00 to 11:00 AM Central** (14:00 to 16:00 UTC). The founder expected **today, November 3, 2026**. The time-zone reading is correct (9:00 AM on that date in Central daylight time is 14:00 UTC). The booking is a faithful copy of the date Drew's code held, so **the read-back passed ("booked_verified") on a wrong request**: it compares Jobber with the request, not with what the caller meant. What the caller said is **UNVERIFIED** (the log holds no words).

**Root cause (the code paths are VERIFIED by reading; what was said is INFERRED).** (1) `whenFrom` moved a month and day that had already passed to **next year** (an October 4 reading on November 3 became 2027-10-04). A 30-phrase offline probe at the call's clock gave 2027-10-04 only for "October 4", "October 4th" and "10/4"; every November 3 phrasing gave 2026-11-03. (2) `chooseOffer` had **no upper bound**, and the calendar was read for only the next 15 days, so a date eleven months away looked "free" because nothing was known about it. (3) The spoken offer and read-back named the day but **not the year**. (4) The Jobber read-back cannot catch a wrong request.

**Offline fixes made since (BUILT and TESTED OFFLINE; NOT committed, NOT pushed, NOT in CI, NOT loaded by the running server).**
- **Settle timer and caller speech:** the wait before "ask only what is missing" is 3000 ms (was 1200 ms) and is held while the caller is speaking (new `speech` transport event from the voice service's own speech start and stop; a lost stop stops counting after 10 s; the 12 s "did not catch that" reprompt is held too). Files: `call-transport.ts`, `realtime-transport.ts`, `fast-call-handler.ts`.
- **Date safeguards:** a month and day already past is **flagged and clarified, never moved to next year**; a four-digit year the caller says is used as said; a day that does not exist is not a date; a date beyond today plus the 14-day horizon is refused with a spoken reason; the search never runs past the last bookable date (`lastBookableDate`); three unusable dates end the call with a person to follow up; the offer, the read-back and the booked line **speak the full date with the year and the time**; the calendar is **read again immediately before any Jobber write** (a taken time ends the call with nothing booked; an unreadable calendar likewise); the booking adapter refuses a visit more than 30 days ahead. Files: `fast-parsers.ts`, `fast-availability.ts`, `fast-call-handler.ts`, `jobber-booking.ts`.
- **Test-only confirmation emails (default OFF):** after `booked_verified`, one fixed test email to the founder-approved customer address and one to the owner address (name, service, address, full date and year, time, price, Jobber job number), through the sender's own SMTP server (Yahoo or Gmail, app password) with the pinned dependency `nodemailer@10.1.7`. Recipients are held in the protected vault and reached only by role; `FAST_EMAILS` must be exactly `on`; a failed email never changes the booking; "accepted" is never reported as "delivered". Files: `fast-email.ts`, `fast-email-copy.ts`, `founder-voice-fast.ts`, `fast-test-setup-main.ts`, `founder-voice-entry.ts`, `fast-call-handler.ts`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`. The sender login and both addresses are **saved** (protected; not read back); emails are OFF.
- **Private scripts (outside the repository):** `fast-test-enter-emails.ps1` (four hidden prompts; names the failing field, never a value) and `fast-test-start.ps1` (emails OFF unless `-Emails` is given; removes `FAST_EMAILS` at the start and the end of every run; prints `EMAILS: ON` or `OFF` before START; texts stay OFF; Jobber setting and the eight checks unchanged).
- **Approved caller:** on 2026-11-03 the saved customer and owner test numbers were swapped (a copy of the encrypted file was made first), so the phone the founder calls from is the approved caller. This changed no code.

**Offline test result (Windows, 2026-11-03, after the last change):** the full `npm test` script ran **1882 tests, 1880 pass, 0 fail**; the 2 that neither passed nor failed are presumed to be the same 2 `todo` tests as in the earlier record (the todo count was not printed this run: UNVERIFIED). Focused suites after the last email change: 64 of 64. Not run on the Linux CI runner; no CI run covers these changes.

**Tests that were changed, reported as the definition of done requires (0.2, item 2):** the offer, read-back and booked-line expectations in `fast-call-handler.test.ts` and `fast-call-joined.test.ts` now include the year; two "a past day or month rolls forward" assertions in `fast-parsers.test.ts` now expect the clarifying flag; one event-order assertion in `realtime-transport.test.ts` now includes the two `speech` events; the dependency guards in `live-gate.test.ts`, `approval-store-sqlite.test.ts` and `reporting-ledger-sqlite.test.ts` now allow exactly two dependencies (the SDK and `nodemailer`), and a new guard requires that only `fast-email.ts` imports the mail library and that the lockfile matches the pin. No other protected test was changed.
