## 5a-54. Live Tests #2 and #3, the fixes since, the email check, the commits and CI status, and what is still unverified (2026-11-03 evening; records only; this documentation edit changed no code and ran no restart, call or email)

**Sources.** `founder-voice.log` (fixed codes and counts only; it holds no caller words), the read-only Jobber lookups (queries only), GitHub Actions' API, `git log`, and the founder's own statements (marked as such). No personal value, number, address, host or key is recorded here.

**Live Test #2 (5:14 PM Central; server started 5:09 PM from `f38e82c`): FAIL.** The address was asked twice instead of three to six times. Then the offer (an 11.6 s line, "$199") was cut by caller speech **0.22 s before it ended** (verdict `cut`); the replay (18 s, with the approved "Please wait until I have finished, then answer." instruction) was cut 0.5 s in; the third replay was `held` (an early yes); the call ended `UNCONFIRMED` and nothing was booked. No silence timer fired. The founder reported the price sounding like "19, 199"; the cause is UNKNOWN (no audio is kept; the text sent was exactly "$199"). The same pattern had ended the read-back on 2026-11-03 at 2:34 PM.

**Fixes for Test #2: commit `92c6093` (CI #53 success, run 41740758580).** A bounded tail tolerance: a confirmation line cut by the caller starting to speak counts as played only when what is left is at most 2000 ms and at least a quarter of the closing question had already played (the closing is the trailing run of question sentences; a sentence with a digit or a dollar sign never counts, so the price, date, time and address must have been heard); the replay wording and the replay cap are unchanged; the log records `tail: true`. The price is spoken in words ("one hundred ninety-nine dollars") in the offer and read-back; the texts, the emails and the Jobber amount keep 199.

**Live Test #3 (about 5:59 PM; server PID 54388 started 5:56 PM from `92c6093`): PASS on the two fixed defects; new defects found.** Two calls reached the server: call 1, the caller hung up after 20 s (cause UNKNOWN); call 2, 112 s, `BOOKED`. Jobber **job #2**: Wednesday, November 4, **2026**, 9:00 to 11:00 AM Central, status upcoming, total $199; one new job (two ledger entries in all). VERIFIED LIVE: the tail rule applied (the offer was cut 0.5 s before its end, `tail: true`, no replay); the right year; Central time; the price; texts skipped (`FAST_TEXTS_SKIPPED`); no email event (emails were OFF). Defects: prompts started on top of the caller and were cut after 0 to 900 ms (the day question asked twice, the size question three times, the read-back replayed once after a cut 0.54 s in); the address question was asked once. **UNVERIFIED:** whether 9:00 AM was the time requested (the founder was asked), and how the price sounded (not reported). CL-1 counts **no** clean completion yet.

**Fixes since, offline only (BUILT and TESTED OFFLINE; NOT live-verified; NOT in the running server).**
- `a454651` (CI #54 success, run 41746713501): a duplicate-email guard (Jobber's duplicate flag, and one email set per booking key per server process); the callback number the caller confirmed in the OWNER's email only (never in the customer's email, a subject or a log); and `email-check` (without `--send` nothing is sent and no connection opens; with `--send` ONE marked sample with invented details goes to the owner address only).
- `de4dbbf` (CI #55, run 41750957245: **success**, completed 2026-11-04 01:06:54 UTC; it was recorded as pending when this was first written): a bounded turn hold (a question waits while the caller speaks and 600 ms after, at most 3 s, then is spoken anyway; the fixed lines are never held; words said before a confirmation line was spoken confirm nothing and cause no replay); WHEN and SIZE fragments ("um", a time without a day, "two bedrooms" alone) wait the same held 3 s settle window as the address; the texts-off goodbye no longer says "This test line does not send text messages" (it is now "You are booked for {day, year} at {time}. Goodbye.", plus the email line when emails are on); and the log line `FAST_HOLD {waitedMs, ignored}` (two numbers, written only when a hold happens).
- Known limits: a past month and day, or a bare "the 1st", is clarified rather than moved to next year, so near New Year the caller must say the year; the speech detector's own half-second pause is unchanged; the 600 ms grace and the 3 s caps are untested live.

**Email status.** `email-check --send` was run once: the Yahoo mail server **accepted** ONE sample for the owner address, and the founder reported it **received** (the founder's statement; inbox versus spam was not stated). "Accepted" is never "delivered". **No email has ever been sent from a real booking.** The sender login and both approved addresses are saved (protected; never read back). Emails stay OFF unless the start script is given `-Emails`.

**Tests.** Full `npm test` on the tree that became `de4dbbf`: **1903 tests, 1901 pass, 0 fail, 2 todo** (Windows, local). Mutation checks on every new rule were caught (the hold, its cap, its early-words rule, its grace, the three fragment rules, the goodbye, the email guard, the owner-only number, the check's recipient and its `--send` requirement) and each file was restored byte for byte. CI: #52 `f38e82c` success; #53 `92c6093` success; #54 `a454651` success; #55 `de4dbbf` success (run 41750957245, completed 2026-11-04 01:06:54 UTC; recorded as pending when first written). **Tests changed on purpose (reported as 0.2 item 2 requires):** the spoken-price expectations; six goodbye expectations; the handler and joined test rigs set short hold timings; plus the guard-test changes already recorded in 5a-53. No other protected test was changed.

**How the fast Drew test is started now** (private scripts in `C:\Users\Eldtc\founder-call-backups\`, outside the repository; the full steps are in the runbook's new first section): stop the old server (Ctrl+C in the key-holding window); `$env:INTAKE_ALLOW_LIVE_CALLS = "yes"`; run `fast-test-start.ps1` (check only); then `fast-test-start.ps1 -Serve` and type START. The script removes `FAST_TEXTS` (**SMS stays OFF**) and `FAST_EMAILS` at every run, sets `JOBBER_ALLOW_WRITES=test-account`, runs eight checks (a ninth, the saved email settings, with `-Emails`), and prints `EMAILS: ON` or `OFF` before START. `-Emails` switches emails on for that run only. **The running server is PID 54388 (started 5:56 PM): it has the `92c6093` code and lacks `a454651` and `de4dbbf`.**

## 5a-55. CL-0 Phase 1: the current private configurations backed up, restore PASSED, OneDrive copy MATCHED (2026-11-06; records only; this documentation edit changed no code, configuration, secret, roadmap decision, server, tunnel or call)

**Status: CL-0 stays PARTIAL.** Only the founder, in writing, can set DONE. This is Phase 1 of the backup in `PRIVATE_BACKUP_AND_RESTORE.md` (the four current configuration files). It is not the whole private inventory of that document (section 2).

**What was done (each step approved separately by the founder). The founder ran the installer, the encryption, the extraction, the copy to OneDrive, the download and the deletions, and entered the passphrase; the assistant ran only the tag, the downloads of the installer, and read-only hashing and checks.**
1. **Baseline tag.** Local annotated tag `test8-baseline-59d87be` on commit `59d87be` (VERIFIED: the tag resolves to that commit; not pushed).
2. **Tool.** 7-Zip 26.05 x64. The installer came from the project's GitHub release that `7-zip.org` links to; its SHA-256 matched the value published there (VERIFIED). It is **not digitally signed** (VERIFIED: `NotSigned`), which is normal for this project; the hash is the only check. A cross-check against SourceForge was **UNVERIFIABLE** (the site refused automated requests, HTTP 403). It was installed by the founder and reports version 26.05.
3. **Manifest.** Names, sizes and SHA-256 hashes of the four originals, the code commit and the tag, written as a plain file with **no contents** (the manifest itself is plaintext, and it is also inside the archive). The originals were hashed in place, and **building the archive wrote no plaintext copy of a private file** (it was made straight from the originals). **The restore test did create temporary plaintext copies** (item 6).
4. **Archive.** One 7z archive, AES-256 with encrypted file names, built by the founder straight from the four originals plus the manifest, with the passphrase typed at a private prompt (the assistant never saw it). Size 4,399 bytes; SHA-256 begins `cd80de5ac19d4f2f`. The archive holds the three configuration files (hash prefixes `a03b4a6e`, `db976914`, `7eca0f8b`), the private start script (`3d5f89d4`) and the manifest. The archive stores **file names only, not paths**; the original locations are in section 5a-55's table below.
5. **Encryption check.** VERIFIED by 7-Zip itself: listing the archive without a passphrase asks for one and shows no file names. The raw bytes contain none of 13 plaintext markers (a weaker check, since compressed data also hides text).
6. **Restore test: PASS.** Extracted into a new scratch folder outside OneDrive and the repository: 5 of 5 files; every restored file's size and SHA-256 matched the restored manifest and the original on disk; the restored manifest was identical to the original manifest (699 bytes). **This step wrote temporary plaintext copies of all five files to the scratch folder, including the three private configuration files, which hold the stream path of 2026-11-06.** They existed there while the restore was extracted and verified, and the founder then deleted the scratch folder (VERIFIED gone: it is no longer in the backup working folder). The Windows Recycle Bin holds no entry with the scratch folder's or the restored files' names (VERIFIED by name only; the bin has other entries that were not opened). Whether remnants remain in unallocated disk space is UNVERIFIED; an ordinary delete does not erase them, and nothing here wiped them.
7. **OneDrive copy: MATCH.** The archive was placed in a folder at the root of the founder's OneDrive Personal. The synced local copy matched the local archive, and a copy **downloaded back from the OneDrive website** (its internet-origin marker names the OneDrive site) matched in full: same size (4,399 bytes) and the same full SHA-256. That is the backup document's section 5b rule 6 check. Neither the passphrase nor the archive's contents were used.

| Restored file | Original location |
|---|---|
| `founder-voice-call.config.json`, `founder-voice.config.json`, `founder-call.config.json` | `poc/intake-runner/` (git-ignored) |
| `fast-test-start.ps1` | `%USERPROFILE%\founder-call-backups\` |
| `manifest-2026-11-06.txt` | the backup working folder (outside the repository and outside OneDrive) |

**A defect of mine, recorded.** The first extraction attempts failed ("Can't open as archive", then a wrong-password message) because the extraction command carried a bare `-p`. For creation, 7-Zip prompts for the passphrase when `-p` has no value; for extraction it appears to send an empty one. The archive and the passphrase were not at fault (the archive test passed). The corrected command has no `-p`, so 7-Zip asks when it needs the passphrase. The mechanism is from the assistant's knowledge of 7-Zip, not from a separate test; the corrected command worked. Two incidental exposures in the assistant's own output are also recorded: a OneDrive web address with an account folder identifier (from a download marker) and, earlier in the session, screenshots of the old, dead tunnel address and part of a destination number in the Twilio editor. No key, token or passphrase was shown.

**UNRESOLVED / not proven (each needs its own explicit approval; none is authorized by this record).**
1. **No USB copy.** No drive was plugged in. The two-destination direction is not complete.
2. **OneDrive account checks UNVERIFIED:** two-step sign-in, storage plan and quota, version history and whether old versions can be deleted, sign-in activity, and recovery of the account (backup document, section 5b).
3. **The OneDrive folder name contains "Drew"**, against rule 4 of section 5b (neutral names). The archive's own name is neutral. Renaming is the founder's choice.
4. **Restore only on the same Windows profile and computer.** Fine for these four files (none is DPAPI-bound). A restore on another profile or computer, and a restore from the OneDrive copy by extraction (only its hash was compared), have not been tried.
5. **No schedule** for the monthly restore test, and no retention count has been decided.
6. **Point in time.** The archive holds the stream path of 2026-11-06. The quick tunnel has died three times in three days, and every repair rotates that path and changes the TwiML Bin; the archive goes stale each time. A restore brings back a path that no longer matches, so a restore is followed by a new tunnel and a new TwiML Bin entry (recovery order in the backup document).
7. **Phase 2 is not started.** NOT backed up: the intake and contacts databases (needs the read-only snapshot route), the logs, the contact key and the other DPAPI-bound items (consent evidence, booking ledger), which need the wrap-and-restore export code that does not exist; the other private scripts; the TwiML Bin text; the preserved paused work. The Jobber credentials, tokens and test numbers are re-entered after a restore by design.
8. **BitLocker or Device Encryption on this computer is UNKNOWN** (not readable without administrator rights). Nothing here depends on it.
9. **Copies of the archive remain in the founder's Downloads folder** (one named with " (1)", one without; the second was not compared). They are encrypted. The 7-Zip installer file also remains in the backup working folder. Deleting them is the founder's decision.
10. **Old configuration backups** (`.bak.json` files and the older copies in `%USERPROFILE%\founder-call-backups\`) hold dead stream paths and hosts. They are git-ignored and were not touched or cleaned.
11. **`PRIVATE_BACKUP_AND_RESTORE.md` was not changed** and still says "NOT yet carried out"; this section is the record of what has been done. The passphrase is not recorded anywhere in the repository, and that file does not and must not hold it.

**What this does NOT prove or change.**
1. **Address validation (Stage 1 item 1.1 in `DREW_PRODUCT_ROADMAP.md`) is BUILT as an interface only.** No provider is chosen or connected. The HUD-USPS crosswalk (one preferred city and state per ZIP, no street-level check; its storage and redistribution terms are UNVERIFIED and a permission request to HUD is drafted, not sent) and Google Address Validation (street-level; pricing and caching terms read, latency and SKU UNVERIFIED) were compared in writing only. The Test #12 street error would be caught only by a street-level source.
2. **The commit token says which commit was HEAD at startup, not that the working tree was clean.** `FAST_*` lines still carry no call id; calls are separated by `CALL_STREAM_ACCEPTED`.
3. **The scorecard cannot yet pass a call from the current log.** It has no `FAST_STEP`-based evidence for caller, name and street confirmation, it needs the private attestation (call time, founder verdict, address and time matches, emails received), and the 2026-11-05 call scores a false FAIL because it predates the current event names. An address-validation row is not yet a scorecard check.
4. **The cause of Test #12 is still unexplained.** Jobber job #9's content was not read by Claude. None of the four commits is claimed to fix it.

**Blocked (each needs the founder).** A restart on `c12a144` (keyed window) before Test #13; a founder-run Test #13 and then the three consecutive clean completions; the Test #12 account and the Jobber job #9 comparison; the Google Cloud setup (project, billing, a small daily quota, a restricted key) and the founder-run trial, then the Billing SKU report (up to 48 hours); the HUD reply and the choice of address source; wiring a real validator into `validateAddress`; the push and green CI for all 13 commits; stream secret rotation, the named tunnel as a service, the old quick tunnel (PID 19096); cancelling old Jobber test jobs #1 to #9; stale backup documents.

## 5c. Status board and preservation checkpoint (2026-10-28; checkpoint at commit `6470b86`, workflow added in the commit after it)

**HISTORICAL (four-agent plan).** The founder retired the four-agent development direction on 2026-11-02 and chose ONE residential-cleaning AI agent (record 5a-52). Read this section as history unless the single agent actively uses what it describes.

**This section is the current summary. Sections 5 and 5b are dated snapshots.** It keeps three things apart, as `docs/PRODUCT_CONTINUITY.md` rule 5 requires: what the founder approved, what is **tested offline** (an automated test passes against stand-ins and offline models: it says nothing about the live service), and what the founder **saw on a live call** (a recorded result). A test passing is never written here as "works live".

**Standing rules in force.** Review, commit, push and verify completed, tested work, then report each result (founder rule, 2026-10-27; section 1b of this file). That rule does not authorise starting the next task, using the API key, restarting the server, changing live configuration or Twilio, placing a call, or any live or paid action: each needs its own instruction. One task at a time, each approved first. Researching a tool is not approval to build it (2026-10-28). No secret, key, relay path, tunnel host or real phone number in the repository, which is public.

Drew is residential-only: the fixed residential question and the decline line are approved. The $25 first-standard-clean discount is one offer (the greeting's special and the simulation fixture's discount); the fixture's base amount and limits are test data. The caller hears their own name in the read-back (caller only; never in a text model's history or a log), and the voice is told captured and missing field labels. The first-cleaning question is exactly **"Is this your first cleaning with Drew Cleaning?"**, spoken as a fixed line: yes means first cleaning, no means returning customer. Optional cleaning fields (last professional clean, home empty, priority areas, number of rooms) are read back and saved only with the caller's own evidence; being asked alone is not evidence. Required-field checks, the clarification limits (one ask plus two clarifications), masking, the contact vault and the dashboard contract are preserved. **GPT-Live stays paused** (below).

Its first runs on the Linux runner **failed** on one voice-service test (the full-call test): it sent its "sound during the notice" after the notice could already count as heard, so on a fast runner the sound was rightly forwarded and its exact-count wait never matched (a race in the test, not a product defect; on Windows the same test passed). The test was made deterministic (it now sends the sound while the notice is certainly still playing and also checks the sound is not forwarded afterwards) in commit `18b75d0`, and **the run on `18b75d0` succeeded**. One other voice-service test ("settings: a transcriber that documents hints gets English by default ...") also failed once, on one earlier run, in 0.3 seconds with no detail captured; **its cause was not determined** and passing later runs does not prove it. The workflow now annotates every failing test with the first lines of its own failure message, so a recurrence explains itself. The two known masker gaps are todo, not failures. Settings needed: none (Actions was enabled; a public repository needs no approval for the owner's own pushes). The raw log requires a GitHub sign-in even on a public repository, so the run page's job summary and annotations carry the results.

The audit of what is built, missing, reusable and undecided for each offer is in [`docs/ROADMAP_BACKLOG.md`](ROADMAP_BACKLOG.md) section 3b (it points to existing steps and `DRF` items rather than repeating them; the one new requirement is `DRF-06`, owner text alerts). No price or package is set anywhere. The cleaning-first order (section 2), the SIMULATION-versus-LIVE rules (3a), the dashboard rule (1a) and one-task-at-a-time approval (1b) are unchanged.

**When each kind of change belongs.** *Packaging or wording changes* (names, what an offer includes, how it is described) can be made at any time but may claim only what the current phase has verified live. *Documentation changes* belong with each step they describe and with every live result. *Software changes* belong to the phase named above, in the order of the phases, one authorized task at a time; none is authorized by this section. Working on a later phase's design is not approval to build it (section 7).

## 6. Open founder decisions that gate the order

1. Cleaning categories and their required fields (step 2). Proposed inputs, not approved, are in `docs/research/CLEANING_INTAKE_RESEARCH.md`.
2. Pricing rules and who approves them (steps 3-4), per business, including the quote-versus-estimate policy for each service (section 3a). Moving any business from SIMULATION to LIVE is its own explicit decision.
3. Contact retention beyond the 30-day pilot, who may read contacts, and whether the number may be read back in full (step 1 hardening).
4. Whether the callback number may be suggested from caller ID, with the caller's confirmation.
5. **Decided by the founder (2026-10-26):** the preview budget is 8000 ms. Still engineering values, not decisions: the read-back caps (`MAX_READBACKS = 2`, second unclear answer, 20 s wait).
6. **Decided by the founder (2026-10-26):** the confirmed closing line is "Thank you. Your request is recorded. Goodbye." with no follow-up promise. Open: whether it may ever promise a follow-up (not while no callback process exists).
7. Whether and when to commit and push the Option A and contact-capture work.

8. For the two-minute dashboard (section 3b): whether the owner list may show a caller's name after owner login, how the contact reveal is audited and retained, and when the "nothing implies a booking" rule may be relaxed.

9. Whether and when to apply the `opening` block and the services skill to the live config (a new digest, new TwiML in the Twilio bin and a restart), the disclosure wording and its legal check, and whether the business wants a special.

10. **Decided by the founder (2026-10-27):** the phone voice moves to OpenAI speech-to-speech with a Drew Cleaning receptionist persona, tested with invented details only (see the OpenAI voice plan in section 5). OpenAI replaces the Claude API inside the phone agent; Claude Code remains the builder and tester. Open: approval of the persona wording (drafted offline, not wired) and the voice, consent-before-audio (whether consent is collected on Twilio before any audio reaches OpenAI), the data terms for real customers, and the disclosure wording if a second vendor hears the audio.

10a. **Decided by the founder (2026-10-27), recorded in `docs/DREW_CALL_TO_BOOKING_SPEC.html`:** Drew converses natively on Realtime with controlled server tools; Drew is residential-only; the $25 first-standard-clean discount is defined. Still open: the tool design, the commercial-route replacement wording, whether the fixture's eligibility limits are the business's terms, and real rates.

11. **Open gaps kept visible (2026-10-27).** (a) **Consent-before-audio:** with the Realtime adapter as built, the consent notice is spoken through OpenAI, so the caller's first words, including a no, are heard by OpenAI before consent is given; the fix needs the call logic to accept a consent already collected on Twilio, and until then this is only acceptable for founder tests with invented details. (b) **The interrupted read-back:** a read-back that was sent but talked over part-way can still be confirmed by a yes, because the interruption path does not invalidate it (only a read-back that was never sent, or that the voice service reports as not played, is now protected); the same applies to the main read-back on the Twilio carrier.

(The dashboard constraint in section 1a and the one-task-at-a-time rule in section 1b are **not** open decisions.)

## 7. Rules for whoever continues this

**[2026-11-02: any rule below that lets tested work be committed and pushed automatically is superseded by section 0.5 rule 1: every commit, push and live action needs its own explicit approval.]**

- Read `docs/PRODUCT_CONTINUITY.md` first, then section 1a above. The dashboard rule is permanent and founder-locked.
- Keep the order in section 3. If a step is skipped, say so in the register.
- Follow section 1b: one task at a time, explicit founder approval before each begins, and the cycle implementation, automated tests, live validation when applicable, founder review. Finishing a task never authorises the next.
- A number in this file ("1261 tests") is a dated snapshot. Re-run the suite and read `git status --short` and `git log origin/main..HEAD --oneline` for the current state.
- **Founder's standing rule (2026-10-27): review, commit, push and verify completed work, then report each result.** When a task is finished and its tests pass, review the exact changed files for sensitive data and unrelated changes, commit them, push to origin/main without force, verify that GitHub matches local HEAD, and report each of those results. The rule covers completed, tested work only. It does not authorise starting the next task (section 1b still applies), and it does not relax the separate rules against using a key, restarting the server, changing Twilio, placing a call or any live or paid action without a separate instruction. (`docs/PRODUCT_CONTINUITY.md` rule 8, "never commit or push without being asked", predates this rule and is not yet updated.)
- **Milestone and backlog (2026-10-28):** the active milestone is M1 "Reliable Intake"; the approved requirements beyond it are in `docs/ROADMAP_BACKLOG.md`. **Every task handoff states the current milestone (with its status) and the next eligible backlog item first.** Recording a backlog item is not authorization to build or activate it.
- Do not put personal data, secrets, the relay path or keys in any document.
